# Hearth — Changelog archive (v230–v475)
 
Detailed changelog entries older than the newest ~10 versions live here, moved out of `HEARTH-notes.md` on 05/07/2026 to keep the per-session read small. **Read this file only when a specific old version is referenced** — day-to-day sessions don't need it. A condensed one-line index of every version stays in `HEARTH-notes.md` ("Shipped" section).

Maintenance: when a new version's detailed entry is added to `HEARTH-changelog.md` and its "Recently completed" section passes ~10 entries, move the oldest detailed entries from there to the TOP of the list below, keeping this file newest-first. (`tests/checks.sh` prints a note once that section holds more than 12.)

- **v475: a general-purpose Knee Programme joins Train (19/09/2026).** A sixth entry in `PROGRAMMES_TRAIN` (`knee_general`, 12 exercises) plus its card in `renderTrainPrograms` — the two hand-listed halves that adding a programme always needs, and the card's `key` is the array index, so **append only, never reorder `PROGRAMMES_TRAIN`**. First programme to use `type:'reps'` (the warm-up and the six strength moves; the physio session renderer already supported it, nothing had exercised it) and the first to use `perLeg:true` — the three stretches are `sets:4, perLeg:true`, which the existing set-label code reads as two 30s holds a side, i.e. "hold 30 sec, twice each". Tagged **General fitness**, not Physio: it is a generic programme, and its `warning` says to see a physio or GP first. New optional programme field **`info`** — a neutral grey box under the intro's exercise list, `esc()`'d, carrying the after-2–3-weeks progression list. **Do NOT fold `info` into `warning`**: the amber box is for things that make you stop, and burying progression advice in it trains you to skim the box that matters. No data model, sync or export change. Tests changed: none. PR: https://github.com/coshea321/miniature-journey/pull/241
- **v474: Prep/Method/Notes collapse, and Ingredients/Prep/Method/Notes get a real drag-to-resize handle (15/09/2026).** Cathal's original ask ("expandable sections") turned out to mean resizing the text box, not folding it away — clarified after the first cut shipped collapse-only. Both are now in: Prep/Method/Notes are wrapped in a native `<details open>`/`<summary>` (Category, Ingredients, Recipe URL stay always-visible), styled to match `fieldLabel` via `recipeSectionSummary(t)`. Separately, all four recipe-editor textareas gain a **`recipeResizeGrip(id)`** drag handle — CSS `resize:vertical` alone leaves no visible grip on most mobile browsers, so this is a JS handle wired via Pointer Events (`wireRecipeResizeGrips`) that sets the textarea's height directly on drag. **Do NOT rely on `resize:vertical` alone for a mobile-facing textarea again** — it silently does nothing there. No data model, sync or export change. Tests changed: none. PR: https://github.com/coshea321/miniature-journey/pull/240
- **v473: Settings sheet regroups around Visible sections, custom categories fold behind a tap (13/09/2026).** Third pick from the 12/09 UI/UX review (backlog entry ③). The sheet was already titled "⚙️ Settings" with a proper close `aria-label` — the actual defect was the subtitle "Manage your custom categories" sitting above an empty `customCatsList`, burying **Visible sections** (the thing you actually came for) below it. Dropped the subtitle; **Visible sections** now leads, its old three-sentence description replaced with one line, "Five on the bar, the rest under More."; custom categories moved into a native `<details>`/`<summary>` disclosure (the same fold pattern already used for the Firebase setup help), collapsed by default; Import/Export and Delete all are unchanged and in the same order. `renderCustomCatsList()` still targets `#customCatsList` unchanged — it now just renders inside a closed `<details>`. No Settings tabs added. `secVisible`/`syncPrefs` restore-from-backup untouched (that's audit F4). No data model, sync or export change. Tests changed: none. PR: https://github.com/coshea321/miniature-journey/pull/239
- **v472: Recipes' four maintenance pills fold into one 🧰 Tools sheet (13/09/2026).** Second pick from the 12/09 UI/UX review (backlog entry ②). `renderRecipes`' `selectHdr` now shows just **Week plan** (kept standalone — it's a view, not a tool) and a **🧰 Tools** button opening a `.overlay`/`.modal-box` sheet (the same pattern as Weather/More) listing Tidy, Prep helper, Calories and Select as `.more-row` rows. Category chips are untouched, below the header. **Every existing click handler (`rcpTidyBtn`/`rcpPrepBtn`/`rcpCalBtn`/`rcpSelStart`) is byte-for-byte unchanged** — only the markup summoning them moved into the sheet; the sheet itself is torn down and rebuilt with the rest of `recipesContent` on every render, so no explicit close-on-navigate logic was needed. Also fixes `recipeFocusHint`: it used to show the classic-tick sentence right beside a Focus button whose on-state does the opposite. It's now hidden entirely while Focus is off and shows only the existing on-state sentence when on — no new copy. No data model, sync or export change. Tests changed: none. PR: https://github.com/coshea321/miniature-journey/pull/238

- **v471: the Home week strip hides on a week with no training in it (13/09/2026).** Last pick from the 12/09 UI/UX review (backlog entry ④). `renderWeekStrip` now tracks whether any of its seven days held a session and sets `el.hidden` from that, **together with the `💪 Training this week` header** (given an id, `#homeWeekHdr`) — an orphaned title reads worse than the row of empty dots it was introducing. Toggled **both ways**: `renderWeekStrip` runs on every Home render, so the strip returns the moment a session lands. **The non-obvious bit: `.home-sec-hdr` and `.home-week-strip` each set an explicit `display` in a class rule, which beats the browser's own `[hidden]{display:none}`** — so `el.hidden` was a silent no-op until `.home-sec-hdr[hidden],.home-week-strip[hidden]{display:none}` was added. Any future `el.hidden` on a styled container needs the same check. **Do NOT extend this to the Training today-card** — `#tcTrainCard` is the only door into Train when its bar icon is off (default), so hiding it on a quiet day strands the section; that was offered and cut. Option A's ★ Today / Grocery / Last medicine hides stay rejected, and both 🎉 spotlight empty lines stay — they read as a reward for being done. No data model, sync or export change. Tests changed: none. PR: https://github.com/coshea321/miniature-journey/pull/237

- **v470: Log medicine leads the Medicine tab, plus four missing accessible names (13/09/2026).** First pick from the 12/09 UI/UX review, confirmed by Cathal. `renderMedicine` now emits the **Log medicine card first** and `bottleFreshnessCardHtml(bd)` after it — bottle tracking stays, it just no longer occupies the first slot on the 2am screen. Its controls are wired off `el.querySelectorAll` after `innerHTML`, so the reorder needed no wiring change. A one-line hint under the quick chips ("Tap Calpol or Nurofen for a weight-based estimate — then check the leaflet") says what the empty Dose box is waiting for; the dose working text went from `#7A5A7A`/13px to `#5A3A5A`/14px so the safety copy is no longer the palest thing on the card. **No strength, formula, rounding, 10ml cap or interval advisory changed** — the `checks.sh` dosing pins all still match. Accessible names added to Trip Import (icon-only, where Plants Import already had one), the 23 Notes format-toolbar buttons plus its legend `?`, and the list ★ today star (with `aria-pressed`). `.modal-close` grew 30px → 40px — the X is how you leave Settings / More / Sync. No data model, sync or export change. Tests changed: none. PR: https://github.com/coshea321/miniature-journey/pull/235

- **v469: mobile UI polish for sheets, filters and empty states — and "Clear filters" actually clears the search (12/09/2026).** The v467 polish work, rebased onto v468 and renumbered (one PR per version; its original PR went stale behind two merges). Bottom sheets use the mobile visual viewport to sit above the software keyboard and scroll the focused control into view, including dynamically-created sheets. The six coloured action bars no longer repeat the section title the green app header already shows. Horizontally scrolling chip and forecast rows gain a right-edge fade. Empty list, plant-area, watchlist, inventory-area and Family Log states now say what produced zero results. **The fix on top:** `listEmptyClearBtn` reset `_travelFilter`/`_todayFilter`/`_recentFilter` but **not `_listSearchQuery`** — so in the search case, the one route the PR notes named as most reliable, the tap re-rendered an identical screen. It now clears the query and the input box too. **The search box is a filter; any future reset here must include it.** No data model, sync or export change. Tests changed: none.

- **v468: sync refreshes its login instead of falsely demanding one (09/09/2026).** Every Firebase Database REST request now goes through `firebaseFetch`: obtain a current token before sending, retry once if Firebase rejects a cached token, and share one in-flight refresh across simultaneous personal/household/SSE work. The re-login banner appears only when the saved refresh credential is missing or Firebase rejects it; ordinary timeouts stay quiet, while a second 401/403 after a successful refresh is identified as rules/access trouble rather than expired login. Removed v373's six-second startup watchdog, which raced an eight-second refresh timeout and could send an unauthenticated request during the final two seconds. Both EventSource listeners now refuse to open without a token in Auth mode and retry transient refresh failures. **Do NOT bypass `firebaseFetch` with `fetch(authUrl(...))` for Database REST.** Test `69-auth-token-refresh.js` added: refresh-before-fetch, 401 retry, offline/missing distinction and concurrent-refresh coalescing. PR to follow.

- **v467: an optional Wikipedia summary on each Watchlist entry (06/09/2026).** Expanding a row now offers **&#x1F4C4; Get a summary from Wikipedia**: one keyless `api.php` call (`generator=search` + `prop=extracts`), the intro cleaned and cut to `WATCH_SUMMARY_MAX` (400), stored on the record as `summary` + `summarySrc` (the article it came from, shown under the text so a wrong match is visible). Refresh and Clear beside it. **This is the first Watchlist fetch, and it does not contradict the "never fetched" rule on the info links** — the constraint is *no API key* (this repo is public), which is why Sports and Weather already call keyless APIs; it runs only on tap, never on load, sync or render, and the stored text reads offline afterwards. **`watchWikiQuery` is the single definition of the question asked** — the link and the API get the same words, via one shared `WATCH_WIKI_KIND_TERM`; do NOT let them drift into two. **`watchIsDisambig` refuses a disambiguation hit rather than storing it** (v466's lesson: "Dune may refer to:" is worse than no summary). **Retitling an entry clears its summary** — it described the old title. Both fields store `""`, never a dropped key (v296). Extract is `esc()`'d at render. **Tests: `30-watchlist.js` EXTENDED** (+12 assertions; the network call itself is deliberately not exercised — a test that hits Wikipedia is a flake with a cable attached). PR to follow.

- **v466: the Watchlist's Wikipedia link says which kind it is (06/09/2026).** `WATCH_INFO_SOURCES` gains an optional `kindTerm` map, and only Wikipedia carries one: `film` → `"film"`, `tv` → `"TV series"`, appended inside the same `encodeURIComponent` as the title. Its base also gains **`fulltext=1`**. `watchInfoLinks` reads the kind through `watchKindMeta`, so a missing or junk kind reads as Film rather than `undefined`. "FBI" now finds the show, not the government agency. **The first cut used Wikipedia's own parenthetical page title (`Dune (film)`) to ride the exact-title jump, and Cathal broke it on the PR link in two taps**: that article exists only when there is a single film of the name, and otherwise `X (film)` is a REDIRECT TO THE DISAMBIGUATION PAGE — Dune and Wall Street both landed there, worse than before the change. `fulltext=1` skips the jump and always returns a ranked list. **Do NOT restore the parenthetical or drop `fulltext=1`** to win one-hop landings for the few titles where the bare article exists. **Do NOT give JustWatch or IMDb a `kindTerm`** — both match on the title itself. **Tests: `30-watchlist.js` EXTENDED** (+7 assertions, including a named `fulltext=1` tripwire). PR to follow.

- **v465: Baby medicine history follows administration time (05/09/2026).** The history inside Baby → Medicine was sorted by record `id`, which reflects when an entry was created, not when the medicine was administered. Backfilling a dose or correcting its date/time could therefore leave the visible chronology wrong indefinitely, even though the Home medicine summaries already used `ts`. One shared comparator now orders both `renderMedicine()`'s copied display list and the datetime editor's saved array newest-first by `ts`, with `id` only as a deterministic tie-break; records without an administration time sink below timed records. Sync, interval advisories and dosing formulas are unchanged. **Test: `68-medicine-history-order.js` ADDED** (7 assertions covering initial order, the real edit/Save path, persisted order, reload and the no-time fallback). 68/68 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v464: test runner baseline-order bug + paste-dispatch fence-stripping (council review ④) (05/09/2026).** `tests/run.js`'s per-case error baseline was captured AFTER `beforeEach`'s reset ran, not before — so a page error thrown during that reset (a fresh navigate/reload between case files) was folded into the baseline and never surfaced; a genuine startup failure reported as a silent green run. Baseline now captured first. **Paste dispatch:** the backlog named one site; there were **four** — `renderPlantImport`, `renderInventoryImport`, `parsePrepReply`, `recipesFromImportText` — each hand-rolling a trim/strip-```-fence/trim sequence. Checked all four individually rather than assuming they matched: three already trimmed before stripping (correct order); only `recipesFromImportText` stripped the fence BEFORE trimming, so a leading blank line (routine from a chat paste) left the fence in place, its `t.charAt(0)==="{"` dispatch check failed, and it silently fell through to the CSV parser with the untouched original text — a valid JSON reply produced zero recipes and no error at all. All four now call **one** shared `stripPasteFence(s)` (trim, strip fence, trim again) instead of their own copy, closing off future independent drift even where the order was already right. **Tests: `67-paste-fence-dispatch.js` ADDED** (10 assertions: leading whitespace, a leading newline before the fence, malformed JSON, a bare object, and a real CSV paste still dispatching correctly). No dedicated automated test for the runner fix itself — reproducing a reset-time page error requires modifying the harness's own scaffolding, so it's verified by direct code inspection plus a full green run instead. 67/67 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v463: custom-category HTML injection (council review ③) + a logout token leak (05/09/2026).** The backlog spec named 2 sites, the preflight grep widened that to 8 — the actual count was **11**: re-grepping past the exact `"+c.X+"` pattern also caught `cardHTML`'s `c.fg`-derived `leftColor` (an item-card's left border, built as `c.fg+"33"`), the add-item category chip's `elCatChip.innerHTML = c.emoji+" "+c.name` (a direct innerHTML assignment, the most exploitable of the eleven), and the Home spotlight card's `cat.emoji`/`bg`/`fg`/`name`. `name` goes through `esc()`; bg/fg through one top-level `catColorSafe(v,dflt)` (accepts only `/^#[0-9a-f]{3,8}$/i`, else `dflt`). **`emoji` does NOT go through `esc()`** — every real category emoji is stored as an HTML numeric entity (`&#x1F966;`) meant to render raw, and the first cut of this fix wrapped it in `esc()` like `name`, which turned `&` into `&amp;` and showed literal entity text instead of the emoji on every category, everywhere. Caught from a screenshot on the live PR, not by the test suite (which only proved nothing *executable* landed, never that a normal category still looked right). Fixed with its own gate, `catEmojiSafe(v,dflt)` (`/^&#x[0-9a-f]{1,8};$/i`, else `dflt`) — see `HEARTH-notes.md` § critical quirks 14. **Do NOT add a second colour- or emoji-validation gate anywhere**, the v428 lesson. Note ids (`n.id`) escaped into their three `data-noteid` attributes too. **Logout:** `logout()` now also clears `hearthIdToken`/`hearthTokenExpiry` (memory) and `hearth_refresh_token` (storage); `getAuthToken()` checks `isLoggedIn()` *before* returning any cached token, not after — a logged-out device no longer keeps working auth. Offline data stays readable post-logout, unchanged. **Tests: `65-category-note-injection.js` + `66-logout-clears-tokens.js` ADDED** (30 assertions, including a regression pin that a real category's emoji still decodes on screen). 66/66 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v457: the food journal’s meal picker is a dropdown that defaults itself (03/09/2026).** The cycling `#foodMealToggle` button is replaced by a native `<select>` (`#foodMealSelect`), options built from `MEAL_TYPES` in `renderFoodView` so a new meal type needs no markup edit. Its starting value comes from **`foodDefaultMeal(date)`**: Breakfast when nothing is logged for that day, otherwise the meal of the **highest-id** entry on it. **Derived from the log, per day — do NOT simplify this into remembering the last meal in a variable**: that carries today’s Dinner onto an empty Tuesday when the date nav moves, and is wrong for a recipe logged to a past date. A hand-picked meal sets `foodMealPicked`, which suppresses the re-derive until the date nav moves — **do NOT drop that flag**, a delete re-renders and would otherwise silently move the next log to an earlier meal. Old default was Lunch; the first item of a day is now Breakfast. UI only, no data-model, sync or export change. Small, well-defined build on Opus. **Tests: `61-food-meal-dropdown.js` ADDED** (18 assertions). 61/61 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v456: an optional note per exercise on Pull/Push Day, and a history card that opens (03/09/2026).** Each exercise on a superset card now carries a `Notes (optional)` box, saved onto the exercise record as `notes` and written as `""` rather than a dropped key when empty (v296). **The note key is `wktNoteKey(ssId, ex)` — no round in it**, so one note covers all three rounds of an exercise and saves once; it is wired on `input`, not `change`, because "Done with round" can advance while the box still has focus. **It is deliberately NOT prefilled from last time the way the numbers are** (`wktLastEntries`) — last week's "shoulder twinged" re-saved as today's note is worse than no note; do NOT "finish" the v437 prefill by adding notes to it. Tapping a history card's summary row now opens `wktHistoryDetailHTML`: every logged set, the kg volume, the note, and session name/kcal — unlogged sets are left out, cardio/yoga records fall back to what they hold, everything `esc()`'d. **Tests: `60-workout-notes.js` ADDED** (18 assertions). 60/60 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v455: an AI prompt for recipe import (03/09/2026).** The recipe import sheet's single paste box now takes an AI chat's `recipe-v1` JSON reply as well as a CSV export — `recipesFromImportText` dispatches on whether the pasted text starts with `{` (after stripping ```` ```json ```` fences some chats add anyway). A **"Copy the prompt"** button (mirrors Plants v378's `plantAIPrompt`/`plantCopyPrompt`) generates the prompt: paste a recipe's text/photo/link into a chat, get back `{"hearth":"recipe-v1", name, servings, category, ingredients, prep, method, notes, kcal, url}` (or a `recipes:[...]` list for several). `ingredients` stays one "amount unit name" line per ingredient — the editor's own textarea shape — so it runs through the ordinary `parseIngredients`/`tidyIngredient` pass, not a new ingredient format. **Add-only, exactly like the existing CSV path**: a name already in the book is skipped as a duplicate, never matched-and-updated, so no existing recipe's favourites/`lastCooked`/grocery prefs are ever in this import's blast radius — this is deliberately narrower than the "general recipe JSON export/import" the backlog flags as needing its own design confirm (HEARTH-backlog.md, Recipe prep follow-ups ⓪). No new fields, no export, no sync/merge change. Small, well-defined build; plain Sonnet. Tests: 34/19 (recipe CSV import) unaffected, unchanged assertions.

- **v454: Prescription expiry on Health, surfaced on Home (02/09/2026).** A new `expiry` date on the Health record — "Prescription runs out" — and a second self-suppressing Home line when one is close. **Medication-only BY THE FIELD TABLE**: `healthExpiryDays` returns `null` for any kind whose `HEALTH_KIND_FIELDS` row has `expiry:""`, so there is no second "is this a medication" test anywhere and **do NOT add one**. `HEALTH_EXPIRY_SOON_DAYS` is 14 (confirmed with Cathal); `healthExpiringSoon()` sorts soonest-first and Home collapses to a count above one, the plants rule. **An expired repeat deliberately does NOT self-suppress** — unlike `healthCountdownLabel`, `healthExpirySoonLabel` keeps returning a phrase once past and Home turns the line red, because a repeat that has already run out is the most actionable state, not the least; **do NOT simplify this into matching the countdown's "" for anything past.** `healthDMY` was split out of `healthDateText` so `expiry` escapes through the *same* code, not a second copy — it arrives from sync and restored backups too. Reverses v449's "no medication-renewal nudge" note; that was the fresh call the backlog asked for, and nothing from the history itself reaches Home. Still no push, no alert. Every record stores `expiry` even when empty (v296). **The date is paired with a Clear button** — the grocery due-date / item-sheet pattern — because an `<input type="date">` has no reliable clear affordance on a phone, and an optional date that puts a line on Home must be unsettable without deleting the record; the row above it has none only because a record's own date is effectively required. Tests changed: `58-health.js` (+16 assertions), `38-test-build-seed.js` (+2, demo repeat expires in 9 days).

- **v453: Watchlist colour scheme + four small fixes from the 02/09 design review (02/09/2026).** Cathal sent a screenshot: one purple everywhere, state only a 19px glyph, kind only a same-sized emoji, two chip rows that looked like the same control. Four hues now, no more: purple = section, green = watched, teal = TV, amber = Film. **State on the card**: Watching gets a purple left stripe + lavender tint, Watched a green stripe + grey tint, To watch stays plain white (it's the bulk of the list); group headers and selected status chips take the same colours; the To-watch circle darkened from `#BEB6D0`. **Kind is a `FILM`/`TV` text pill** (per-kind `tag`/`color`/`bg` on `WATCH_KINDS`); the emoji survive only on the filter chips, whose selected state now takes the kind colour. Also: the watched date shows on watched rows (`watchDateText`, backlog ④); **`watchedAt` is kept when leaving watched** — a stray tap on the cycling tick then a corrective tap loses nothing, the rating rule applied to the date (`tests/cases/30-watchlist.js` assertion flipped deliberately); 🎲 respects the Films/TV chip and only resets the status chip; editor title auto-capitalises and Enter saves, stale "what season you're on" placeholder trimmed. No data-model, sync or export change. Design notes: `HEARTH-watchlist-design-notes.md`.

- **v452: the bottom bar caps at five icons and grows a "More" button (01/09/2026).** With Watchlist on by default the bar was already seven icons wide, and every opt-in section made it worse. Now `NAV_ITEMS` (the bar in DOM order) is split by `applySectionVisibility()`: the first five visible sections stay on the bar, a **•••  More** button appears at the right-hand end, and the rest go into a sheet behind it. Five or fewer and the button is hidden. **The count deliberately includes `lists`, `recipes` and `baby`, which have no Settings toggle** (design call with Cathal, 01/09/2026) — counting only `HIDEABLE_SECTIONS` would pin those three to the bar on top of five toggled ones, making it wider than before. The sheet is built from the nav buttons themselves (icon from `.bn-ico`, label from `aria-label`) so it cannot drift from the bar, and a row **clicks the section's own nav button rather than calling `switchSection`** — `bnWatch`, `bnHealth` and others reset view state first, and reimplementing that is how the two drift. `syncMoreActive()` gives More the highlight while an overflow section is open. The sheet reuses the shared `.overlay`/`.open` pattern, so the v322 back-button handler closes it with no new branch. No data-model or sync change. **Tests: `59-nav-more.js` ADDED** (20 assertions) and **`01-boot-smoke.js` EXTENDED** — it now closes the sheet after the click sweep and exempts `bnMore` from `NAV_MAP` **by name**; do NOT relax that to "any id not in the map", or an unwired new button stops tripping the coverage assertion. 59/59 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v451: Watchlist gets a Watching / To watch / Watched filter that remembers the last choice, plus a Random pick button (01/09/2026).** A second chip row sits below the existing Films/TV chips — All / Watching / To watch / Watched — filtering the same three status groups the screen already stacked. Unlike the kind filter (session-only), the status choice persists in `hearth_watchStatusFilter` — device-local, `hearth_`-namespaced like the auth keys so a test-build wipe leaves it alone — and is reused as the default next time the section opens. Picking Watched forces that group open instead of also requiring the separate collapse tap. Both chip rows only render when there's an actual choice (the existing v379 rule, now applied to status too). The new 🎲 header button picks uniformly from everything not marked watched (todo + watching both count), resets both filters to All so the pick is always the one row visible, opens it inline and scrolls it into view. UI-only, no data-model or sync change. `tests/cases/30-watchlist.js` unaffected (68 assertions, still green). 58/58 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v450: the Health nav icon actually opens Health (31/08/2026).** Cathal's report from the live app: the icon was on the bar and tapping it did nothing. **`bnHealth` was the only bottom-nav button in the file with no click handler** — v449 wired the section into sixteen places and missed the one that binds the tap, so it was reachable by global search and by the Home countdown but not by its own icon. Also adds the missing `.bn-btn.health-active` colour, a class v449 applied but never defined. **Why nothing caught it:** `01-boot-smoke.js` already clicked every nav button, but only asserted nothing *threw* — and a button wired to nothing throws nothing — and it skipped `display:none` buttons, which is every opt-in section. Both holes closed: each button, hidden ones included, is now clicked from a *different* section and must land on its own, with a `NAV_MAP` coverage assertion for buttons added later. **Do NOT re-narrow that sweep to visible buttons** — hidden-by-default is exactly where this hides. **Tests: `01-boot-smoke.js` EXTENDED** (10 → 12 assertions), **verified by negative control**: with the handler removed the suite fails with exactly one failure, `bnHealth -> home (expected health)`. 58/58 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v449: a Health section — the family's medical history, per person (31/08/2026).** 🩺 opt-in nav icon, OFF by default, household-shared. GP visits and what was said, conditions, diagnoses, test results, medication, vaccinations and clinic contacts. One store `fl4_health` holding **one record type with a `kind`** (seven of them), the way a trip booking carries a type — seven stores would have meant seven merges, seven tombstone sets and seven pieces of the backup payload to keep in step forever. **Three role lists, not the kind order, decide where a record renders**: upcoming (visit/test — things you attend), standing (condition/medication — states, pinned above the history), history (visit/diagnosis/test/vaccination — the events). **A booked appointment is just a visit whose date hasn't come yet**; on the day you open the same record and write what was said, which is why there is no separate appointment kind. Screen order: Coming up → Conditions → Medication → History → Contacts. **The History group is ONE mixed reverse-chronological feed** — that is what makes it a history rather than a filing cabinet; **do NOT split it back into a group per kind.** An undated event sinks to the bottom, never the top. Every record carries a `files` list of labelled links — letters, scans, results — **LINKS, never bytes** (the v428 rule); do not add uploads. `person` stays free text with a datalist, never an enum. Whole-record newest-wins by `updated` + `fl4_tomb_health` on both channels, exactly like Plants/Watchlist/Inventory, with `files` riding inside the record so the nested array needs no merge of its own. Home gets **one** self-suppressing Today line — the next future visit or test — and nothing else. **This section deliberately LOGS NOTHING.** Track has the household medicine log (v380) and Body; Baby has the dosing calculator. **Do NOT add a medicine log, a dose calculator or a weight field here.** **Do NOT add a medication-renewal nudge to Home without asking.** **`files` and `phone` are the only things that become hrefs, and both are rebuilt rather than emitted** — every file url through the shared `applianceLinkUrl` at save, on import AND at render; `phone` as a `tel:` href from digits only. `healthDateText` escapes its parts for the same reason. That is the 30/08 audit's F3 finding applied from the start; **never add a second copy of either gate.** Every field stores `""`/`[]` when empty, never a dropped key, or `mergeHealthData`'s v296 fill resurrects it. **Written first as a forward-looking reference book and REFRAMED before merge** on Cathal's call — the old shape led with "Coming up" and grouped by kind; `HEALTH_KIND_ALIASES` maps its `appointment`/`prescription` records onto visit/medication rather than trusting the unknown-kind fallback to be right by luck, and `healthFileList` reads its single `link` field forward. **Three separate things in the file are now called "health" and none are each other** — the section, the pre-existing always-true `syncPrefs.health` key, and the grocery CATEGORY id. Commented at the `syncPrefs` line; do not tidy them together. **Tests: `58-health.js` ADDED** (102 assertions, including tripwires on both href gates, the date escaping, the mixed-feed order and the undated-sinks rule); `38-test-build-seed.js` EXTENDED for the nine demo records. 58/58 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v448: every Full Body pose gets short cues, with the full paragraph one tap away (30/08/2026).** The pose screen used to show one long paragraph while a timer ran — accurate, but not readable mid-pose. Each of the **35** Full Body poses (both sides of every v447 switch, not just the reviewed ones) now carries a `cues` array of 3–5 short bullets, shown by default, with **Cues / Details** tabs above them. Details holds the **unchanged** reviewed paragraph plus the hold time. Design confirmed with Cathal first: Cues is the default on every pose, and Details does not stick. **The reset is DERIVED — `yogaPoseView()` returns Details only while `SS.yViewFor === SS.yPoseIdx`. Do NOT replace that with `SS.yView = "cues"` at each advance:** there are four advance paths and a fifth added later would silently inherit the previous pose's Details view. **A pose with no `cues` array shows the paragraph with NO tabs** (the whole Hips & Lower Back flow), so tabs only appear where a second view exists — do not "fix" that by faking a one-item cue list. Worth recording: the first cut of the injection hung `cues` off the *slot* object rather than the pose for the seven `orig:[ … ]}` lines, because it inserted before the last `}` on the line instead of the pose's own; the new test caught it, and the fix tracks brace depth. **Tests: `57-pose-cues-details.js` ADDED** (19 assertions, with a move-the-index-by-hand tripwire for the derived reset). 57/57 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v447: the two Full Body Flows become one, with the substituted poses switchable (30/08/2026).** Cathal's call after comparing them: keep the review's **additions** outright, and make its **substitutions** a choice. So `YOGA_FLOWS_TRAIN` drops from three flows to two. Scapular Press, Supported Child's Pose and Supported Tree L/R are now plain members of the flow; the six replaced poses plus the dropped nerve floss are **slots** — `YOGA_FB_BASE` carries `{slot:'id'}` markers, `YOGA_FB_SLOTS` holds both versions, `buildFullBodyFlow()` expands them against **`fl4_yoga_variants`**. Picked on the session intro (new `.yv-*` picker, `aria-pressed`, 32px targets), remembered, and the pose count, duration and programme card all follow the choice. Design confirmed with Cathal before building: switches on the intro, floss as a seventh on/off defaulting to off, reviewed as the default. **An unset OR corrupt slot value resolves to the REVIEWED side — do NOT invert that default**, it would silently switch off the work done for the foraminal stenosis, the L4/L5 bulge and the hernia. **Do NOT flatten `YOGA_FB_BASE` back into a plain pose array** (the slots are the feature) and **do NOT assume a fixed flow length** — `kneehug` is one-to-two. The session reads **`SS.yFlow`**, built once in `openYogaSession`; `YOGA_FLOWS_TRAIN[0]` is only the at-load snapshot and nothing downstream may read it. `fl4_yoga_variants` is deliberately device-local — not synced, not exported, not seeded — to avoid the half-wired `secVisible`/`syncPrefs` shape the 30/08 audit flagged. `YOGA_TRAIN_TOTALS` replaced by `yogaFlowTotal(flow)` since totals are no longer fixed; **`YOGA_TRAIN_DESCS` deleted — it was already dead**, zero readers. **Tests: `56-fullbody-variants.js` ADDED** (37 assertions, corrupt-value-fails-safe tripwire); `55-train-safety-cues.js` sections 5–6 rewritten, since "absent from the file" became "absent unless he asks". 56/56 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v446: Pull Day, Push Day and Full Body Flow re-reviewed against a more precise diagnosis (30/08/2026).** Cathal supplied the exact history — a **4 cm hiatus hernia, mild bilateral L5/S1 _foraminal_ stenosis and an L4/L5 disc bulge**. That is not the condition the v415 review was written for. Foraminal narrowing (not central canal) closes on **extension, side-bending and rotation**; an L4/L5 bulge is loaded by **end-range and loaded flexion**. So the generic "flex to open the canal" prescription is only half right for him, and the target became the **middle of the range in both directions**. `WK_TRAIN` gains a shared `note` (shown on the workout intro screen in the same amber box the yoga and physio intros use) and a `cue` per exercise (shown on each superset card, new `.ss-ex-cue`); conditioning too. **Then re-cut against his actual kit — a pull-up bar (assisted only), rings and a step ladder** — because the first pass cued an incline bench and a chair he does not own. Three `name` changes: **Rows → Ring Rows** (rings put the spine in a straight unloaded line and self-regulate the load by foot angle, where an unsupported bent-over row is loaded lumbar flexion plus a brace), **Pull-ups / Inverted Rows → Assisted Pull-ups** (superset A is now the horizontal pull, so B is unambiguously the vertical one, and assisted is the prescription rather than a fallback) and **Dips → Bench Dips**, cued off a ladder rung, with ring dips explicitly ruled out. The **rear-foot rung height** on the split squat is the sharpest equipment finding: a high rear foot drives the lumbar extension that closes the foramen, so the cue calls for a LOW rung. Load is light dumbbells and a kettlebell, so the three `weighted` exercises keep their weight box and the cues say where the load goes — dumbbells hanging at the sides (under the centre, not out in front), the kettlebell in the hand **opposite** the standing leg on the single-leg deadlift, where it counterbalances the reach instead of pulling him into a twist. The ring row is the exception and says so: nothing to load, log the reps. **Do NOT re-type those three to `reps`** — that was asked and answered. Nine cues re-tuned in Full Body Flow (Revised) — twists and side stretches made small and supported, the fold propped, Cat capped at mid-range, cross-legged sitting perched on a block. `YOGA_TRAIN_NOTES[2]` rewritten; **`YOGA_TRAIN_NOTES[0]` written for the first time**. **Exercise `id`s are FROZEN — they key the saved session and `wktLastEntries`' prefill. Do NOT "tidy" an id to match a renamed exercise;** the two renames deliberately go through `name` only, which costs the prefill for those two (matched by name) and nothing else — and the weights would not have carried over anyway. **The original Full Body Flow (index 0) is deliberately still byte-for-byte unchanged** — v415's side-by-side call stands — **so its new note is the only thing naming the four poses to modify (Cat-Cow, Dead Bug, Bridge Pose, double Knees to Chest). Do NOT blank it while the flow stays as it is.** Out of scope and NOT touched, logged in the backlog: the Hips & Lower Back flow and the Decompression programme both still carry central-canal "flexion opens the spinal canal" wording. **Tests: `55-train-safety-cues.js` ADDED** (29 assertions — frozen-id, no-plain-Dips/Rows, and an equipment block pinning that the cues name kit he owns and that the dip cue keeps ruling out ring dips); `48-workout-prefill.js` fixture renamed to follow. 55/55 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.
- **v445: a restored saved meal stops disappearing, and an import finally says what it restored (30/08/2026).** Three fixes from the 30/08 audit plus one Cathal found reviewing them. **(1)** v443 gave saved meals deletion tombstones for the sync merge but left `importBackupData` alone, so a restored meal reappeared and the next pull filtered it straight back out. The restore now CLEARS the tombstone, like recipes/plants/watchlist/appliances beside it. Resurrecting is deliberate (Cathal, 30/08): saved meals follow the list-item rule, **not** the medicine/growth "a delete of safety data must stick" rule. **Do NOT convert this into a tomb-FILTER** — that reintroduces the bug, and v443 deliberately avoided filtering here so a test-build re-seed can still restore the demo meals. **(2)** Reviewing (1), a restored meal AND a restored food entry both reported **"Nothing new to import"**: `importedSummary` knew only lists/recipes/trips/notes, so nine other collections restored silently. All thirteen are counted now via the `IMPORT_LABELS` table, falling back to a plain total past three kinds so the toast fits a phone. **A restore that added records must NEVER summarise as `""`** — that is exactly what becomes "Nothing new to import", and a silent restore reads as a broken backup file. Add a counter whenever you add a store to the restore. **(3)** The handler called `renderList()` and nothing else, from a Settings screen, so nothing repainted until you navigated away and back; now `flushSyncRenders` (which carries its own per-section guards) plus an explicit `renderTrackView()` for the active Track tab. **(3b)** Same shape again, found in the same review: **deleting** a saved meal called `render()`, which rebuilds only the manager sheet, so the chips in the Food view behind it kept the deleted meal until you left the section. `renderFoodView()` now runs on delete too. **This version fixed four instances of ONE pattern — a mutation repaints its own view and forgets the view beside it** (the v424 watchlist flush, the import repaint, this delete, and the import summary's blind spot). Assume there are more: when adding a mutation, ask what else on screen reads that store. **(4)** Piggybacks: `watchlist` added to both `flushSyncRenders` calls (the v424 backlog item), `appliances` added to the EXPORT COVERAGE comment, and the notes' Current-version line, which v443 left reading v442. **Tests: `53-saved-meal-delete-sync.js` EXTENDED**, 11 → 26 assertions, with tripwires on the tomb-clear, the non-empty summary and the on-screen repaint — **all three verified to fail when their fix is removed.** Note the repaint tripwire asserts the **DOM**, not the store: every storage-level assertion in that file passed while the delete was visibly broken, which is exactly why it reached Cathal. When a fix is about what the user SEES, assert the screen. 54/54 + 4 SW-phase green. [PR #204](https://github.com/coshea321/miniature-journey/pull/204)

- **v444 was WITHDRAWN, not shipped (30/08/2026).** It would have guarded the service worker against caching a Cloudflare Access login page as the app shell (audit finding F6). Building it disproved the finding: a page navigation carries `redirect: "manual"`, so the worker's background refresh resolves to an **opaqueredirect with `status === 0`**, which the existing `status === 200` check already refuses. **The scenario cannot happen and there is nothing to fix.** Found only because the new test still passed with the guard removed — the negative control is the reason this never shipped. **Do NOT re-open F6 from reading that `status === 200` line**; it is sufficient, and `HEARTH-audit-2026-08-30.md` holds the measurements. The version number v444 is deliberately skipped.

- **v443: deleting a saved meal now sticks, and the home screen shows the day's calories (28/08/2026).** Cathal's report — "the demo usual breakfast comes back every time I delete it". `fl4_saved_meals` was the **one** personal collection merged without deletion tombstones: `applyPersonal` unioned local and incoming by id, so any device still holding a copy re-added it on the next pull — and demo data that reached the cloud before the v420 push guards could therefore never be deleted at all. Now tombstoned exactly like `food_log` and `track_med` beside it: `addTomb` on delete, `saved_meals_deleted` in the push payload, filtered on merge. **Do NOT drop the tombstone filter to "simplify" that union back into a plain by-id merge** — that is the bug; a saved meal's id is `Date.now()` at creation, so a tombstone can never block a newly saved meal, and the 90d purge keeps the map tiny. `importBackupData` is deliberately left alone: the test-build seed is applied through it, so tomb-filtering there would stop a re-seed restoring the demo meals. The new **🔥 Calories today** home card measures "on track" against **pace** — the goal spread over an 07:00–21:00 window, band a fifth of the goal with a 300 floor — not just the running total. **There is deliberately NO "behind" state**: eating less than planned is not something to nag about, so under pace reads as on track and only ahead-of-pace or over-goal ever colour the bar. Self-suppressing — hidden until something is logged today. Reads `fl4_cal_goal`, not the TDEE card's computed goal, so it says something useful before any height/weight profile exists. **Tests: `53-saved-meal-delete-sync.js` ADDED** (11 assertions, resurrection tripwire) and **`54-home-calories-card.js` ADDED** (17, no-"behind"-state tripwire). 54/54 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v442: the food journal suggests as you type, and a saved meal can become a recipe (27/08/2026).** Two asks in one version. Typing two or more characters into "What did you eat?" opens a panel above the add form offering **past log entries (\U0001F553) and recipe names (\U0001F4D6)**, ranked name-start > word-start > anywhere, then by how often it was logged. A tap fills the text and, where there is one, the calories. Datalist was rejected on the repo's own v-recipe-category note that Android often won't surface it \u2014 this is a plain div panel inside the sticky add form. **Do NOT prefill an auto-ESTIMATED figure from a past entry.** A suggestion carries only a hand-typed kcal (or a recipe's per-serving figure); an estimate is left for `estimateCals` to regenerate, so the entry stays flagged `calAuto` instead of a guess being laundered into a number that looks checked \u2014 the v434 rule, applied to the suggestion path. **Saved Meals** now has a "\U0001F4D6 Make recipe" button per row: it **copies**, never moves, each logged line becomes an ingredient via `parseIngredients`, servings starts at 1, and the same calorie rule applies \u2014 the total is only carried into `kcal` when **every** item was hand-typed; one estimated line blanks it. A clashing name is refused, and a converted row flips to "Open", which is a second deliberate tap rather than the manager teleporting you to Recipes. **Tests: `51-food-autosuggest.js` ADDED** (26 assertions) and **`52-saved-meal-to-recipe.js` ADDED** (24), both with the estimate-must-not-be-promoted tripwire. Piggyback: the `HIDEABLE_SECTIONS` comment and the `HEARTH-notes.md` size parenthetical. 52/52 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v439:** **TDEE — the food journal's calories, the bodyweight log and your height/age/sex combined into a daily burn, a weight-loss goal and a food target (26/08/2026).** A "🔥 Daily Calories" card at the top of **Track → Body** (written as "Train → Body" in the original entry and corrected 27/08/2026 — Body has been a Track sub-view since v339; the `trainBodyView` div id is a deliberate leftover, not a location). Two estimates, crossfaded by how much data exists: **formula** (Mifflin-St Jeor BMR × an activity multiplier, from a new `fl4_profile` store) alone under a 14-day weight span, **measured** (mean intake minus the least-squares slope of the weight trend × 7,700) alone from 28 days, blended between. The goal is a rate (0.25/0.5/0.75 kg per week), through two hard guards: never below 1,500 kcal, never a deficit above 25% of TDEE, and never a goal above the burn. Writes `fl4_cal_goal` behind a button, never silently. **Do NOT add the Train section's calories to the TDEE or the goal.** A weight-derived TDEE already contains every workout that helped cause the weight change; adding the Train kcal on top double-counts them, inflates the target by hundreds of kcal, and produces an app that says "on track" while nothing moves. Training burn is displayed as context with a "don't eat it back" line and is deliberately never a term in any sum. **Do NOT treat an unlogged food day as a zero-calorie day** — the mean is taken over logged days only and the whole measured run is refused below 80% coverage; averaging in the blanks understates intake and so understates TDEE. Two fixes ride along: cardio priced itself off a hardcoded 70 kg while workout sessions used the logged weight, so the same effort logged a different burn depending on which half of Train it went through (both now use `estimateSessionKcal`); and the 28-day window used `> cutoff`, dropping the reading taken exactly 28 days ago — found by the new test. **Tests: `49-tdee.js` ADDED** (56 assertions) with a deliberate tripwire — a 1,500 kcal training day must not move the TDEE or the goal by one calorie; if it ever fails, someone has wired exercise back into the sum. `38-test-build-seed.js` EXTENDED to 38. 49/49 + 4 SW-phase green. PR to follow.

- **v438:** **Push/Pull days open pre-filled with last time's numbers, and the rest between sets is 90s not 50s (26/08/2026).** `restSec` is 90 on all four supersets (the intro card's "50s rest" line is hardcoded separately — it moved too). The prefill reads the last saved session with that `sessionName` out of `fl4_workouts` and matches **per exercise name, per round**, so round 3 opens on last time's round 3 rather than round 1 smeared across all three. **`wktFillEntry` writes what it shows back into `SS.wEntries`** — the value must be a real value, never a placeholder, because the `change` handler only fires on an edit and an untouched round would otherwise save 0, which is the exact opposite of "only edit if needed". `SS.wTyped` separates a number typed today (wins for later rounds) from one merely pre-filled (doesn't). A 0 last time stays blank; free-form log entries carry no `sessionName` and are never a source. **Tests: `48-workout-prefill.js` ADDED** (10 assertions). 48/48 + 4 SW-phase green.

- **v437:** **The Calorie helper can be pointed at one section, and opens on Favourites (26/08/2026).** A chip row on the copy step — All · ⭐ Favourites · every category in use — built the same way the recipe list builds its own, and **defaulting to Favourites with the same fallback to All when nothing is favourited**, because that is the section Cathal works in and any other default means a chip tap every single time. `calHelperPool(includeAll, filter)` takes the section as a second argument; omitting it keeps the pre-v437 "every section" behaviour, so no existing call changed. **The section and `includeAll` are separate axes and must stay that way** — section says WHICH recipes, `includeAll` says whether the ones already priced are among them; a change that makes one replace the other silently sends out the wrong set. The copy button, the toggle and the explainer all read in the section's own noun ("Copy 1 favourite", "Include the 2 favourites that already have a figure"). **The chip row renders on the EMPTY state too** — an exhausted section with no way off it is a dead end, and switching sections is the whole point of the row. A chip tap resets the batch index, which pointed into a pool that no longer exists. **Tests: `47-recipe-calorie-helper.js` EXTENDED** to 63 assertions — the two axes combining rather than replacing, the Favourites default and its no-favourites fallback, the batch reset, and an exhausted section still offering chips to escape with. Demo seed: Fish Pie is now an unpriced favourite, so the default view has something to do. 47/47 + 4 SW-phase green.

- **v436:** **A Calorie helper for Recipes — copy the unpriced recipes into a chat, paste the reply back, review, apply (26/08/2026).** The same round trip as the v397 Prep helper, writing v434's `kcal` field instead of `prep`: a 🔥 Calories chip in the Recipes header, the copy step with the batching and clipboard code reused (`PREP_BATCH_CHARS`, `prepCopyToClipboard`), and the `=== <id>` block reply format reused verbatim — bare integers have even less to get wrong than prep text. The pool **defaults to recipes with no figure yet**, with a toggle to re-price the whole book; the export carries name, servings (the answer is per serving), ingredients and the full untruncated method, because "deep fry in 1L of oil" is the difference between 400 and 900 kcal. Review is a proposal: every number is editable, a recipe that already has a figure starts **unticked**, and nothing is written until Apply. **`applyCalImport` writes `kcal` and nothing else** — that narrowness is the whole safety argument, same as `applyPrepImport`; do NOT widen it to other fields without a fresh design call. **Do NOT change the batch advance to match the prep helper's:** on the default pool the applied recipes drop OUT of it, so the remaining unpriced ones renumber from the start and advancing the index skips a batch's worth of recipes — only the whole-book pool is stable enough to step through. **Tests: `47-recipe-calorie-helper.js` ADDED** (41 assertions), covering the pool default, a fenced reply with units and comma-grouped numbers, junk and out-of-range numbers being reported rather than written, that review writes nothing, and that apply moves `kcal` and `updated` only. Demo seed: two recipes deliberately left unpriced so the helper has something to do on a test build. 47/47 + 4 SW-phase green.

- **v435:** **The food journal's day total now always equals the entries listed under it (26/08/2026).** Spotted in review of the v434 test build: the bar read 1,590 kcal above two entries totalling 820. `renderFoodView` groups the day by `meal` as an index into `MEAL_TYPES` (`e.meal === mi`) but totals it with a plain reduce, so an entry whose `meal` was anything else counted toward the total and the goal bar while rendering under no heading — invisible, unexplained, and only in the number Cathal actually reads. The source was v407's demo seed, which wrote `meal:"breakfast"`/`"lunch"` as name strings; the seed is fixed, and `getFoodLog` now normalises through a new `foodMealIndex` (index in range → itself, a known meal name → its index, anything else → Snack, so it is at least visible). **Normalise in `getFoodLog`, do NOT push the check out to the renderers** — the same one-place contract as `getRecipeBook`'s null guard; spread across readers is how one gets missed again. **Tests:** `46-recipe-to-food-log.js` EXTENDED to 38 assertions (every entry for a day is listed whatever its meal value, and the day total and the per-meal figures both equal the sum of what is listed); `38-test-build-seed.js` EXTENDED to 36 — the seed's meals must be numeric indices, never names. 46/46 + 4 SW-phase green.

- **v434:** **A recipe can now be logged straight into the food journal, priced off a calories-per-serving figure you type on the recipe (26/08/2026).** One new scalar on the recipe, `kcal`, entered in the editor under "Serves"; a 🍳 "Log to food journal" button on the recipe detail view opens a sheet with a portions stepper (half-portion steps, floor 0.5, ceiling 10) and the four meal chips, defaulted from the recipe's category. Logging writes ONE food-log line — `{date: today, meal, text, cal, calAuto:false}` — never one line per ingredient. Recipes merge as whole records by `updated`, so `kcal` rides through sync, backup and export with no field map to edit. **Do NOT "improve" this by estimating from the ingredients.** `FOOD_CAL` is mixed-basis — butter 720 and cheese 400 are per 100g, egg 70 and bread 80 are per item, coffee 5 is per cup — and no per-unit basis is stored to multiply the ingredient amounts against, so a sum is out by a factor of ten on exactly the fatty ingredients that dominate a total. Typed, editable, shown with its working: the medicine-dosing rule applied to food. **Tests: `46-recipe-to-food-log.js` ADDED** (31 assertions) with a deliberate tripwire — the fixture recipe is named "Pizza", which is in `FOOD_CAL` at 270, and carries a typed 800; if 270 ever reaches the log, the estimator has been wired in. 46/46 + 4 SW-phase green.

- **v433:** **A plant file holding several plants can now UPDATE the plants you already have, matched by name, instead of only adding duplicates (24/08/2026).** Each entry in a multi-plant `plant-v1` file gets its own destination dropdown (update a named plant · add as new · skip · retarget to any other plant), defaulted by `plantNameMatches` — exact name, case- and whitespace-insensitive. One match pre-selects; **several start unset and block Import**; none defaults to new. Applied via `importPlantsByPlan`: one `getPlants()`/`savePlants()` for the whole file rather than a loop over `importPlantInto`, which would push the book once per plant. The single-plant screen floats the same-name plant to the top. **Do NOT make the matching fuzzy** — no Levenshtein, prefix or substring. A wrong automatic match silently overwrites the wrong plant's care notes, and the two most similar names in a plant book ("Aloe Vera" / "Aloe Vera 2") are exactly the ones most likely to be different plants. **Tests: `23-plant-import-export.js` EXTENDED** to 79 assertions, including a rendered check that two matches start unset and that Import writes nothing while a row is unchosen. 45/45 + 4 SW-phase green.

- **v432:** **Plants get an optional photo-link field — a URL to a picture of the plant kept somewhere else, distinct from the stored thumbnail (24/08/2026).** New `photoLink` scalar: an input in the editor under the photo chooser, and a "🖼️ Photo of this plant ↗" row on the detail view between the care history and the write-up. Unlike `photo` (base64, deliberately never in a file) `photoLink` DOES travel in a `plant-v1` file — it is a URL, so it costs the paste box nothing — and is hand-listed in both `plantExportObj` and `plantApplyImport`. **It is the only plant field that becomes a tappable href**, so it goes through `applianceLinkUrl` — the appliance links' existing http(s)-only gate — on save, on import, and again at render time via `plantPhotoLinkUrl`. **Do NOT give plants their own copy of that gate**: two copies of a security check is how one of them drifts (the v428 lesson). Import refuses a non-http(s) link rather than storing it, leaving any existing link alone. **Tests: `23-plant-import-export.js` and `38-test-build-seed.js` EXTENDED** (round trip, the `javascript:` refusal, render-gate parity, one seeded plant with a link and one without). 45/45 + 4 SW-phase green. *(Small, well-defined field addition; the session ran on a top-tier model — see the PR body.)*

- **v431:** **Appliances (Home inventory) gets an optional FD number field — the factory-date code on Bosch/Siemens rating plates, alongside model and serial (20/08/2026).** Resurrects PR #185, which was left unmerged while v428–v430 shipped a much wider Appliances→Home inventory model underneath it (value, receipt, manual, photos, the `inventory-v1` file) — rebuilt by hand on top of current `main` rather than merged, since the original diff no longer applied cleanly. Threaded through all **five** hand-listed spots the data-model doc now calls for: `applianceSearchText`, the detail view (`rec.fd`, tap-to-copy, now a third copy row), the editor (`apEdFD` input + save handler), and both halves of the `inventory-v1` file (`inventoryExportObj`/`inventoryRecordFrom`) — the export/import pair didn't exist yet when PR #185 was first written. Plain string field, no merge-contract change (v296 generic field-fill already covers it). **Tests: `tests/cases/44-appliances.js` EXTENDED** — search haystack, the detail view's now-3 copy rows, editor prefill/save, and the file export/import round trip. 45/45 + 4 SW-phase files green. *(Small, well-defined field addition — plain Sonnet, no model-check needed.)*
- **v430:** **Plant reminders can now be switched off individually — a watering toggle for outdoor plants, and a seasonal pause for feeding (Cathal's ask, 20/08/2026).** Two new scalars: `waterOff` (boolean, "Outdoor plant" checkbox) suppresses the watering reminder entirely while keeping `waterDays` intact for if it moves back inside; `feedPauseFrom`/`feedPauseTo` (month selects, e.g. September→April) pause only the feed reminder for that window, wrapping across the year boundary when `from > to`. Both are gated inside `plantDueIn`/new `plantFeedPaused`, so pills, sort order, the care panel's status line ("watering reminder off (outdoor)" / "feed reminder paused for the season") and the Home overdue count all pick them up with no per-caller changes. Added to `plantExportObj`/`plantApplyImport` per the plant scalar-field rule. **Tests: `22-plants.js` and `23-plant-import-export.js` EXTENDED** (gating, the wraparound month arithmetic via a testable `month` override on `plantFeedPaused`, and the export/import round trip). 51/51 files green.
- **v429:** **Swipe left/right now moves between tabs in Baby and Track too, matching the existing Lists gesture (Cathal's ask, 20/08/2026).** One shared touchstart/touchend listener on `document.body` (not per-section) dispatches by `currentSection`, requires mostly-horizontal movement (≥60px, `dy < 0.6×dx`, <600ms), finds the current tab among the visible `.bsn-btn`/`.tsn-btn`/`.type-btn` buttons by its `data-*` attribute, and `.click()`s the neighbour — no wraparound at either end. **Caught pre-merge (Cathal's PR test link) and fixed in the same version, not a v430:** the first cut put the listener on `#lifeSection`/`#babySection`/`#trackSection` themselves, which only extend as tall as their rendered content — on a sparse view (e.g. Baby → Medicine with no doses logged) the blank space below the content isn't part of that div, so touches there never reached the handler. Binding to `document.body` (which always spans the viewport via its `min-height:100dvh`) fixes that for all three sections at once, which is also why the three near-identical IIFEs were merged into one. Guards: Lists still skips while `itemSheet` is open; Baby now also skips while `toothModal` has class `open` (its one full-screen modal); Track has none. No data-model, sync or storage change. No new test file — DOM gesture wiring, same as the untested existing Lists swipe. 45/45 files + 4 SW-phase files green. *(Ran on Sonnet — small, well-defined UI change; no model-check prompt needed.)*
 - **v428:** **Appliances became the Home inventory — the same records, now good enough for an insurance claim (19/08/2026).** Three new fields (replacement value, where the receipt is, a link to the photos), a totals card, and an `inventory-v1` export/import file. **The store is still `fl4_appliances` and every internal name is still `appliance*`** — the key is wired through sync, tombstones, the backup payload and the search registry, so only the labels changed. **No second category field: the v379 area chips already are the room-by-room axis a claim is made on**, so a sofa and a bike just get an area. **Photos are a LINK (OneDrive), never bytes** — photos remain the one thing that threatens the localStorage budget, and a claim wants full-resolution ones anyway. Value stores `""` when cleared, never a dropped key: an undefined field is refilled from the other phone by the v296 merge fill, so a deliberately cleared valuation would come back. A junk value refuses the save rather than storing 0 — **do NOT "improve" that into rounding**. **Import is add-only by design; do NOT turn it into a match-by-name update** without a fresh design call: the worst a bad paste can do now is give you duplicates you can delete, never a silently overwritten valuation. The totals card always names how many records are unvalued — a sum that quietly ignores half the house looks authoritative and is wrong. **Tests: `44-appliances.js` EXTENDED** (value parsing/formatting, the totals card, the photos URL gate, the file round trip, the import screen) and **`38-test-build-seed.js` UPDATED** for a five-record demo that now includes a sofa and a bike. PR #186.
- **v427:** **Token release — a version bump and nothing else, to prove the v426 update path works unattended (19/08/2026).** No code change of any kind: only the two `VERSION`/label strings. The point is the test. Cathal's Android install had been wedged on v421 through v422–v425 (every one of those installs died in `cache.addAll`) and was recovered by hand via `chrome://serviceworker-internals` → Unregister. That proves the worker was the problem; it does NOT prove the fix works. **A device that picks up v427 on its own, with no unregistering, no site-data deletion and no Force app update tap, is the actual evidence.** If it does not, the `addAll` theory was incomplete and the next step is teaching `tests/server.js` to fail a named asset so the install path can be tested properly rather than reasoned about. Deliberately no other change in this version, so the result is unambiguous. Docs in the same PR: the recovery ladder in `HEARTH-notes.md` § sw.js. [PR #184](https://github.com/coshea321/miniature-journey/pull/184)
- **v426:** **A stuck service worker can no longer wedge the app on an old version, and there is now a way out from inside the app (19/08/2026).** **Note the ordering trap this shipped with — the in-app button only helps a device that can already RECEIVE v426; a device already wedged on an older build cannot reach it. Recovery ladder for that case is in `HEARTH-notes.md` § Service worker.** Cathal's Cloudflare install served v421 while incognito served v425; the only known cure was unregistering the worker in devtools. **Cause: `install` used `cache.addAll`, which is all-or-nothing** — one failed asset (a blip, a 404, a redirect to a login page) rejects the whole install, so the new worker never activates and the old one serves forever. Now best-effort per asset: `Promise.all(ASSETS.map(u => c.add(...).catch(() => {})))`. A worker that activates with an incomplete cache is strictly better — the fetch handler falls back to the network and the next open refills it. **Never put `addAll` back.** Plus a **🔄 Force app update** button in Settings: `clearAppInstall()` unregisters every worker and deletes every cache, then reloads. **It deliberately never touches `localStorage`** — data, login and household all survive — which is what makes it safe behind a button. A line under it names both versions ("Running v426 · installer stuck on v421") so the mismatch reads as a stuck installer rather than a failed update. **The v382 label rule was NOT changed:** a same-or-older worker still writes the label, and `tests/sw-cases/02-sw-update-banner.js` pins that — an attempt to change it here failed that test and was reverted rather than rewriting the test. **Tests: `tests/cases/45-force-update.js` ADDED (12 assertions)** — the version-agreement predicate, the control being wired, and that `clearAppInstall()` leaves both an `fl4_` key and `hearth_user` intact. **The `addAll` change itself is NOT directly covered by a test** (making one asset fail on demand needs server support the harness doesn't have); the argument is structural — a `Promise.all` of individually-caught adds cannot reject. 48/48 files green. [PR #183](https://github.com/coshea321/miniature-journey/pull/183)
- **v424:** **New Appliances section — saved records of the machines around the house (18/08/2026).** One record per appliance: name, area, brand, model, serial, bought/warranty dates, where it was bought, a link to the manual, notes. Built on the Plants/Watchlist contract — store `fl4_appliances`, household-shared, whole-record newest-wins by `updated`, `fl4_tomb_appliances` tombstones — plus the v379 area chips (reusing `plantAreas`/`plantAreaKey` by mapping `.area`→`.room` rather than copying them). Model and serial rows are tap-to-copy, which is the moment the section exists for. **Opt-in nav icon, OFF by default**; the way in is the global-search group, which searches model/serial via `applianceSearchText` and is the one group whose tap deep-links to the record itself. **Warranty dates are SHOWN, never alerted on** — no badge, no Home line, no notification; do not add one without a fresh request. **`applianceManualUrl` gates the manual link at BOTH save and render** — `normalizeRecipeUrl` alone would prefix `javascript:alert(1)` into `https://javascript:alert(1)` and pass it. **Tests: `44-appliances.js` ADDED (84 assertions), `38-test-build-seed.js` EXTENDED 28→31, additive only.** 45/45 files green; the manual-link gate verified non-vacuous by removal. *(Ran on Opus — the session started as a design conversation, not a build.)*
- **v423:** **Recipe Focus mode no longer crosses lines out — a tap in Focus moves the highlight and nothing else (Cathal's ask, 17/08/2026).** v421 shipped Focus so that tapping the highlighted line ticked it (grey-out, plus strike-through on ingredients) *and* advanced the cursor. Cathal's call: greying a line out belongs to classic mode only; in Focus the highlight is the whole progress indicator. The Focus branch of the one delegated click listener now just moves `_recipeFocusKey` — tapping the cursor advances to the next line in page order, tapping any other line jumps there. **`_recipeDone` is therefore provably empty for a whole Focus session**, which is what let `recipeFocusFirstUndone` become `recipeFocusKeyAt(nodes, index)`: the cursor is pure page position, no "skip the done ones" search. Unchanged: no wrap past the last line, the classic-tick stash/restore across the toggle, and **off-mode is still byte-for-byte v405**. **Tests changed: `tests/cases/43-recipe-focus-mode.js` EDITED** — the tick-on-tap assertions become never-crosses-out assertions (including a full walk of the recipe ending with `doneKeys().length === 0`); the now-unreachable "jumping onto a ticked line clears it" case is replaced by a jump-back case. `36-recipe-cook-ticks.js` untouched and still green. *(Ran on Opus; small, well-defined change — Sonnet territory.)*
- **v422:** **The service worker no longer answers `/cdn-cgi/` requests — Cloudflare Access's login round trip was being eaten by our own cache (12/08/2026).** `/cdn-cgi/` is Cloudflare's reserved namespace, and it is where Access runs its login redirect and its `/cdn-cgi/access/authorized` callback. Those are **same-origin navigations**, so they took the shell branch of the fetch handler — whose tail is `caches.match(request,{ignoreSearch:true}).then(c => c || caches.match('./index.html'))`, i.e. **any** same-origin navigation that misses the cache is answered with our cached `index.html`. On a device with Hearth installed, Cloudflare's login response was therefore replaced by our own app: the round trip never completes and the Access cookie is never set. One line, next to the existing `sw.js` and cross-origin early-returns: `if (url.pathname.startsWith('/cdn-cgi/')) return;`. Safe by construction — nothing of ours is ever served from `/cdn-cgi/`, so nothing can be excluded by accident. **This was found by reading the code, not from the reported symptom** (an `ERR_FAILED` on the callback in Edge, which did NOT reproduce in Firefox and was probably a stale nonce) — but the test's negative control then demonstrated it outright, so it is an observed bug rather than a predicted one. **Tests: `tests/sw-cases/04-sw-cdn-cgi-passthrough.js` ADDED (5 assertions)** — asserts the precondition (worker in control, shell genuinely cached, so the fallback *could* fire), then drives a real navigation via an iframe to a non-existent `/cdn-cgi/` path and requires the server's 404 rather than our shell. **Verified non-vacuous: removing the one line gives `hearth: true` — the iframe really does receive Hearth's cached shell — and fails exactly that case.** 47/47 files green. **What this does NOT fix, and should not be mistaken for a fix:** an installed device still serves the cached shell without consulting Cloudflare at all, and Firebase is a different origin so it was never gated — so an existing install keeps opening and keeps syncing regardless of Access session state. The gate is a front door, not a lock that re-checks the people already inside. *(Ran on Opus — service-worker territory, on Cathal's standing list. In hindsight a well-defined one-liner: buildable on Sonnet from this entry.)*
- **v421:** **Recipe Focus mode — the design-confirmed feature from the 12/08/2026 Opus chat, built exactly as recorded.** A display layer over the v405 cook-along ticks: a `🔦 Focus` toggle pill on the recipe detail hint row highlights only the next thing to do (ingredients → prep → method, in page order) and dims everything else via a new `.rcp-focusing`/`.rcp-now` CSS pair. **No new stored data on a recipe and nothing synced or exported** — the cursor (`_recipeFocusKey`) is memory-only, living and dying with `_recipeDone`/`resetRecipeTicks` exactly like the ticks it sits on top of; the on/off flag is a device-local `fl4_cookfocus` localStorage key, deliberately left out of `buildExportPayload`. Confirmed shape: tapping the **highlighted** line ticks it and advances the cursor to the next undone line in DOM order; tapping **any other** line just moves the highlight there. **Two calls made in the design chat that Cathal did not explicitly rule on (flagged on the PR for review, not re-litigated here):** jumping onto an already-ticked line clears that line's tick; there is **no wrap** — the cursor goes to `null` once the last reachable line is ticked, even if an earlier line was deliberately skipped, and a re-render (servings tap, sync push) must never revive it. Off-mode is byte-for-byte the existing v405 tap behaviour — the branch in the one delegated click listener is untouched when Focus is off. **Tests: `tests/cases/43-recipe-focus-mode.js` ADDED (38 assertions, sibling to 36 rather than an edit to it)** — pins the off-mode no-op, the cursor starting at the first ingredient, both tap rules, the jump-back-clears-tick rule, the no-wrap finish (including that a servings re-render doesn't revive a skipped line), the flag surviving a switch to a different recipe, and that nothing is ever written to the stored recipe (`updated === 1` pattern). `36-recipe-cook-ticks.js` itself was not changed and still passes unmodified.
- **v420:** **A test build's demo data can no longer leave a device whose host was promoted to production — the root-cause half of the 11/08/2026 incident (v419 fixed the amplifier).** `fl4_testseed` is written **only** by a test build, so finding it while `!_isTestBuild` proves this origin *used to be* one: exactly the Cloudflare Pages case, where the store was seeded as a test build, v418 made the hostname production, and nothing wiped it. **Design confirmed by Cathal before building (11/08/2026): a banner, NOT a boot-time wipe.** The wipe-on-promotion shape was offered and **rejected** — it is destructive, self-triggering, and the incident is a poor argument for letting boot code delete storage unasked. **Do not "simplify" this into an automatic wipe.** New **`_seedTainted`**, computed once next to `wipeTestStore()` (inside a `try` — a locked-down `localStorage` must not blank the app), which ⓐ shows a **red** `seedTaintBanner` (distinct from the orange test-build one, `z-index` 1201 vs 1200) and ⓑ makes **both** `pushPersonal()` and `pushHousehold()` return early. **Receiving is deliberately left ON** — pulling real data down is how you tell the demo records from your own, and a tainted device cannot contaminate anything while sending is off. Clearing is a **tap**: `clearTaintedStore()` reuses `wipeTestStore()` then reloads, and is inert unless `_seedTainted` (belt-and-braces guard, pinned by a test). **Login survives the clear** — `hearth_user`/`hearth_token`/`hearth_household` are outside the `fl4_` namespace — so the reload logs back in and pulls the household down clean, and v419's read-before-push guard is what makes that empty-then-refill sequence safe. **Tests: `tests/cases/42-tainted-store-guard.js` ADDED (6 assertions)** — banner element exists and stays hidden on an untainted page; `_seedTainted` false on an ordinary page; `clearTaintedStore()` wipes nothing when untainted (**the tripwire: if that ever fails, a real device is one boot from wiping itself**); a tainted device makes **zero** network calls from either push; and a positive control proving the same stubbed setup *does* push without the taint. **Verified non-vacuous: removing the two guard lines gives `calls: 2` and fails exactly that one assertion.** **45/45 files green.** *(Ran on Opus — same incident, same sync/data-loss territory as v419.)*
- **v419:** **`pushHousehold()` now refuses to push until this device has successfully READ the household — the household twin of the v411 personal guard (11/08/2026).** `pushPersonal()` has had `if (!_personalFetchOk) return;` since v411; `pushHousehold()` had nothing, and the asymmetry matters *more* on the household side because **the push is a wholesale `PUT` to `/shared`** — a device holding the wrong contents does not merge into the household, it **replaces** it. **This is the mechanism behind the 11/08/2026 demo-data incident** (see the Cloudflare entry below): the Pages origin was promoted to production by v418 while still carrying the test-build demo household in its localStorage, and the merged real+demo state went up and out to every device. New **`_householdFetchOk`**, set by any *authorised read*: ⓐ `fetchHousehold`, which now distinguishes a **401/403** (guard stays closed, reusing the existing `_FETCH_AUTH_FAIL` sentinel that v411 introduced for exactly this) from an **empty household returning a literal `null`** (a real success — Firebase REST answers a not-yet-existing path with null, and reading that as failure is v411's lesson ②); ⓑ `handleHouseholdEvent` on a `"put"`, so a device whose first fetch failed on a blip is not left **silently receive-only** for the whole session (v411's lesson ①, which only shows up because `startSSEFallback` re-fetches solely when the stream goes STALE); ⓒ **`createHousehold` sets it explicitly** — a household we just invented has nothing to read, and without this the creating device could never push into its own new household. Reset on **leave household** and on **logout**. **Scope call worth knowing:** `logout()` now also resets `_personalFetchOk`, which it never did — after a logout/login on the same page the previous session's flag carried over, so a re-login could push before re-reading. Same class of bug, one line, fixed here rather than left as a known hole. **Tests: `tests/cases/41-household-push-guard.js` ADDED (16 assertions)** — guard starts closed; a successful read opens it and applies; an empty (null) household opens it and applies nothing; 401 and 403 each leave it closed while still running the callback; a network error leaves it closed; a failed fetch followed by a live SSE put opens it; `logout()` closes both guards. **Verified non-vacuous, and precisely: removing the flag set in `fetchHousehold` fails exactly the two read-opens-the-guard assertions and nothing else** (the SSE assertion still passes, correctly — it is set on a separate path). **44/44 files green.** *(Ran on Opus. Warranted — sync/merge territory, and the design had two real judgement calls: the create-household path needing an explicit open, and null-vs-401 needing to stay distinguishable.)*
- **v418:** **The Cloudflare Pages hostname is now a production host — first shipped step of the Cloudflare Access move (11/08/2026).** The Pages project is deployed and live at **`miniature-journey-b9p.pages.dev`** (free tier, `pages.dev` first — no custom domain bought, per Cathal's call). `_isTestBuild` read `hostname !== "coshea321.github.io"`, so the new Pages site came up as a **test build**: orange banner, `fl4_*` wiped, demo household seeded, Firebase sync/login hard-blocked. Correct behaviour from a guard that only knew one address. Replaced the single comparison with **`_prodHosts`**, an exact-match allowlist of the two production homes, and `_isTestBuild` is now `!_isPreview && _prodHosts.indexOf(hostname) === -1`. **The exact match is load-bearing and must NOT be loosened to a suffix/wildcard test:** Cloudflare Pages also publishes every branch and every past deployment at `<branch|hash>.miniature-journey-b9p.pages.dev`, and an "ends with `.pages.dev`" check would promote all of them to live Firebase access — the precise thing the test-build guard exists to prevent. `_isPreview` is untouched, so a `file://` page is still a preview (not a test build), which is what the unit phase depends on. **Tests: `tests/cases/38-test-build-seed.js` EXTENDED, 26 → 28 assertions, additive only** — one pins `_prodHosts` to exactly the two intended hostnames, the other asserts a branch-preview host is NOT on it (the tripwire for a future suffix rewrite). `tests/run.js:18` and `tests/sw-cases/01-sw-registers.js:44` still hardcode `coshea321.github.io` and were deliberately **left unchanged** — the SW phase serves under that host and asserts it is not a test build, which remains true and worth pinning. **43/43 files green.** *(Ran on Opus.
