Warranted: it touches the guard that stands between a non-production origin and real Firebase data, and the plan itself was still fuzzy — the "Pages, not Workers" and exact-match-vs-suffix calls were both made during the session.)*
- **v417:** **Plant list now shows next feed alongside next water, sorted soonest-due-first (Cathal's direct ask, 11/08/2026).** Previously `plantDuePill()` was water-only and the list sorted alphabetically by name. `plantDuePill(p)` generalised to `plantDuePill(p, kind)` — same colour logic (red/amber/green by overdue/today/future), icon switches 💧→🌱 for feed, reusing the 🌱 already used for feed elsewhere (`plantCareRowHTML`). `plantCardHTML` now renders both pills stacked in a small flex column at the card's right edge; a plant with `feedDays` unset still shows only the water pill (`plantDuePill` already returns `""` when `plantDueIn` is null). New `plantNextDue(p)` returns the sooner of the water/feed due-in counts (falling back to whichever one has a reminder set, `null` if neither does), and `renderPlants()`'s sort now orders by that ascending — most overdue/soonest-due plant first — with plants that have no reminder at all pushed to the end, alphabetical as the tiebreak within an equal due-count or among the no-reminder group. **No data-model change** — `waterDays`/`feedDays` and their logs already existed; nothing new to add to the plant export/import scalar lists. **Tests: `tests/cases/22-plants.js` EXTENDED, 63 → 69 assertions, additive only.** New unit coverage for `plantNextDue` (feed sooner than water, water sooner than feed, one-sided fallback when only one interval is set, null when neither is) plus an integration test seeding 4 plants (overdue-on-water, due-tomorrow-on-feed, due-later, no-reminder-at-all) into `fl4_plants`, calling `renderPlants()`, and asserting the `.plant-card` DOM order matches the expected soonest-first sequence with the no-reminder plant last. **40/40 files green.** *(Ran on Sonnet — a well-defined UI/sort change to existing due-date data, no sync/merge/dosing/SW/fuzzy-design territory, so no model-check prompt was needed per the standing rule.)*
- **v416:** **Plants care history — the watering/feeding logs are finally readable (backlog item Plants ③, picked by Cathal 11/08/2026 off a backlog-prioritisation review).** `waterLog`/`feedLog` have stored up to `PLANT_LOG_CAP` (40) entries each since v377, and **until now literally nothing read past `log[0]`** — `plantLastTs` takes the newest entry and every other consumer (`plantDueIn`, `plantAgoLabel`, the care panel) works off that one number, so entries 2–40 were written, synced and never shown. This is the display for them: a "📅 Recent care" card in the plant detail view, directly under the water/feed panel. **Three design calls confirmed via AskUserQuestion before building** — one combined list rather than separate Waterings/Feeds lists; **last 5 with a "Show all (N)" toggle**; each row labelled **date + relative** ("Mon 10 Aug · Watered + fed" … "3 days ago"). **The load-bearing decision is that a row is ONE LOCAL DAY, not one stored timestamp**, and it was taken during the build rather than in the design questions because the reason only surfaced from reading `plantLogCare`: ⓐ **v409 makes a feed write a watering at the same millisecond**, so a per-timestamp list renders one action as two rows ("Fed" and "Watered", same instant); ⓑ two waterings on one day would render as two rows carrying the *identical* "Mon 10 Aug · 3 days ago" label — indistinguishable on screen, and it would read as a rendering bug rather than as history. Grouping by local day collapses both, and it is the grain the whole section already thinks in (`plantDaysSince`, `plantDueIn`, `plantAgoLabel` are all whole-local-day). The row keeps the **newest** moment of its day as its `ts`, and a day where you both watered and fed reads "💧🌱 Watered + fed". A feed with no paired watering (imported data, or a pre-v409 log) still appears, as feed-only. **`plantCareEvents(p)` is a pure read — it never writes to a plant**, same contract as the v396 derived-prep fallback, and the stored logs keep their full per-timestamp detail for anything that wants it later. **Expand state is `_plantHistoryOpenId`, keyed to the plant id rather than a bare boolean** — deliberately, because `_plantOpenId` is assigned at 13 separate sites and a boolean would have needed a reset at every one of them; keying on the id means opening a different plant collapses on its own with zero extra touch points (pinned by a test). Corrupt/zero log entries are skipped rather than rendering "Invalid Date". **No data-model, sync, export or import changes** — `waterLog`/`feedLog` are intentionally never in a `plant-v1` file (see the Plant data model rule) and that is unchanged; no new scalar, so `plantExportObj`/`plantApplyImport` are untouched. **No test-build seed change needed** — the v407 seed already gives its 2 demo plants care logs either side of their watering interval, so the new card has content on the PR link. **Tests: `tests/cases/22-plants.js` EXTENDED, 40 → 63 assertions, additive only — no existing assertion changed.** The grouping rules (paired feed+water → one row, two same-day waterings → one row keeping the newest moment, water-then-feed-later → one row marked both, feed-only, newest-first ordering, corrupt entries skipped, the pure-read pin), then the rendered detail view end to end: the card appearing, exactly 5 rows by default, "Show all (8)", the combined row's wording, expand → 8 rows, "Show less", collapse back, a different plant NOT inheriting the expanded state, and a brand-new plant showing no empty box. **Verified non-vacuous, and precisely: keying the grouping on the raw timestamp instead of the day fails exactly the two same-day-collapse assertions** and nothing else (the paired feed/water case still passes there, correctly — identical timestamps collapse either way, so that assertion pins the cross-log merge, not the day grouping). **43/43 files green.** *(Ran on Opus, which was more than this needed — a well-defined read-side render change; plain Sonnet would have been fine from this entry, and the one judgement call is now recorded above.)*
- **v415:** **New Train programme: "Full Body Flow (Revised)" — a side-by-side copy of Full Body Flow rebuilt around a clinical review Cathal supplied 10/08/2026 (lumbar stenosis + a 4 cm hiatal hernia).** Deliberately a **third flow, not an edit of flow 0** — the original stays exactly as it is so the two can be run against each other before anything is retired; that call is Cathal's to make after testing. 28 poses, ~20 min (1205s), added as `YOGA_FLOWS_TRAIN[2]` with a new card in `renderTrainPrograms` (`type:"yoga", key:2`), placed directly under the original. **Substitutions from the review:** Dead Bug → **Alternating Heel Slides** (the old cue said press the back "firmly" into the floor, which invites bracing and breath-holding — bad with a hiatal hernia; heel slides give the same trunk control with an exhale-on-the-slide cue); double Knees-to-Chest → **Single Knee to Chest** left/right, held **behind the thigh** so the thigh never compresses the abdomen; Cat-Cow → **Cat to Neutral** (round on the exhale, return only to a flat back, never down into Cow — lumbar extension narrows the space the review is trying to protect); **Sciatic Nerve Floss removed entirely** — it is covered more fully in the Nerve & Hip programme and was condition-specific filler in a general flow; Bridge's 55s hold → **Low Dynamic Bridge**, 6–8 controlled reps, arms flat, no interlacing underneath. **Additions filling gaps the review named:** **Quadruped Scapular Press** (shoulder-girdle work without adding a plank), **Mountain with Calf Raises** (ankle/lower-leg), and **Supported Tree** left/right with fingertips on a wall or chair — balance was the clearest missing component. **Cue changes throughout:** shorter stance + tailbone tuck + hands-on-hips option in both Low Lunges and both Warrior Is; Bird Dog's leg capped at hip height with an exhale-on-extension cue; Child's Pose propped on a bolster with a tabletop rock-back alternative if folding brings on reflux; Reclined Butterfly and Savasana both offer a wedge under the whole upper torso. **New `YOGA_TRAIN_NOTES` array, index-aligned with `YOGA_FLOWS_TRAIN`** (empty string = no note), rendered on the session intro screen in the same amber warning box `renderPhysioSession` already uses for `prog.warning` — it carries the 2–3-hours-after-eating rule, the stop-signs (regurgitation, chest/upper-abdominal pressure, symptoms travelling further down a leg), the red flags needing urgent assessment (saddle numbness, bladder/bowel change, foot drop, progressive weakness), and the honest framing that this is mobility and core work, not a complete fitness programme — it belongs alongside the strength days and walking. Escaped through `esc()`, unlike the physio `warning` which is interpolated raw. **Second, unrelated half — a correction to v414's History buttons, folded in here at Cathal's direction rather than held for its own version.** v414 grew **both** `.hist-add` and `.hist-del` from 32px to 40px and added no accessible names. That was not the confirmed design: the Opus design session earlier the same day took the call via AskUserQuestion and Cathal chose **grow the `+` only, leave the `✕` at 32px** — growing both was explicitly on the rejected list, because it makes an unconfirmed delete that writes a synced tombstone exactly as easy to hit as the safe, frequent add. **The reason it shipped wrong is the process, not the build session: the design PR (#166) recording that decision was never merged**, so `main`'s pending note still read only "the safe version is growing the buttons themselves to ~40px" — ambiguous about *which* buttons — and the build session had no way to know. **This is exactly the failure mode the design-here/build-on-Sonnet handoff exists to prevent, and it failed because the notes commit sat unmerged behind a later PR.** Worth remembering: a design PR is not optional bookkeeping, it is the input to the next session. v415 restores it — `.hist-del` back to `32px`/`font-size:14px` with a load-bearing CSS comment saying why the two sizes deliberately differ, `.hist-add` left at 40px, and `.hist-add.in-list` keeps its own 18px override (that variant belongs to the button that stayed big). **Plus the `aria-label`s v414 also missed**, the v413 `.ppc` shape: `.hist-add` announced as just "plus, button", so it now names the item and reflects state ("Add <name> to your list" / "<name> is already in your list") and `.hist-del` gets a name alongside its existing `title`, both `esc()`'d in `renderHistory` (~9341) exactly as the surrounding string-building does. Zero behaviour change, no `role`/`tabindex` — both are already real `<button>`s. **PR #166 was closed as obsolete rather than merged**: `main` had already ticked off ①/②/③, so its only remaining value was the rationale, which is now folded into the pending entry above. **No data-model, sync, export or storage changes** — flows are static constants and the workout-history record keys on `YOGA_TRAIN_NAMES[idx]`, which now resolves for index 2 automatically. **No new test file** — this is static content plus one array lookup in a renderer; `node tests/run.js` re-run as-is, **43/43 files green**, no existing test touched. *(Ran on Opus per the standing model-check rule — safety-adjacent content. The build itself was well-defined; a plain-Sonnet session could have done it from this entry.)*
- **v414:** **Pending item ③ — the History `+`/`✕` buttons (`.hist-add`/`.hist-del`) grown from 32px to 40px, with `font-size` scaled proportionally (22→28 for the `+`, 14→18 for the in-list ✓ state and the `✕`).** The 5th-external-review triage (10/08/2026) had judged the tap-target complaint real but its proposed fix — an invisible ±9px halo copied from `.chk::before` — unsafe here specifically: `.hist-add`/`.hist-del` sit side by side with only a 10px gap inside a row that is itself tappable, so a symmetric halo on `.hist-del` would overlap its neighbour and the row body, making an unconfirmed delete (which writes a synced tombstone) easier to hit by accident. **Confirmed with Cathal via AskUserQuestion before building: grow the buttons themselves instead, no halo.** CSS-only, three lines (index.html ~96–98), no JS/behaviour change. `node tests/run.js` re-run as-is, no new test needed. **43/43 files green.**
- **v413:** **Pending item ② — `.ppc` per-person packing circles get `role="button"`, `aria-pressed` (read from `item.checks` at render time, since `renderList()` rebuilds this markup from scratch on every change) and `aria-label="Packed for <name>"` using the household's real Cathal/Petra names.** No behaviour or visual change — accessibility metadata only.
- **v412:** **Pending item ① — deleted the dead `insertBlock`/`wrapCode`/`updateFmtButtons` functions** (pre-v318 notes-editor remnants, zero call sites — the live formatting toolbar is the `.fmt-btn.ne-fmt`/`data-fmt` handler set). Pure deletion, ~30 lines, no behaviour change.
- **v411:** **Fix: a device could go silently RECEIVE-ONLY for a whole session — merging everything arriving from the other phone and pushing nothing back, with no banner and no toast (from a code review Cathal supplied 10/08/2026; its item 1.3 flagged the guard, and the real exposure turned out to be worse than the review described).** `pushPersonal()` bails while `_personalFetchOk` is false — a good guard, so we can never overwrite remote data we haven't seen — but **exactly one place set it: `fetchPersonal()`'s success path.** Two independent ways that stuck: ① **a network blip on the first fetch, then a healthy stream.** `startSync()` calls `fetchPersonal(cb)`; the `.catch` runs `cb` so `startPersonalListening()`/`startSSEFallback()` still start, but the incoming SSE `put` only ever called `applyPersonal` — it never set the flag — and **`startSSEFallback` re-fetches only when the stream is STALE**, so a *healthy* stream means `fetchPersonal` is never called again. The flag stayed false until the app was fully reloaded. ② **a brand-new account, and this one is permanent.** Firebase REST returns literal `null` for a path that doesn't exist yet, and the 401/403 branch signalled itself with a bare `null` too — so an empty remote took the auth-failure exit. **Found while building, not from the review: that exit doesn't call the callback either**, so a fresh account never ran `startPersonalListening()`/`startSSEFallback()` and left `_homeAwaitingFetch` true — no sync in *either* direction, ever, and every reload reads `null` again and repeats it. (Pinned by the non-vacuous check, which reported `cbCalled:false, timedOut:true` on the old code.) **Fix, both halves of one guard:** ⓐ `handlePersonalEvent` sets `_personalFetchOk = true` on any `put` — Firebase sends the whole node as the first put on every connect, so a put *is* a successful read and satisfies exactly what the guard protects against. Deliberately set **before** the `p.data && typeof p.data === "object"` check, since a read that found an empty remote is still a read; deliberately **not** set on `patch`, which is a partial update and already delegates to `fetchPersonal`. ⓑ the 401/403 branch returns a new private `_FETCH_AUTH_FAIL = {}` sentinel and the second `.then` compares against that instead of `null`, so an empty remote is treated as the success it is while **the auth guard is untouched** — a 401/403 still refuses to open the guard, which is the entire reason it exists. No data-model, export, or sync-protocol changes: this only widens *when* pushing is unblocked, never what gets merged or sent. **Tests: new `tests/cases/40-personal-push-guard.js` (14 assertions), no existing test file touched.** As in v390 this can't be driven through `pushPersonal()` itself (its first guard is `if (_isTestBuild) return;` and every `file://` test page is a test build), so the assertions pin the flag transition that gates it, with `fetchWithTimeout`/`applyPersonal`/`isLoggedIn` stubbed and restored: the reported scenario end to end (failed fetch → guard closed → a put opens it), put-with-data, put-on-an-empty-remote, `patch` NOT opening it but still re-fetching, a malformed event body, then the negatives that matter — 401, 403 and a dead network all still refusing — plus the empty-remote and populated fetches, and a pin that the sentinel is a private object no remote payload can be mistaken for. **Verified non-vacuous, and precisely:** reverting both halves fails exactly the 5 assertions that pin the fix while every auth/negative assertion still passes. **43/43 files green** (40 case files + the 3 SW cases). *(Ran on Opus, correctly per the standing model-check rule — this is sync logic, and the empty-remote half was invisible in the review and only surfaced from reading the callback flow.)*
- **v410:** **Fix: the weekly meal-plan startup banner's "Plan it" tap chained straight into a confirm-dialog offering to add the filled week to groceries — easy to tap through without registering what it was agreeing to (Cathal 10/08/2026: suspected recipes were being added to groceries without him specifically tapping "add to groceries").** **Traced first, not assumed:** every place that can push a meal-plan recipe onto groceries — `addWeekToGroceries` (the Week Plan screen's green button, `mpAddWeekBtn`), a single recipe's own "add to groceries" flow (`addRecipeToGroceries`), and the banner's confirm-dialog callback — requires an explicit tap; `setMealPlanDay`, the day picker, servings +/-, "Mark cooked" and "Swap recipe" touch nothing grocery-related. **The real gap was the banner's OWN flow**: tapping "Plan it" ran `runMealPlanFill()` and immediately chained a generic `confirmDialog("Add this week's planned dinners to your grocery list?", …)` with no preview of which meals — a two-tap sequence (Plan it → OK) that reads as one action and is easy to confirm on reflex. **Design choice confirmed via AskUserQuestion** (of three options — name the meals in the dialog; land on the Week Plan screen and use the existing button; both): **land on the Week Plan screen.** The banner's click handler (`index.html` ~10917, the IIFE right after `maybeShowMealPlanBanner`) no longer calls `confirmDialog`/`addWeekToGroceries` at all — after `runMealPlanFill()` it sets `_recipeScrollPos`, sets `_recipeView = "plan"`, and calls `switchSection("recipes")`, which itself calls `renderRecipes()` and dispatches to `renderMealPlanner()` (the existing `_recipeView === "plan"` branch, same path the Recipes list's own "📅 Week plan" button uses). The filled days are now visible before any grocery action, and adding to groceries stays the pre-existing deliberate tap on `mpAddWeekBtn`. No changes to `runMealPlanFill`, `addWeekToGroceries`, `setMealPlanDay`, or the day picker/sheet. **No new test file** — this is DOM click-handler wiring (which screen a tap navigates to), not new business logic; verified by the existing `tests/cases/15-mealplan-fill-rotation.js` (Fill's own behaviour, untouched) plus a full manual code trace of every grocery-adding call site, and the hearth-verifier's edits-landed/`node --check`/file-integrity/version-bump checks. **52/52 files green.** Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v409:** **Plants: logging a feed now also logs a watering at the same time, since feeding requires watering (Cathal's ask 09/08/2026).** One-directional — watering alone still never logs a feed. `plantLogCare(id, kind)` shares one `Date.now()` timestamp between the two logs when `kind==="feed"`, pushing onto both `feedLog` and `waterLog` (each still capped at `PLANT_LOG_CAP`). `plantUndoCare` mirrors it: undoing a feed entry also removes the paired water entry, but only when it's still the newest `waterLog` entry with the exact matching timestamp — a manual watering logged afterward is never discarded. `tests/cases/22-plants.js` covers the pairing, the one-way direction, undo-cleans-the-pair, and (via direct storage manipulation, not a second `plantLogCare` call, to dodge same-millisecond timestamp collisions in synchronous test runs) that undo-feed leaves a later manual watering untouched.
- **v408:** **Fix: the banner's demo-data reset only half worked (Cathal 09/08/2026, testing v407 on the PR link: "resetting doesn't restore all data (coffee remained deleted) and the status didn't change (newly done remained done)"). v407 was never merged — this is the same PR/branch, so there is no shipped v407 in the wild.** **Root cause: `resetTestData` wiped storage and then called `importBackupData` on the LIVE page.** `loadListData` returns the in-memory `listData` whenever it is populated and never re-reads storage, so the merge ran against the copies the page was already holding — and `importBackupData` is **additive BY ID**, so every seeded item whose id was already in memory was filtered out as "already here", the edited copy survived, and `storeSet(LIST_CONFIG[lt].key, …)` wrote that edited copy straight back. **The asymmetry is what made it confusing rather than obviously broken: DELETIONS came back** (a deleted id isn't in memory, so the seed's copy counted as genuinely new) **while anything edited IN PLACE — a tick, a rename, a due date, a category — did not.** Reproduced before touching anything, with a throwaway harness probe over four scenarios: history-entry delete → restored; item delete → restored; recipe/plant/watchlist delete → restored; **tick everything done → `done=8` before the reset, `done=8` after it.** A half-restored reset is arguably worse than none, because you can't tell which half you're looking at. **Fix: `resetTestData` no longer seeds at all — it wipes and reloads.** `wipeTestStore` takes `fl4_testseed` with everything else (it is `fl4_`-prefixed), so the reload lands in phase 1 with no marker, wipes again into a genuinely fresh parse, and phase 2 seeds into empty memory — **exactly the same path as the once-per-version auto-seed, which was correct all along and is why the first open of a version always looked right.** One code path, one behaviour. The `fl4_secVisible` write that `resetTestData` used to do itself is dropped too: phase 1 already does it, and doing it in both places was the same duplicate-orchestration smell that hid the bug. **The version bump is load-bearing here, not routine:** `fl4_testseed` in Cathal's browser already held `v407 · 09/08/2026`, so shipping this fix as v407 would have left his test link finding a current marker and skipping the reseed entirely — he'd have seen the same stale data and concluded the fix didn't work. **Tests: new `tests/cases/39-test-build-reset.js` (11 assertions), no existing test file touched.** It can't call `resetTestData()` directly — that's behind `_isTestBuild` (false on `file://`, pinned by case 38) and ends in `location.reload()` — so it exercises the two **orderings** the reset chooses between, which is where the bug actually lived: seed-into-live-page vs seed-as-fresh-load. Includes a deliberate **regression pin** asserting that the live-page ordering still fails to undo a tick (if someone "simplifies" `resetTestData` back to seeding in place, that pin is the tripwire, and its failure message says to re-check rather than delete it), the matching assertion that the fresh-load ordering does undo it, the deletions-came-back asymmetry that made the report read as "not all data", a full round trip (tick + rename + hand-added item + deleted item + deleted recipe/plant/watchlist → every count back to baseline), and a pin that `wipeTestStore` clears the seed marker — without which the reload finds it current and the reset silently does nothing. **42/42 files green.** **Honest note: one run during this change failed `34-recipe-id-uniqueness` and `35-duplicate-recipes` with `ReferenceError: … is not defined`** — both run BEFORE the new file and neither touches test-build code, so it is the known harness/CDP timing flake from the v400 entry, not this change; four consecutive clean runs afterwards. Flagged rather than swallowed in case it recurs on CI. *(Ran on Opus. The diagnosis genuinely wanted it — the bug is invisible in the diff and only shows up if you know `loadListData` caches — but the fix itself is four lines.)*
- **v407:** **Every PR test link now opens with resettable demo data, and every PR body now carries a pre-merge checklist (Cathal 09/08/2026: "add resetable test data to every test link in every pr so I don't use imported real data. also add as default in every pr, simple tests for me to do before each merge").** **The problem was an origin, not a feature gap.** Every "👉 Try this version" link is served from **raw.githack.com — ONE origin for every branch and every version ever opened there** — so localStorage is shared across the whole history of PR test links. The v324 note that test data "lives (and stays) on the test address only" was true and also misleading: it stays there *across PRs*. The documented way to get realistic content (export a real backup, import it on the test page) therefore left real household data sitting on the test origin, and it was still there in the next PR's link. **Fix: a test build reseeds itself ONCE PER VERSION.** `fl4_testseed` holds the version string last seeded; on the first open of a given version the whole `fl4_*` store is wiped and a fixed demo household is laid down, and a reload of the SAME version does nothing (a refresh mid-test doesn't destroy what you were doing). **Four design calls confirmed via AskUserQuestion before building:** ① fresh every version, not once-ever and not button-only — the whole point is that a new PR starts from an identical known state; ② demo content for **every** section, so a PR touching trips/baby/plants has something to poke at; ③ the orange banner doubles as the manual reset (tap → confirm → reload); ④ the checklist is a fixed smoke block **plus** change-specific steps. **Two-phase, and the split is load-bearing: the WIPE runs at the top of the script (right under `_isTestBuild`), before any module-level code reads storage**, so every parse-time cache — `sectionVisible`, `syncPrefs`, `hearthUser`, `listData` — initialises exactly as it would on a fresh device; **the SEED runs just above `var bootSection`**, once `importBackupData` and its helpers exist. Doing both late would leave the caches holding the pre-wipe values; doing both early is impossible, the helpers aren't defined yet. `fl4_secVisible` is written in phase 1 (all eight hideable sections on, including the opt-in Train/Family log/Plants/Sports) precisely because it is read into a var at parse time. **The seed goes in through `importBackupData()` — the SAME merge the real backup restore uses — not a pile of hand-written `storeSet` calls**, so the demo household can never drift into a shape the app doesn't already read, and a field added to `buildExportPayload` becomes seedable for free. `syncPrefs` is deliberately NOT in the seed: `importBackupData` doesn't apply it and it's a parse-time var anyway, so a value written there would sit in storage doing nothing. **Ids come off `_tsId()` (fixed base, and the counter is reset at the top of every `buildTestSeed()` call) and dates off `_tsDay`/`_tsAt`/`_tsTs` (relative to today).** The counter reset is not tidiness — **the first draft didn't have it and the test caught it**: `importBackupData` is additive by id, so a second seed with fresh ids landed as a *second copy of everything* (8 recipes, 2 trips, 16 grocery items) instead of being a no-op. Relative dates are what stop the demo going stale — the trip always starts next week, doses are logged this morning, the meal plan covers the days ahead. **Content:** four lists with history deep enough for the suggestion chips, list + global + work notes, 4 recipes (ingredients built by calling `parseIngredients` on plain text rather than hand-writing ingredient objects), a 3-day meal plan pointing at those recipes, a Lisbon trip with all five booking types and every field the v292/v293 export map carries, baby growth/doses/milestones/an opened bottle, 2 plants with care sections and logs either side of their watering interval, 3 watchlist entries, workouts + bodyweight + BP, family log, tracked meds, food log, saved meal, and the `{name, lines}` grocery-recipe chip. Names are obvious placeholders ("Demo Chicken Traybake"). **The demo doses are sample UI data, never a dosing reference** — the v291 advisories compute against them, which is the point of having them. **Nothing here can run on the real app:** both entry points are behind `_isTestBuild`, which is false on `coshea321.github.io`. **Second half — the checklist.** New `.github/pull_request_template.md` is now the canonical PR shape; `CLAUDE.md` step 5 and `/release` step 7 both say to fill it in section for section, because **the GitHub API does not apply a template automatically** — a PR opened by Claude only gets it if it is reproduced by hand. The **"Always check" block is five boxes copied verbatim on every PR** (app opens, orange banner, demo data present, no red bug badge, banner reset works) — identical every time on purpose, since that is what catches a change that quietly breaks boot or an unrelated section — plus a **"Check for this change"** block written per version: 2–4 steps naming the section, the tap and the expected result, in non-coder language, with an explicit instruction to say "this one is invisible in the UI" rather than invent a tap that proves nothing. **Tests: new `tests/cases/38-test-build-seed.js` (24 assertions), no existing test file touched.** A `file://` page is a *preview*, not a test build, so `_isTestBuild === false` there and the guard can be tested for real — `resetTestData()` must leave a planted "real data" item alone and must not write the seed marker. Then the seed itself: unique ids across the whole household, the meal plan starting today, the trip in the future, and every section asserted by count through the real import path (8 grocery items, 8 history entries, 4/2/5 on the other lists, 4 recipes with 7 parsed ingredients on the traybake, every planned meal pointing at a recipe that exists, 5 bookings with boarding/gate/seats intact, 3/2/3 baby records, 2 plants with logs, 3 watchlist, 2/3/2 train records, 2/2/3 trackers, notes on both tabs, the `{name, lines}` chip shape, the calorie goal) and finally the idempotence check that caught the id bug. **41/41 files green.** **Honest note: the auto-seed path itself is not covered end to end** — it only fires when `_isTestBuild` is true, which no test phase produces (the file:// phase is a preview, and the SW phase maps the *production* hostname deliberately). What is covered is everything the auto-seed calls; the trigger is three lines of version comparison. *(Ran on Opus. The demo-data half genuinely wanted it — the phase-1/phase-2 split and the parse-time cache reasoning are exactly the kind of thing that goes quietly wrong — but the checklist half would have been fine on plain Sonnet.)*
- **v406:** **Fix: bringing an item back from the Done section never fed the list's history (Cathal 09/08/2026: "when I re-add something from the Done section it doesn't get added as an entry in the history"). Small and well-defined — this would have been fine on plain Sonnet.** **The gap was that v342's "a revive is a re-purchase" rule was only ever applied to ONE of the five ways an item can come back.** History is written exclusively by `addToHist` / `addToHistFor`, and `addItemToCurrent` (typing a name that matches something sitting in Done) is the only revive path that called it. Everything else flipped `done` to false and saved: **`toggleItem` (untapping the tick in the Done section — the one Cathal hit), the home quick-pick, the recipe "add ingredients" pass and the pasted-list import.** The last three are the clearest tell that this was an oversight rather than a decision — in each of those functions the *new-item* branch calls `addToHistFor`/`addToHist` and the revive branch two lines above it doesn't. Consequence: the item's History entry kept its old count and old `dates` series (so the v303 replenishment clock never reset for anything you habitually tick and untick), and an entry deleted from the History tab stayed deleted, because `clearHistTomb` only runs from inside `addToHist`. **Scope was confirmed via AskUserQuestion before building, and two paths were deliberately left OUT:** ① **Reopen all / Unpack all** — one tap would bump the count and stamp today's date on every done item at once, which would wreck the replenishment signal wholesale; ② **`togglePersonCheck`** (a travel item comes back to unpacked when one person unticks their circle) — that reads as correcting a tick, not as needing the thing again. **New guard, `reviveWasMisclick(it)` + `REVIVE_HIST_GRACE_MS = 60000`:** unlike the typed re-add, an untick is one tap away from a tick, so ticking something by mistake and immediately untapping it would otherwise inflate "Added Nx" by one every time. Items already stamp `updated` when ticked done, so the guard is a pure read of that with no new state — **and every call site must read it BEFORE `updated` is re-stamped**, which is why each of the four sites captures a local flag on the line above rather than checking inline. Only the count is at stake either way: `histRecordDate` already dedupes the date series per day. **Two ordering details that are load-bearing:** ⓐ in `toggleItem` the `addToHist` call sits at the very END of the loop body, after the v361 repeat block, and reads a `_wasRevived` flag captured before anything mutates — a repeating task's tick also sets `done` back to false, and a naive "done is false now" check would log every recurring completion as a re-purchase (pinned by a test); ⓑ the recipe and paste paths pass **`existing.name`, not the parsed `clean` name**, and run after `catId` settles — grocery matching is fuzzy (`groceryNameKey`), so a pasted "eggs" can land on an item stored as "Egg", and history/`capHistToLiveLimit`/the History tab's in-list check all compare on the exact stored name. **Tests: new `tests/cases/37-revive-history.js` (18 assertions), no existing test file touched.** The guard as a pure function incl. null-safety; the untick revive bumping count, refreshing `lastUsed`, appending today to `dates`, **creating an entry that didn't exist at all** (a synced-in or pruned item), and resurrecting a tombstoned entry; then the three negatives — ticking done, the repeat reschedule, and a tick undone inside the grace window; plus the v342 typed path still returning `"restored"` and still feeding history. **Verified non-vacuous, and the first draft was not:** referencing the new helper directly threw a `ReferenceError` on the old code and skipped every behaviour assertion, so section 1 now calls through a stub — on the pre-fix build it fails cleanly at 2a/2b/2c/2e/2g (count stuck at 3, dates stuck at `["2026-01-01"]`, the missing entry still `undefined`) while the section-3 negatives pass, which is the right shape: the pins are the fix, the negatives are guardrails. **40/40 files green.**
- **v405:** **Cook-along ticks — tap any ingredient or step in a recipe and it greys out; re-entering the recipe clears them (Cathal 07/08/2026: "is it possible to click on any part of a recipe so that it gets greyed out after it has been completed, but everything gets reset when you reenter the recipe?").** **Two design calls confirmed via AskUserQuestion before building:** ① **everything you *do* is tappable** — ingredient lines, Prep steps and Method steps — while the title, Serves, Notes and Source stay inert, because they're reference rather than tasks; ② **leaving the Recipes tab and coming back KEEPS the ticks** — only tapping "← All recipes" (or opening a different recipe) clears them, so glancing at the calendar mid-cook doesn't lose your place. **The state is deliberately display-only: `_recipeDone` / `_recipeDoneId` are in-memory module globals, never written to a recipe and never synced.** This is "where am I in tonight's cook", not a property of the recipe — persisting it would mean a partner opening the same recipe sees half of it struck through, and it would need a sync field, a merge rule and export/import handling for something that is over when dinner is. It also means a full app reload clears the ticks, which is the right trade and is stated in the on-screen hint. **Keys are positional (`i3` from the ingredient's index in `r.ingredients`, `p0`/`m2` from document order within the Prep/Method block) precisely so a re-render repaints the same ticks** — a servings change, a favourite toggle and an incoming sync push all rebuild `renderRecipeDetail` from scratch, and losing the ticks on any of those would make the feature useless mid-cook (all three pinned by tests). **Three implementation details that are load-bearing and must not be "tidied":** ⓐ **the delegated click listener is wired ONCE behind `_recipeTickWired`** — `#recipesContent` is a permanent element whose innerHTML is replaced, so a per-render `addEventListener` stacks a listener on every servings tap and a click then toggles N times, which reads as "the tick doesn't work" on even counts (this was written wrong first, caught before committing, and is now pinned by a test that re-renders three times and taps); it follows the existing `_tidyInputWired` / `_grocPickWired` pattern. ⓑ **`wireRecipeTicks` marks up the Prep/Method blocks AFTER innerHTML, rather than changing `formatMethod` / `derivedPrepHtml`** — those are shared string builders and marking them up in place would leak cook-along classes into anything else that ever renders a method. ⓒ **the tap toggles the class in place instead of re-rendering**, so the page never moves under your hands. `formatMethod` closes and reopens its `<ol>` around a sub-heading, so the `"ol > li, p"` selector walks document order across BOTH lists and the keys run on rather than restarting (pinned — restarting would grey a step above the one you tapped). Ingredient section headers, method sub-headings and the derived-prep footnote are all excluded: they aren't things you do. Styling is the one bit that had to go in the stylesheet rather than inline — the detail view sets `color` inline on both the `<li>` and its inner `<span>`, so `.rcp-done` needs `!important` to win; ingredients get a strikethrough (short, label-like, reads like the grocery list), method steps only fade (a struck-through sentence is hard to re-read). **Tests: new `tests/cases/36-recipe-cook-ticks.js` (29 assertions), no existing test file touched** — what is and isn't tappable, tap/un-tap, the wired-once check, nothing written to the stored recipe (`updated` unchanged, no `done`/`checked` on ingredients), ticks surviving a servings change / a full `renderRecipes()` / a tab switch, re-entry clearing them, a different recipe not inheriting them, plus the awkward shapes: a prose-only method (one `<p>`, no `<ol>`), a derived prep list and its footnote, the two-`<ol>` sub-heading case, and a recipe with no prep or method at all. **39/39 files green.** *(Note for calibration: this ran on Opus but was a well-defined change — plain Sonnet would have been fine.)*
- **v404:** **The block parser stops losing a recipe to one stray character (Cathal 07/08/2026: "the code is now copyable but gave an error when pasting into the app", with the paste attached).** **Reproduced before touching anything, and the diagnosis was worse than the report:** his paste began **`.=== 1782917881957`** — a single stray dot before the marker — and under v402's strict `^\s*={2,}\s*(\d+)\s*$` that line failed to match, so it AND the four prep lines beneath it were swallowed as "preamble before the first block". The Salmon recipe **disappeared with no message at all**; a trace on his text gave 2 blocks where there should have been 3. **Silently losing a recipe is far worse than refusing the paste**, so this fixes both halves independently: ① **the marker is now deliberately forgiving** — `PREP_BLOCK_MARK = /^[^A-Za-z0-9]*={2,}[^0-9\n]*(\d+)/` accepts anything non-alphanumeric around it, so a stray dot, a list dash, markdown bold (`**=== 123**`), a heading (`### === 123`), a quote marker, a missing space (`===123`) and a trailing recipe name (`=== 123 Salmon`) all parse. Checked against real prep text for false positives — "Preheat the oven to 200C", "Cut into 1 cm cubes", "Simmer 20-30 minutes", `Slice into 1/8" slices` and even "Mix 2 == 2 maybe" all correctly do NOT match, because a step would have to *begin* with punctuation and then `==`. ② **belt and braces: `PREP_BLOCK_HINT` catches any line that mentions `===` near a number but didn't become a block, and `parsePrepReply` returns an error QUOTING that line** rather than skipping it — so the silent-drop failure mode cannot recur even if the marker misses something new. `parsePrepBlocks` now returns `{entries, missed}` rather than a bare array; the routing test uses the same HINT so a dotted-marker reply still reaches the block parser instead of falling through to JSON. **Verified against Cathal's real paste verbatim in the harness** (temporary case file, deleted after): all **8 recipes** now come through, the stray-dot recipe keeps all four of its steps, the `⅛"` inch mark survives, and nothing is reported unmatched. **Tests: `tests/cases/33-recipe-prep-roundtrip.js` extended, 96 → 106 assertions, additive only.** The stray-dot case and its step count; bold/dashed/heading/named/tight markers; ordinary prep steps NOT being mistaken for markers; and the missed-line error including the offending text. **Verified non-vacuous, and informatively so:** restoring the strict marker fails six assertions, and the stray-dot case then produces *the new error message* instead of silence — which proves the two halves of the fix work independently of each other. **38/38 files green.** **Honest note recorded at the time: from his paste I would have expected the app to import 7 of 8 rather than show an error, so what he saw may have had another cause; the silent-drop defect was real and reproducible either way, and the fix also converts any remaining unreadable-marker case into a message that names the line.**
- **v403:** **Ask for the prep reply inside a code fence again (Cathal 07/08/2026: "a new sonnet chat creates a wall of text that is not easily copyable on mobile"). Prompt text only — a self-inflicted regression from v402, and a small, well-defined change that would have been fine on plain Sonnet.** v402 fixed JSON's escaping problem but its prompt said **"no code fence"**, and that one phrase threw away the thing that actually makes a reply copyable on a phone: **a fenced block renders in a chat with a one-tap Copy button; loose prose has to be hand-selected.** JSON at least usually arrived fenced, so v402 made copying *worse* than what it replaced even while fixing the parse failures. Fix: the prompt now opens with "Put your WHOLE reply inside a single code block (three backticks before and after), so it can be copied in one tap on a phone", and a new format rule closes the obvious loophole — "the code block is the whole reply… do not split it into several code blocks." **No parser change was needed and none was made**: `parsePrepBlocks` already ignores an opening fence (it lands before the first `=== id`, so the `if (!cur) return;` preamble skip drops it) and filters closing fences with its `/^\`\`\`/` check, and `parsePrepReply`'s block-marker test uses the `m` flag so it still routes correctly when the marker sits inside a fence. **Tests: `tests/cases/33-recipe-prep-roundtrip.js` extended, 90 → 96 assertions, additive only.** A fully fenced reply parsing; fence markers not leaking into the prep text; a language-tagged fence (```text); a fenced reply with prose either side; plus three prompt-wording assertions (asks for a code block, no longer forbids one, states the single-block rule). Verified non-vacuous: restoring the "no code fence" wording fails exactly the two prompt assertions. **38/38 files green.** **Lesson worth keeping: when the destination is a chat window, the reply format has two separate requirements — parseable (v402: no escaping) AND copyable (v403: fenced). Optimising one at the cost of the other is not a fix.**
- **v402:** **Prep-helper replies move OFF JSON to a quote-free "=== id" block format (Cathal 07/08/2026: "the json that a new chat comes back with quite often doesn't work first time and is not easily copyable", with the failing reply attached).** **Root cause found in his paste in seconds and confirmed by running it through a parser:** the reply contained `"prep":"...slice thinly into ⅛" slices on a mandolin..."` — **an unescaped inch mark, which closes the JSON string** and rejects the entire paste (`Expecting ',' delimiter`). This is not a rare slip: inches, `9x5"` tins and quoted names are ordinary in recipe text, so **asking a model to remember JSON escaping mid-recipe is a losing battle** — the format was changed rather than another rule added to the prompt. **New format: `=== <id>` on its own line, then one prep step per line, blank line between blocks.** No quoting, no escaping, nothing to get wrong; `"`, `'` and `\` are all just text. It also answers the second half of his complaint — one enormous single-line JSON blob is horrible to select on a phone, a line-based block is not. **And it degrades gracefully where JSON could not: a reply cut off part-way still imports every COMPLETE block before the cut** instead of failing outright, which matters given the truncation history in v398/v399 (pinned by a test). **`parsePrepReply` routes on the marker** — a paste containing a `=== <digits>` line goes to the new `parsePrepBlocks`, everything else falls through to **the JSON path, kept unchanged**, so a reply already in flight in a chat window is never stranded. The book-matching half was extracted into **`matchPrepEntries(list)`** so both formats share one implementation (id first, name fallback, one row per recipe, unmatched reported) — behaviour-preserving for the JSON path. `RECIPE_PREP_FILE_TAG` still exists and still gates that fallback; it is simply no longer mentioned in the prompt. Tolerances in the block parser: prose before the first block is ignored, a stray ``` fence is dropped, bullet glyphs are stripped from steps (reusing `METHOD_BULLET_MARK`), and a block with no steps under it yields no row rather than empty prep. **Tests: `tests/cases/33-recipe-prep-roundtrip.js` edited, 74 → 90 assertions.** Additive except for **one pre-existing v397 assertion that was changed**: it checked the prompt contained the JSON tag, which is no longer true now the prompt asks for blocks — it now checks the prompt shows the block format instead. New coverage uses **Cathal's actual failing case** (`1/8" slices`) plus quotes/apostrophes/backslashes surviving, the truncated-reply case, the tolerances, JSON still being accepted, a block reply not being second-guessed by the JSON path, and the three prompt-wording checks. Verified non-vacuous: disabling the block route fails the file outright. **38/38 files green.** *(Test-writing note: the backslash assertion is deliberately `indexOf`, not a regex — escaping a literal backslash through two layers of source is exactly the class of mistake this change exists to stop making, and the first draft got it wrong.)*
- **v401:** **Duplicate-recipe finder and repair (Cathal 06/08/2026: "I would like to solve the duplicate issue"; v400 fixed the cause, this clears what the old id generator already created).** **Three design calls confirmed via AskUserQuestion first:** ① group by **same id AND same name** (not one or the other); ② unwanted copies are **deleted with tombstones**, like the Delete button, not local-only; ③ **suggest the fullest copy, he confirms**. **Two group kinds, deliberately distinct on screen because they're different problems:** `"id"` — records sharing an id, the v400 importer bug, where only the first was ever reachable by `find()`/`findIndex()` so the twins couldn't be opened, favourited, deleted or given prep; `"name"` — same name, different ids, two genuinely working records you probably only want one of. **A record belongs to exactly ONE group** — anything already in an id group is not also offered as a name duplicate, or resolving one group would silently change another (pinned by a test with a book that is both). **`recipeRichness(r)`** scores what a record actually carries (ingredient count, prep, notes, URL, category, fav, rotation, plus bonuses for v366-canonical bracketed ingredients and hand-set v368 grocery prefs) and only ever **suggests** a keeper — the fullest copy is usually the one that's been worked on, which is exactly the asymmetry in Cathal's book where one copy was tidied and the twins were raw. **Entry point is a self-hiding banner on the recipe list, not a fifth toolbar button** — this is a one-off repair, not a tool you reach for, and it disappears the moment the book is clean (the v362 "New week — plan dinners?" shape); it counts extra COPIES, not groups, and is suppressed in select mode. **Two things in `applyDupRepair` are load-bearing and must not be "simplified":** ⓐ it operates on **`_dupState.book`, the snapshot the choices were reviewed against, NOT a fresh `getRecipeBook()`** — that function re-parses from storage on every call and hands back new objects, so identity comparisons against the reviewed records all miss and nothing is ever deleted (this was a real bug during the build, caught by the tests); a **length-mismatch guard** aborts with a toast if the stored book changed mid-review rather than deleting by stale index. ⓑ **a tombstone is written only for an id that is genuinely leaving the book** — in an id group the keeper SHARES its id with the copies being dropped, so an unconditional `addTomb` would delete the keeper on the next sync. Both pinned, and both verified non-vacuous (always-tombstoning fails exactly the "does not tombstone the id it keeps" assertion; disabling the richness suggestion fails exactly the preselect and kept-copy assertions). **Tests: new `tests/cases/35-duplicate-recipes.js` (37 assertions), no existing test file touched** — the finder against a clean book, junk entries, id pairs, an id triple (Cathal's real Lentil Bolognese case), name pairs, case/space folding, the one-group-per-record rule, group ordering, blank names; the richness ordering and its null guard; then end to end — the banner appearing and counting copies, the fullest copy preselected, the id-group explanation, nothing written before Apply, the right copy kept, an unrelated recipe untouched, the id-group tombstone rule, the name-group tombstone rule in both directions, overriding the suggestion by tapping, the compare view, `<img src=x onerror=…>` in a name rendering as text, the banner disappearing once clean, and the empty state. **38/38 files green.**
- **v396:** **Recipe prep section — an optional `prep` field, plus a derived fallback so the whole existing recipe book shows one without a migration (Cathal's ask 06/08/2026: "add a preparation section within or before the method that describes the prep work before the cooking begins — how difficult would it be to add to all existing recipes?"; designed and built on Opus, the fuzzy-design trigger being the back-catalogue half of the question rather than the field itself).** The field on its own was the easy part (the v393 `url` shape exactly); **the question actually asked was about the back catalogue**, and three options were put to Cathal: leave old recipes blank, a Tidy-style pass that guesses which opening method steps are prep, or derive a prep list from the ingredient lines. He picked **field + auto-seeded prep**, and **Prep before Method as its own section** (both via AskUserQuestion). **The derived reader is the load-bearing part.** Recipes already say most of their prep inside the ingredient lines — the canonical v366 shape puts it in the bracket ("Garlic (minced)"), untidied ones use a comma clause ("Onion, finely chopped") or a " - " clause. `derivedPrepSteps(r)` reads all three: brackets are split on commas and taken directly (`splitGroceryName` deliberately never looks inside one — see its own comment at rule ⓐ), and `splitGroceryName(...).prep` supplies the other two shapes. **Every candidate fragment must contain a known prep word to survive**, which is what stops the same brackets' MEASURES being listed as prep — "(425 g, rinsed)" yields "rinsed" and drops "425 g", and "(or 30 g margarine)" yields nothing. **`RECIPE_PREP_WORDS` is deliberately a SEPARATE, wider list from `GROCERY_PREP_WORDS`, not an extension of it** — that list drives what gets stripped out of a *shopping* name, so widening it to get better display wording would have quietly changed what lands on the grocery list, a far bigger blast radius than this feature earns (pinned by a test asserting `marinated` is in one list and not the other). **The fallback stays a fallback: nothing derived is ever written to a recipe by rendering it** (same contract as the v365 rescue-at-render). A typed `prep` replaces the derived list rather than stacking with it, and the derived block carries a quiet "Read from your ingredient list. Edit the recipe to write your own." footnote so it never reads as something Cathal typed and forgot. **The editor does NOT pre-fill the textarea** — that would have written the derived text into the record for real the next time he saved anything at all; instead a "↓ Use the prep from my ingredients (N)" button appears only when there is something to derive, and it **appends rather than overwrites** anything already typed. Typed prep renders through the existing `formatMethod`, so it numbers exactly like the method (v394) with no new rendering code. **No sync, export or import changes** — `prep` is a new scalar riding inside the existing whole-record newest-wins `fl4_recipebook` contract, same as `url` (v393) and `inRotation` before it; the CSV importer has no prep column to map. `recipeSearchText` gained `r.prep`, so both recipe search and global search (v387) see it. **Tests: new `tests/cases/32-recipe-prep.js` (39 assertions), no existing test file touched.** Covers the reader against all three storage shapes, measures/`or`-alternatives/section-headers producing no row, multiple notes in one bracket joining, recipe order, mid-word non-matching ("uncut", "biscuit"), null/empty inputs, the two-list separation; then the detail view end to end — a pre-v396 recipe showing a derived Prep section, Prep rendering before Method, the stored record staying untouched by rendering, a typed prep replacing the derived list, no heading at all when there is nothing to show, and prep text containing `<img src=x onerror=…>` rendering as text; then the editor — the field not being pre-filled, the button appearing only when there is something to derive, appending instead of clobbering, and `prep` saving on both the new and existing paths. Verified non-vacuous twice, each failing exactly its own assertions: making `isPrepPhrase` always return true fails exactly the five measure-rejection assertions (with "425 g" and "or 30 g margarine" appearing as prep); disabling the derived fallback fails exactly the four derived-view assertions plus the ordering probe. Restored, re-confirmed **35/35 files green**. One test-probe gotcha worth remembering: `innerText` applies `text-transform:uppercase` in Chrome, so a heading assertion has to use `textContent` or it searches for "Prep" in "PREP".
- **v395:** **Fix: ingredient amounts not recognised at all in a recipe pasted from an AI chat (Cathal's report 03/08/2026, screenshot of a freshly-added "Spinach rice" recipe — no amount was bold on any ingredient and the serving-size stepper did nothing; build-ready for plain Sonnet, no design call needed).** Diagnosed by first reproducing on the exact ingredient text he'd typed ("50 g olive oil" etc. parses fine in isolation), then asking him to copy/paste the actual pasted text back — **every single line began with a U+200B zero-width space**, invisible on screen but sitting directly in front of the leading digit, confirmed by char-code inspection (`charCodeAt(0) === 0x200b` on all 15 lines). `parseIngredients()`'s amount regex requires a line to **START** with a digit, so this blocked recognition identically on every line regardless of the actual number or unit — and the render-time rescue (`rescueIngredientMeasure`, added in v365 for exactly this "amount stranded in the name" class of bug) couldn't recover it either, because it re-parses `ing.name`, which still carried the same leading U+200B. Root cause: text copied out of an AI chat interface (this was an AI-translated Greek recipe) is a source `CLAUDE.md`'s existing ingest-noise handling (bullet glyphs, checkboxes, HTML tags) never anticipated. **Fix:** one line added at the top of `parseIngredients()`'s per-line loop — `line = line.replace(/[\u200b\u200c\u200d\u2060\ufeff\xad\u200e\u200f]/g, "")` — stripping zero-width space/joiner/word-joiner, BOM, soft hyphen and the two directional marks from **anywhere in the line**, not just the front, since one could just as easily land between a number and its unit. No data-model, sync, or export changes — pure text-cleaning inside the existing parser, same class as the bullet-glyph stripping right below it. **Tests:** `tests/cases/17-recipe-parser-compound-range.js` EXTENDED (30 → 35 assertions, no new file — this is squarely "does `parseIngredients` recognise the amount", the same question that file already answers for compound/range shapes) — a leading ZWSP no longer blocking recognition, a zero-width char landing mid-line between the number and its unit, multiple stacked invisible chars (ZWSP+BOM), a non-quantified line ("Salt, to taste") still correctly getting no amount after stripping, and a whole multi-line pasted block parsing every quantified line correctly. **Written as `\u`-escapes in the test source, never literal invisible bytes** — deliberately, both for readability/diffability and because a first draft using literal invisible characters directly in the test file failed with a CDP `SyntaxError` the identical content passed as `\u` escapes did not; the exact mechanism wasn't run to ground (V8-via-Node accepted the literal bytes fine via `new Function()`, only the CDP `Runtime.evaluate` path used by the test harness rejected them) but escapes are the correct form for invisible characters in source either way, so the safer form was kept without needing the root cause. Verified against Cathal's real pasted text directly: all ten quantified ingredients (50g olive oil, 200g rice, 1 liter water, 500g spinach, etc.) now parse amount+unit correctly; the three non-quantified lines ("Salt, to taste", "Black pepper, to taste", "Juice and zest of 2 lemons...") correctly still get no amount, unchanged from before. `node tests/run.js`: **34/34 files green**. Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v394:** **Recipe ingredients render as bullets, the method as a numbered list (Cathal's ask 03/08/2026: "can we auto format the recipe ingredients to use bullet points and the directions to use numbered points?"; small and well-defined — would have been fine on plain Sonnet).** **Display-only, in `renderRecipeDetail` — the stored recipe text is never rewritten**, so opening the editor still shows exactly what was typed or pasted, and nothing syncs as a result of looking at a recipe. **Ingredients:** the `<ul>` already existed but every `<li>` carried `list-style:none`, so there were no bullets — now `list-style:disc` with the list indented 20px; the `<li>` takes the accent colour (#B5651D) so the bullet is warm and an inner `<span>` puts the text back to the reading colour. Section headers (`isHeaderIng`) keep `list-style:none` and get `margin-left:-20px` to stay flush left, so "For the sauce" doesn't sit in the bullet column. **Method:** `formatMethod` used to emit `<p>`/`<br>` paragraphs — it now emits a real `<ol>` and the numbers come from the list, not the text, so a typed "1." can never double up as "1. 1.". Split out into a new **`methodSteps(text)`** helper returning `{type:"step"|"head", text}`, with **two splitting modes** because a pasted method is either explicitly numbered or it isn't: with 2+ marked lines a marked line starts a step and every unmarked line after it is a **continuation** (a hard-wrapped paste stays one step instead of exploding into fakes); unnumbered, one line = one step unless the line reads as a wrap (starts lower-case AND the previous step didn't end a sentence). **`METHOD_STEP_MARK` is deliberately narrow** — 1–2 digits and a `.`/`)`/`:` that must be followed by a space — so "180C. Bake…", "2 - 3 minutes", "1.5 kg" and "1/2 cup" are never mistaken for markers and stripped (pinned by a test). Bullet glyphs in a pasted method are dropped. A short line ending in ":" ("For the sauce:") renders as a sub-heading, not a step, and the `<ol>` **reopens with `start=N+1`** after it so numbering continues instead of restarting at 1. A single unbroken block of prose stays a plain paragraph — a lone "1." in front of it is just noise. The v250 run-together split ("…cool. 2. Whisk…") is preserved. Escaping via `esc()` is unchanged. **Tests: `tests/cases/31-recipe-step-list.js` ADDED (23 assertions) — a new file, no existing test modified.** Covers both splitting modes, the continuation rules, marker stripping, the numbers-not-eaten cases, sub-headings and `start=` continuation, escaping, the single-paragraph case, and an end-to-end detail-view render checking computed `list-style-type` on ingredient vs header rows plus the stored method text being untouched. **34/34 files green.** Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v393:** **Recipe URL — a place to save a link to the original recipe (Cathal's ask 03/08/2026: "add a place to add urls for each recipe"; build-ready for plain Sonnet, no design call needed).** New optional `url` field on recipe records. **Editor:** a "Recipe URL (optional)" text input added after Notes in `openRecipeEditor`, saved on both the new-recipe and existing-recipe paths. **Detail view:** a "Source" block renders after Notes when `r.url` is set — a clickable 🔗 link, `target='_blank' rel='noopener noreferrer'`. **New helper `normalizeRecipeUrl(u)`:** trims, and prepends `https://` when the typed value doesn't already start with `http://`/`https://` — this is a safety guard as much as a convenience: without it, a value like `javascript:alert(1)` typed into the field would land in an `<a href>` unescaped (`esc()` only escapes HTML metacharacters, not the URL scheme) and execute on tap; forcing the string through the `https://` prefix neutralises that class of input while also letting Cathal type a bare `example.com/recipe` without the scheme. No data-model change beyond the one new field — recipes have no dedicated JSON import/export (only the CSV importer, unchanged; it already folds a "source" column into notes text, which was left as-is since a dedicated field for manually-added recipes doesn't affect that separate CSV path), so no other sync/export site needed updating. `getRecipeBook()`'s existing null-guard (v385) and the whole-record newest-wins recipebook sync contract already carry a new scalar field through untouched (same class as the `inRotation` field added earlier — no special-casing needed). **Tests:** none added — this is a pure UI/data-field addition with no merge, dosing, or parsing logic of its own; `node tests/run.js` re-run as-is, **33/33 files green** (no regression). Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v392:** **Watchlist: a "Watching" group and an optional season (Cathal's ask 03/08/2026, minutes after v391's PR opened: "let's add a watching section as well and add an optional ability to add what season we're on, episode not needed").** Shipped as a **second commit on v391's branch/PR #153, deliberately breaking the one-PR-per-version rule** — v391 was still unmerged, and v392 edits v391's own code, which exists nowhere else. A fresh branch off `main` would not have contained the code being changed. PR #153 was retitled and its body rewritten to cover both versions; the merge-state hazard the rule guards against was handled by re-checking the PR was still open immediately before pushing. **"Watching section" read as a third group on the Watch screen, not a new nav section** — stated in the reply rather than asked, since a whole nav entry for "things in progress" would be an odd shape and the surrounding words ("as well", alongside the season field) point at the list. **The one genuinely load-bearing decision: three states are stored as TWO BOOLEANS (`watching` + the existing `watched`), NOT one `status` string.** v391 is already in Cathal's hands via the test link and may reach a phone before v392 does, so the store must stay readable by a v391 build: it knows `watched`, would read `status:"watching"` as *not watched* for the entire list, and would push that back. An unknown `watching` field it simply ignores, and `mergeWatchData`'s v296 field-fill carries it through untouched. `watched` always wins and `watching` only means anything while `watched` is false — an invariant `watchSetStatus` enforces on every write, pinned by a test. `watchStatusOf(w)` is the single interpreter; nothing else reads the raw booleans. **`watchToggleWatched` was deleted, not left alongside the new functions** (the codebase's duplicate-definition history makes a superseded twin a real hazard) — replaced by `watchSetStatus(id, status)`, `watchCycleStatus(id)` and the `WATCH_CYCLE` table. **The tick control now cycles in the order things actually happen** — not started ○ → watching ▶ → finished ✓ → not started — and the expanded row *also* gets an explicit three-button status picker, so the cycle is a shortcut for people who know it rather than the only route. Groups render **Watching first** (most-recently-touched first), then To watch, then the collapsed Watched group; "what are we in the middle of" is the question this screen gets asked most. **Season is optional, TV-only on screen, and clamped 1–99** (`watchSeasonOf`, 0 = unset, junk reads 0 not NaN) — episodes deliberately not built, exactly as asked. The editor's season row is **hidden rather than removed** when the kind is Film, and a season left behind by switching a show to a film **stays in the record while not rendering** — never destroy something he typed; both halves are pinned by tests. Leaving `watched` still preserves the ★ rating (the v391 rule, now applying to a three-way move rather than a toggle). No sync-protocol, export-key or store changes — `watching` and `season` ride inside the existing `fl4_watchlist` records, so all four sync paths and the backup needed no edit at all. **Tests: `tests/cases/30-watchlist.js` MODIFIED, 49 → 68 assertions — a deliberate test change, declared in the PR body per policy, and necessary because `watchToggleWatched` (which three assertions drove) no longer exists.** New coverage: a v391-shaped record with no `watching` field still reading correctly (the mixed-version case), the watched-wins invariant both ways, the full three-step cycle including `watchedAt` being stamped on arrival at watched and cleared on leaving, an unrecognised status being refused rather than corrupting the record, the -1 guard extended to the two new mutators, `watchSeasonOf` clamping 0/-1/100/"abc"/null to 0, the Watching group rendering *above* To watch, the season chip showing on a TV entry, a film's stale season being invisible **but still stored**, and the expanded row offering all three statuses. Verified non-vacuous twice: making `WATCH_CYCLE` skip "watching" fails the cycle assertions with the entry jumping straight to watched; removing the `meta.key === "tv"` gate fails exactly one assertion, with "Season 5" appearing on the film. Restored, re-confirmed **33/33 files green**. Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v391:** **Watchlist — a new 🍿 section for TV shows and films to watch, household-shared (Cathal's ask 03/08/2026: "a place to record a list of tv shows and movies to watch, also optionally link to free source of info for each"; designed and built on Opus, correctly — he opened with "this is not fully thought out", which is exactly the fuzzy-design trigger in the standing model-check rule).** **Three design calls confirmed via AskUserQuestion before any code was written:** ① **its own hideable section**, not a 5th list type — the four list types are hard-coded in **13 separate sync/backup/export sites** (scout-verified) and a watchlist carries fields (`kind`, `rating`, `watchedAt`) none of them have, so the list route would have meant editing 13 working sync paths for a fun feature; the section route is more new code but it is *all* new code, touching nothing that already works. ② **Auto info-links plus an optional pasted link** (he picked both over either alone). ③ **Fields = title + TV/Film + watched tick + free-text note + ★ rating**; "who wants to watch it" was offered and **not** picked — deliberately left out, logged in Pending below rather than silently built. **Data model:** `fl4_watchlist`, household-shared, whole-record newest-wins by `updated` with `fl4_tomb_watchlist` tombstones — a straight copy of the Plants (v377) contract, because one title is one edit unit and needs no nested merge. Records are `{id, title, kind:"film"|"tv", note, link, watched, watchedAt, rating, addedBy, added, updated}`. `mergeWatchData` carries the v296 field-fill (an older build's copy can't wipe a field it never heard of) and drops nulls on **both** sides — `mergePlantsData` returns nulls untouched, a latent gap this copy deliberately closes rather than inheriting. **The info links are the interesting part and are deliberately not an integration:** three chips per entry — JustWatch (where it's streaming, `/ie/`), Wikipedia, IMDb — **built from the title at render time via `encodeURIComponent`, never stored and never fetched.** No API key, no third-party code in the page, nothing that can go stale, and zero network traffic until he actually taps one. They're *search* URLs, not deep links, precisely because resolving a title to a real id would need exactly the API dependency this avoids (the same reasoning that rejected reverse-geocoding in v274 and nutrition APIs in v317). The optional pasted `link` reuses the list item sheet's own normalisation verbatim — bare domains get `https://`, anything that isn't http(s)/mailto/tel is refused with a toast. **UI:** rows with a tick circle (tap = watched) and a tappable body that expands in place to show the info chips, any pasted link, the ★ rating (watched entries only) and Edit/Remove; watched entries drop into a **collapsed "Watched · N" group**, the Done-group idiom he already knows from Lists. Kind chips (All / 🎬 Films / 📺 TV) appear only when both kinds are actually present, and a filter whose kind vanishes (retagged, removed, or dropped by a sync from the other phone) falls back to All rather than showing an unexplained empty list — the v379 pattern and the v383 empty-state lesson applied on the first try. Un-ticking **keeps** an existing rating rather than clearing it: a mis-tap must not silently destroy something he typed. **Nav default is ON, deliberately and against the recent precedent** (sports/train/famlog/plants all default off) — this is a feature he asked for, and a section he'd have to go hunting in Settings to switch on isn't delivered; the bar goes to 7 icons, which is already a supported state (all 9 buttons can be enabled today), and one tap in Settings turns it off. **Wiring:** the standard 6 hideable-section touch points, plus watchlist blocks in all four sync paths (`pushPersonal`/`applyPersonal`/`pushHousehold`/`applyHousehold`), a `_rDirty.watchlist` flag with a `!_watchEditing` guard in `flushSyncRenders` (an incoming sync must not redraw a half-typed entry away), a `closeTopOverlay` branch so the phone back button offers "Discard changes?" from the editor (v345 pattern), `watchlist` in `buildExportPayload` + an additive restore block in `importBackupData`, and the export coverage comment updated. **Deliberately NOT built:** no Home card (Home is already long, and unlike Plants this section has a permanent nav slot), no global-search group, no poster art or any image (photos are the one thing that threatens the localStorage budget — v377's standing constraint), no watched-date display beyond the sort order. **Tests: new `tests/cases/30-watchlist.js` (49 assertions), no existing test file touched** — the merge contract in both directions incl. field-fill, cleared-fields, nulls on either side and the tombstone/re-add pair; `watchInfoLinks` encoding (incl. a `Tom & Jerry?` title that must not break out of the query string) and the no-title case; `watchRatingOf` clamping junk to 0 rather than NaN; `getWatchlist`'s source-level null guard; the tick stamping `watchedAt`+`updated`; same-star-clears-rating; out-of-range ratings refused; **un-ticking preserving the rating**; the -1 guard (toggle/rate/delete against a missing id is a no-op, not a crash); delete writing a tombstone; export coverage incl. the rating riding along; and the render itself — watched group collapsed by default and revealed on open, info links absent from collapsed rows but present (with `rel=noopener`) when expanded, chips hidden when only one kind exists, a vanished filter falling back to All, the empty state, and **a title containing `<img src=x onerror=…>` rendering as text rather than an element**. Verified non-vacuous twice, each failing exactly its own assertion and nothing else: reverting `getWatchlist`'s null filter fails only "getWatchlist drops null entries"; reverting the `esc()` on the title fails only the markup assertion, with `img count: 1`. Restored, re-confirmed **33/33 files green**. Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v390:** **Fix: `applyPersonal` never paused outgoing sync while merging, unlike `applyHousehold` (Cathal's ask 03/08/2026, sixth and last item of the bug queue; build-ready for plain Sonnet — the design call was already made and deliberately deferred at v375, see that changelog entry).** `applyHousehold` has set `syncPaused = true` on entry (before any merge work, cleared in `finally`) since ~v375. `applyPersonal` only ever cleared `syncPaused` in its `finally` — it never set it true on entry — a real inconsistency v375 flagged and explicitly declined to fold into that release ("changing WHEN outgoing sync is paused alters sync timing... wants its own version to be testable in isolation"). The gap: `pushPersonal()` bails early whenever `syncPaused` is true, so a push that fired *while* `applyPersonal` was still mid-merge (rather than strictly before or after the whole cycle) was never held back for it — exactly the race `applyHousehold` has been immune to for several versions. **Fix:** one line — `syncPaused = true;` added at the top of `applyPersonal`, right after its guard clause, mirroring `applyHousehold`'s entry exactly. The existing `finally { syncPaused = false; }` (added in v375, alongside the whole-function try/catch) needed no change — it already resets the flag unconditionally, throw or no throw. No data-model, export, or sync-protocol changes; this only narrows *when* outgoing pushes are held back, never what gets merged. **Tests:** new `tests/cases/29-sync-paused-parity.js` (5 assertions). Couldn't be pinned through `pushPersonal()` itself — its very first guard is `if (_isTestBuild) return;`, and every `file://` test page is a test build, so the push path never reaches the `syncPaused` check inside this harness. Assertions instead pin the actual state transition the fix makes, by hooking `mergeBabyData` (a function genuinely invoked partway through the merge) to capture `syncPaused`'s value at that exact moment: `applyPersonal` now reads `syncPaused === true` while a merge block is actually running and `false` again once the cycle completes; a throw mid-merge (simulated by making the hooked function throw) still clears it via the existing `finally` — the v375 guarantee, preserved under the new earlier `true`; and a direct side-by-side confirms `applyPersonal` and `applyHousehold` now produce identical during/after shapes. Verified non-vacuous: reverting the one-line fix and re-running fails exactly the two assertions expecting `duringMerge === true` on `applyPersonal`, with `applyHousehold`'s own during-merge reading unaffected (proving the test isolates the right function); restored, re-confirmed **32/32 files green**. Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT. **This closes the bug queue Cathal opened 03/08/2026** (① recipe null-crash → v385, ② recipe-search state bugs → v386, ③ recipes-in-global-search → v387, ④ normaliseNotes null guard → v388, ⑤ sw.js cache staleness → v389, ⑥ this item → v390) — nothing outstanding from that list remains.

- **v441:** **the daily calorie goal stops resetting to 2000 (27/08/2026).** Cathal's report, diagnosed on v440 and confirmed here: `fl4_cal_goal` and `fl4_profile` are a bare number and a bare object, so the personal-sync pull took the incoming value unconditionally — and every push sends `storeGet("fl4_cal_goal") || 2000`, so a device that had never set a goal pushed the **fallback 2000** and stomped the real goal on the next pull. Each value now carries its own stamp (`fl4_cal_goal_ts` / `fl4_profile_ts`), written at every local write site, carried through the push payload and the backup file, and compared on merge: strictly newer wins, and **an unstamped value counts as 0, so it can never overwrite a stamped one** — a device still on the old build can no longer stomp the goal. A device with nothing stored still adopts whatever arrives, so a fresh phone picks the goal up. Local-newer schedules a converge push, as `recipebook` does. **Do NOT write the goal with a bare `storeSet("fl4_cal_goal", …)`** — it leaves the stamp stale and the value silently loses the next merge; go through `setCalGoal()`. **Tests: `50-cal-goal-sync.js` ADDED** (23 assertions) with the tripwire — unstamped incoming 2000 must not overwrite a stamped 2400. 50/50 + 4 SW-phase green. [PR #199](https://github.com/coshea321/miniature-journey/pull/199)
- **v440:** **fix Track → Food date header always reading "Today" (26/08/2026).** `foodDateStr(date)` computed its own "today" as `var today = foodDate` — the same global the function is always called with — so `date === today` was trivially true on every call and the header never showed anything but "Today", even though the entries below correctly followed the prev/next day arrows. Fixed by computing the real calendar date inline instead of aliasing the global. Small, well-defined fix; no model-check or design confirm needed per CLAUDE.md.
- **v400:** **Two prevention fixes: the CI flake, and the recipe-id collision that created Cathal's duplicate recipes (07/08/2026).** ① **CI had been red on 3 of 4 runs on PR #157 and it was never the code.** The logs showed every mechanical check green (`node --check OK`, byte size, version bump v395→v399) and then `Test runner crashed: Error: Chrome did not expose a page target within 10s` — the suite never ran at all. `tests/harness.js` polled for a CDP page target for 40×250ms; a cold GitHub runner routinely needs longer.
