Raised to 60s (`LAUNCH_TIMEOUT_MS`, with the poll interval named rather than implied) and the error now includes the Chrome binary path. **A slow start is not a test failure**; a genuinely broken Chrome still fails, just 60s later. **Test-infrastructure change, declared in the PR body per policy.** ② **`recipesFromCSV`'s id generator was `Date.now() + rowIndex + Math.floor(Math.random()*1000)`, and `Date.now()` is CONSTANT across the import loop** — so N recipes were drawn from a span of ~1,100 values. Simulated at Cathal's book size: **99.9% chance of at least one collision, 6.4 colliding records expected for 118 recipes**, which is exactly what his exported book contained (Lentil Bolognese ×3 on one id, Homemade Beef Jerky ×2, Vegetarian Chili, Dilled Chickpea Burger, Iced Coffee). **This is not cosmetic: every reader keys on the id via `find()`/`findIndex()`, so only the FIRST record with a given id is reachable** — the twin cannot be opened, favourited, deleted or given prep — **and neither `applyPersonal` nor `applyHousehold` dedupes by id** (both `.map()` over the local book and keep every entry), so a duplicate propagates to the other phone and persists forever. Replaced with **`nextRecipeId(taken)` + `recipeIdMap(rb)`**: monotonic within a page session, checks the ids already in use, and **reserves each id it hands out** so a bulk import cannot collide with itself or with the existing book. Values stay in the ms-timestamp range, so nothing that sorts or compares ids changes behaviour. Applied at both recipe sites (`recipesFromCSV`, the editor's new-recipe path). **The Trips editor's identical-looking `Date.now() + random(0..99999)` at ~line 4689 was deliberately left alone** — it is a single creation per tap, not a loop, so the birthday maths doesn't apply; changing it would widen a Recipes fix into Trips for no benefit. **Tests: new `tests/cases/34-recipe-id-uniqueness.js` (12 assertions), no existing test file touched** — 500 ids in a tight loop all distinct, monotonic, still in timestamp range; `recipeIdMap` ignoring junk entries; a pre-taken id being skipped and the returned id being reserved; a 150-row import producing no duplicates; a second import not reusing an id already stored; five editor-created recipes distinct; plus two assertions documenting the failure mode itself (with a duplicate id, `find()` reaches only the first record while the second sits unreachable in the store). Verified non-vacuous by restoring the old formula: the 150-row import drops to **142 distinct ids of 150** — 8 collisions, matching the simulation — and the later-import assertion fails too. Restored, **37/37 files green**. NOTE: v400 fixes the CAUSE only. The duplicates already in Cathal's book need the repair tool (v401).
- **v399:** **Prep-helper batching by CHARACTERS, not by recipe count (Cathal 06/08/2026: "it cuts after I'm not sure how many recipes", pasting back the export text, which was itself cut off mid-recipe).** Fourth commit on PR #157, same deliberate one-PR deviation. **Two diagnoses from what he pasted, and the first one corrected my own v398 work.** ① **He was still on v397** — provable from the paste itself: the rules had no GROUP BY line and the header read `--- MY RECIPES (118) ---` rather than `BATCH n OF m`, so v398's batching had never reached his phone. ② **But v398's batch size would not have fixed it anyway.** `PREP_BATCH_SIZE = 20` was sized against a synthetic test book averaging ~374 characters per recipe; his real book is nothing like that — a three-line "Chicken Soya" sits beside "Gluten Free White Bread" (a ~4,000-character method) and "Thirst Quencher", whose stored method is an entire scraped Healthline article. Twenty of the short ones is a trivial paste; twenty of the long ones is bigger than the whole synthetic book was. **Count was simply the wrong unit.** Fix: `PREP_BATCH_CHARS = 12000` and a new **`prepBatchRanges(rb)`** that packs recipes into contiguous batches until the budget is hit, returning `[{from, to, blocks}]`; `prepBatchCount(rb)` now takes the **book, not a number** (call sites updated), and the header reads "BATCH 2 OF 9 (recipes 15-23 of 118)" so a paste is self-describing even when batch sizes differ. **A recipe whose own block exceeds the entire budget gets a batch to itself rather than being dropped** — a recipe you cannot copy at all is worse than one long paste — and the recipe after it still exports (both pinned). **Tests: `tests/cases/33-recipe-prep-roundtrip.js` edited, 69 → 74 assertions.** Unlike v398's purely additive extension, this one **rewrote** the v398 batching assertions, because they asserted the old count contract (`prepBatchCount(118) === 6`, fixed slice ids) which no longer exists; the equivalent coverage is preserved in the new form (slicing, headers, clamping, chips, copy-button label, apply-advances) with new assertions for budget compliance, contiguity, no-recipe-dropped, differing batch sizes, and the oversized-recipe case. **Two fixture lessons worth keeping:** a first attempt used long recipes every 5th, and a second used a 10-long repeating pattern — **both packed into a perfectly even [20,20,20]**, because a periodic size pattern divides into the budget and comes out even by arithmetic, making the "batches differ in size" assertion vacuous. The fixture is now deliberately **lopsided** (first ten recipes huge, the rest one-liners). Separately, the "applying advances to the next batch" assertion was clicking the **last** chip, which correctly has nowhere to advance to — it now starts from the first. Verified non-vacuous: removing the budget check collapses everything into one batch and fails the file outright. **36/36 files green.**
  - **Data finding, NOT fixed here, flagged to Cathal (06/08/2026):** his exported book shows **duplicate recipe records sharing the same `id`** — Lentil Bolognese ×3 all as `1782249126200`, Homemade Beef Jerky ×2 as `1782249125527`, plus Vegetarian Chili, Dilled Chickpea Burger and Iced Coffee. The copies differ in content: one has v366/v367-tidied ingredients, the twins are raw (one still carries `▢` scrape glyphs), which points at a sync/merge artifact rather than the CSV importer (that dedupes by name). **This breaks the prep helper specifically:** `parsePrepReply` matches with `rb.find()` and `applyPrepImport` with `findIndex`, so only the FIRST record with a given id can ever receive prep — the twins are unreachable no matter how many times he runs it. Almost certainly affects other id-keyed paths too (opening a recipe, fav, delete). Needs its own investigation and a design confirm before any dedupe tool is built — do not silently merge or delete a duplicate.
- **v398:** **Prep helper, second pass — batching, a revised prompt, and the fix for the re-run dead end (Cathal's report 06/08/2026 after using v397 on his real 118-recipe book: "I updated the prompt based on grouping work done in another chat… also it only copied around 19 out of 118 recipes. And when I tried to update a recipe's prep after it had previously been updated it didn't work").** Third commit on PR #157, same deliberate one-PR deviation as v397 (it edits v397's own code). **Both bugs reproduced headlessly before fixing, per the v386 discipline, and the first one was NOT what the report literally said.** ① **"only copied 19 of 118" was not a copy failure — it was the REPLY being truncated.** Measured in the harness: a 118-recipe book is only ~44k characters going out (about 374 per recipe), which any chat accepts; but a prep entry per recipe asks for roughly 8-10k tokens of *output*, and the answer simply stops partway. 19 is exactly the shape of that. **Fix: batch the export at `PREP_BATCH_SIZE = 20`** — `prepExportText(batch)` now takes a 0-based batch and slices, `prepBatchCount()` derives how many, the copy screen grows numbered chips (only when there is more than one batch — a small book must not sprout a control that says "1 of 1"), the copy button names the batch, the payload header says "BATCH 3 OF 6" so a paste into a fresh chat is self-describing, and an out-of-range batch clamps rather than yielding an empty copy. **`applyPrepImport` now returns to the copy step with the next batch already selected** instead of exiting to the recipe list — working through six batches would otherwise mean re-opening the helper and re-finding your place six times. Single-batch books are unaffected and still exit as before, which is why v397's existing "closes back to the list" assertion still passes untouched. ② **The re-run dead end.** On a second pass every recipe already has prep, so the v397 pre-tick rule (`accept = !existing`, deliberately chosen by Cathal so nothing he wrote is replaced by default) left **zero** rows ticked, the Apply button disabled, and its label reading "Apply to 0 recipes" — a screen that looks broken rather than one that is waiting for input. Confirmed exactly in the repro (`preticked=0 applyDisabled=true`). **The pre-tick rule was kept — it's the right default and he chose it** — and the *explanation* fixed instead: a card appears when any row would replace something, saying how many and why they start unticked, with **Tick all / Untick all** buttons, and the disabled Apply now reads "Tick the ones you want". The card is absent entirely on a first run where nothing would be replaced. ③ **Prompt replaced with Cathal's revised wording** (developed in another chat): the big addition is **GROUP BY WHAT GOES IN TOGETHER** — a step is one moment in the method, not one ingredient, so ingredients that hit the pan together become one line — plus an explicit "include the spices and the garnish", and a stronger no-preamble instruction ("no explanation, no note, no apology, even if something in the recipes is unclear"). The regrouping of loose one-ingredient lines now counts as a licensed improvement to prep that already exists. No data-model, sync or export-payload changes; `applyPrepImport` still writes only `prep` + `updated`. **Tests: `tests/cases/33-recipe-prep-roundtrip.js` EXTENDED, 43 → 69 assertions — a deliberate change to an existing file, declared in the PR body per policy, and additive only (no v397 assertion was weakened or removed).** New coverage: the three new prompt rules present; a single-batch book getting no batch header; a 118-recipe book splitting into 6; each batch holding only its own slice; the batch header; out-of-range clamping both directions; a batch being a fraction of the whole payload; the chips rendering, switching, and the copy button naming the batch; applying a batch keeping you in the helper and advancing; then the second pass — pre-tick still zero, the disabled button's new wording, the explanation text, tick-all enabling Apply, untick-all clearing it, the replacement actually landing, and the card being absent when nothing would be replaced. Verified non-vacuous twice: making the export ignore the slice fails exactly the two slice assertions; removing the tick-all card fails the file outright on the missing control. Restored, re-confirmed **36/36 files green**.
- **v397:** **Prep helper — copy the whole recipe book out, have an AI write the prep, paste it back, review it, apply (Cathal's ask 06/08/2026, immediately after v396: "add an export function so I can export all recipes, then have you generate the prep content, I review it and then I import back only the prep field").** **Shipped as a second commit on v396's branch/PR #157, deliberately breaking the one-PR-per-version rule** — the same call as v392, and for the same reason: v396 was still unmerged and v397 edits the `prep` field v396 introduced, which exists nowhere else, so a fresh branch off `main` would not have contained the code being changed. PR #157 was confirmed still open immediately before pushing, and retitled/rewritten to cover both versions. **Two design calls confirmed via AskUserQuestion before any code:** ① export via **copy-to-clipboard**, not a downloaded file — the payload's destination is a chat window on a phone, where a file means a download, a file manager and an attachment step; ② a recipe that already has prep is **shown side by side with the proposal and left unticked**, rather than skipped outright, so improving a half-filled one is possible but replacing his words is always a deliberate tap. **The load-bearing design decision is that this is PREP-ONLY, and it should stay that way.** Recipes still have no JSON export (only the CSV importer), and a full `recipe-v1` round trip would have put every field an import could damage — `groceryName`/`noBuy` shopping preferences, `fav`, `lastCooked`, `inRotation`, ingredients themselves — inside the blast radius of a bad paste. This reads a lot and writes exactly one field: `applyPrepImport` sets `prep` and `updated` and touches nothing else, pinned by an assertion comparing ten other fields before and after (and verified non-vacuous by making it also write `notes`). **Export** (`prepExportText`) puts the instructions AND the recipes on the clipboard as one blob, so there's one thing to paste: per recipe an `id` (matching is on it), name, serves, ingredients via `ingredientToLine` with section headers kept as headings, the method, and **any prep already written** — so whatever writes the reply can see what it would be replacing. The prompt constrains it hard: prep only (not the cooking, which is already in the method), one step per line, no numbering or bullets (the app numbers them, v394), at most 6 lines, work only from what the recipe says, `""` when a recipe genuinely needs none. **The paste side is built for real chat output, not for a clean paste:** a ``` fence is stripped, prose either side is tolerated by taking the outermost `{...}`, and — the v395 lesson applied preemptively rather than after an incident — **invisible characters are stripped before `JSON.parse` and again from each prep string**, since a U+200B inside a JSON string breaks the parse outright rather than just degrading it. Matching is by `id`, falling back to a case-insensitive exact name match if the id was mangled in the round trip; anything unmatched is **reported on the review screen, never silently dropped**; a duplicate id yields one row, first wins. **Review-first throughout** (the v366 Tidy / v378 plant-import idiom): tick per recipe, edit any proposal inline, existing prep shown above the proposed one with a "replaces what you wrote" flag, `confirmDialog` naming how many would be replaced, and nothing written until Apply. Inline edits live in `_prepHelperState.edits` rather than being read off the DOM at apply time, because the screen re-renders on every tick and would otherwise throw away typing (pinned by a test). `_recipeEditing = true` while the screen is open, the same guard the editor and tidy screen use, so a sync landing mid-review can't redraw it away. `applyPrepImport` guards `findIndex` against `-1` for a recipe deleted on another device mid-review, and a row emptied inline means "leave this one alone" rather than blanking the field. **Deliberately NOT built:** no undo snapshot (unlike v366's tidy, which rewrote ingredients wholesale — this writes one field, and the recipe that would lose something is never pre-ticked); no file download; no import of anything but prep; `prepCopyToClipboard` is kept **separate from `plantCopyPrompt` rather than refactoring that into a shared helper** — widening a working Plants function inside a Recipes version buys nothing, and the duplication is noted here instead. No sync, backup or export-payload changes: `prep` already rides inside the existing recipebook record from v396. **Tests: new `tests/cases/33-recipe-prep-roundtrip.js` (43 assertions), no existing test file touched.** Export contents incl. ids/headers/existing prep; the paste side against a clean reply, a fenced block, chatty prose, zero-width characters (both breaking the parse and surviving into the text), a foreign payload, unparseable text, an empty paste, an empty prep string, an unknown recipe, the name fallback, and a duplicate id; then end to end — the pre-tick rule both ways, the replacement warning, nothing written before Apply, `updated` stamped, the unticked row untouched, **ten other fields unchanged**, an inline edit surviving a tick/re-render and being what actually gets written, an emptied row not written, the `-1` guard against a mid-review deletion (no throw, no resurrection), a recipe name containing `<img src=x onerror=…>` escaped in the review, and the helper clearing state on exit. Verified non-vacuous three ways, each failing exactly its own assertions: disabling the invisible-character strip fails only the zero-width text assertion; pre-ticking rows that already have prep fails the pre-tick, the Apply count and the untouched-row assertions; making the apply also write `notes` fails only the ten-field comparison. Restored, re-confirmed **36/36 files green**. One behaviour worth knowing: emptying the recipe book makes `renderRecipes()` re-seed the starter recipe via `seedRecipes()`, so a test asserting "nothing was resurrected" must check for the specific id, not for an empty book.
- **v389:** **Fix: `sw.js`'s `addAll` staleness window (Cathal's ask 03/08/2026, fifth of the bug queue; build-ready for plain Sonnet per the Pending entry, which already scoped the exact one-line fix).** `install` ran `c.addAll(ASSETS)`, and those fetches went through the normal browser HTTP cache — `updateViaCache: "none"` on the registration exempts only `sw.js` itself, not the assets it fetches. GitHub Pages serves `index.html` with a short `max-age`, so a new SW installing inside that window could populate the freshly-named `hearth-vNNN` cache with the **previous build's** `index.html` — cache name says vNNN, contents are vNNN-1. It self-heals on a later open via the fetch handler's existing background refresh, but costs another open or two, and is a second independent reason a version bump can appear not to land (distinct from the v382 falsely-fresh-label bug). **Fix:** `c.addAll(ASSETS.map(u => new Request(u, { cache: 'reload' })))` — forces every install fetch past the HTTP cache, exactly the one-liner the Pending entry had already scoped. **Not verified against live GitHub Pages headers** — same caveat the Pending entry carried forward: this sandbox has no outbound access to `coshea321.github.io` (confirmed again this session via both a direct `curl` and the `WebFetch` tool, both 403). The fix is safe regardless of the actual header value though — forcing a fresh fetch during install can only ever be *at least* as fresh as relying on the HTTP cache, never worse, so shipping without live confirmation carries no real risk. No data-model, export, or sync changes. **Tests: none added, and that's a deliberate call, not an oversight.** The SW test server (`tests/server.js`) always sends `Cache-Control: no-store` on every response by design — `02-sw-update-banner.js`'s own comment says the update tests *depend on* the browser re-fetching `sw.js` rather than reusing an HTTP-cached copy, so weakening that global default to model GitHub Pages' real caching behaviour risks flaking existing coverage. A faithful test would need a new opt-in server mode (real `Cache-Control`, changing response bodies between fetches, browser HTTP-cache priming) — real infrastructure, disproportionate to a one-line, standard-Fetch-API-idiom fix. `node tests/run.js` re-run as-is: **31/31 files green** (no regression). Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v388:** **Fix: `normaliseNotes()` had no null guard (Cathal's ask 03/08/2026, fourth of the bug queue; build-ready for plain Sonnet, no design call needed).** Found while building v384's global search — `n.body`/`n.title` were read straight off each entry with nothing checking `n` itself first, so a `null` in `fl4_notes_global`/`fl4_notes_global_work` (sync merges and bad writes are both capable of producing one) would throw. v384 narrowly worked around it **only inside `globalSearchNotesPool()`**, filtering raw nulls before handing the array to `normaliseNotes()`, and explicitly logged the function itself as still-exposed for every other caller — the Notes section's own rendering (`renderNotesSection()`), opening or highlighting a note (`openNoteById`, `toggleNoteHighlightById`), and the Family Log event aggregator (`renderFamlog()`) all called `normaliseNotes()` directly on the raw store with no pre-filter. **Fix is at the source, not the symptom** — the same shape as the v385 `getRecipeBook()` fix: `normaliseNotes()` itself (index.html ~line 7817) now filters non-object/null entries before mapping, so every caller gets clean data from one place. `globalSearchNotesPool()`'s now-redundant local pre-filter and its explanatory comment were simplified away rather than left as dead-weight duplication of the same guard. No data-model, export, or sync changes — nulls are garbage entries. **Tests:** new `tests/cases/28-normalise-notes-null-guard.js` (9 assertions) — `normaliseNotes()` against `null`/`undefined`/a null entry/multiple leading nulls; the Notes section itself rendering with a null present and still showing the good note; `openNoteById()` and `toggleNoteHighlightById()` both surviving a null entry; the Family Log aggregator surviving a null in either notes store. Verified non-vacuous: reverting the one-line filter fails both this new test file **and** `tests/cases/26-global-search.js` (since its local workaround was simplified away, it now depends on the source-level fix) with the exact `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'body')` the fix prevents; restored, re-confirmed **31/31 files green**. Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v387:** **Global search phase 2 — Recipes (Cathal's ask 03/08/2026: "add recipes to global search"; third of the bug-queue/feature session; build-ready for plain Sonnet per `HEARTH-global-search.md` §8/§9, which had already flagged this as "his call, not a build decision" and left the mechanics scoped).** `GLOBAL_SEARCH_GROUPS` gains a `recipes` entry (🍽, themed `#B5651D` to match the Recipes section); `globalSearchPool("recipes")` reads `getRecipeBook()` (already null-guarded at the source since v385 — no extra guarding needed here); matching reuses v383's **wider** `recipeSearchText(r)` (name, notes, category, method, ingredients) via a new small dispatcher `globalSearchText(key, item)`, rather than the plain `listSearchText` every other group uses — so a query like "penne" or "scrambles" that only a list item's name+notes could never answer now works through global search too, exactly the "which recipe used the chorizo?" case the design doc named as the reason Recipes was the obvious phase 2. **Colour handling refactored slightly:** `GLOBAL_SEARCH_GROUPS` entries now each carry their own `color` (copied through into `globalSearch()`'s returned groups), replacing the render-time `LIST_TYPES[g.key].color` lookup — Recipes isn't a `LIST_TYPES` entry, so the old lookup would have thrown; every existing group's colour is unchanged, just sourced from one place instead of two. **The hand-off needed a new `openRecord("recipes", null, opts)` branch** — Recipes wasn't built in v384 (explicitly deferred, `HEARTH-global-search.md` §4 table). It resets `_recipeFilter` to `"all"` (never silently AND a hand-off with a stale category chip, the v381/v386 bug class) and pre-fills `_recipeSearchQuery` from `opts.query`, opening the search row/input the same way the existing Lists branch does. **The one genuinely tricky bit:** v386 (shipped minutes earlier, same session) made `switchSection`'s `"recipes"` branch unconditionally clear `_recipeSearchQuery` on every arrival, to fix "leaving mid-search and returning leaves a stale search." A naive `openRecord` hand-off would have set the query and then immediately had it wiped by that same reset. Fixed with a one-shot flag, `_recipeSearchPreset`: `openRecord` sets it `true` immediately before calling `switchSection("recipes")`; the arrival-reset checks it first — if true, it consumes the flag and skips the reset just this once; any later, ordinary arrival (nav tap, back button) still gets the full v386 reset. No data-model, export, or sync changes. **Explicitly still out of scope** (per the design doc, unchanged by this version): Trips/Plants/Baby/Medicine/Track remain excluded; no id-level deep link into a single recipe (lands on the Recipes list with the query applied, same as every other group). **Tests:** extended `tests/cases/26-global-search.js` (46 → 58 assertions, no new file) — a new `RECIPES` fixture (including a trailing `null`, mirroring the known `fl4_recipebook` bug class) pins that "milk" finds a recipe via an **ingredient** match (not name/notes), that "penne" — zero name/notes overlap — still finds Chorizo Pasta (proving the wider haystack is actually used), that the null entry doesn't crash the group, that a real "curry" hit now legitimately appears in *both* grocery and recipes (updated from the v384 fixture, which only had a grocery hit), and the full hand-off: a stale category filter reset, the query pre-filled into the Recipes search row/input, the right recipe shown, and — the interaction-fix-specific case — that a LATER ordinary arrival at Recipes (not via `openRecord`) still gets the v386 stale-search reset, i.e. the one-shot flag doesn't get stuck true. Verified non-vacuous three ways: reverting the `GLOBAL_SEARCH_GROUPS` entry crashes the test file outright (recipes group `undefined`); reverting the `_recipeSearchPreset` check alone fails exactly the two hand-off-prefill assertions with the query landing as `""`; both restored, re-confirmed **30/30 files green**. Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v386:** **Fix: two recipe-search state dead ends left by v381 (Cathal's ask 03/08/2026, second of the bug queue; build-ready for plain Sonnet, no design call needed).** v381 fixed search silently AND-ing with a stale Favourites filter, but only in the direction of *opening* search — two other doors to the identical symptom (a real match hidden by a leftover filter, reading as "search does nothing" with no visible cause) were left open, both confirmed by headless repro before fixing. **(a) A category chip tapped while a search was active silently re-ANDed the two** — searching "curry" then tapping "Dinner" (say) could show "Nothing here yet." even with Chicken Curry sitting right there, because the chip only changed `_recipeFilter` and left `_recipeSearchQuery` armed. Fix: the `.rcp-filter` click handler (`renderRecipes()`, ~line 5479) now also clears `_recipeSearchQuery`, hides `#rcpSearchRow` and empties `#rcpSearchInput` whenever a search was active — mirroring v381's fix in the opposite direction (that one resets the filter when search *opens*; this resets the search when a chip is tapped). **(b) Leaving Recipes mid-search and coming back left the query armed and the search row open** — the list read as mysteriously filtered on return with no way to tell why. Fix: `switchSection`'s `"recipes"` branch (~line 10840) now resets `_recipeSearchQuery` + hides the row + clears the input on every fresh arrival, the same pattern v384 already established for the Home search row (`_gSearchQuery` cleared in `switchSection`'s `"home"` branch, not in the renderer itself). **Deliberately NOT inside `renderRecipes()`** for either fix — that function also runs on a chip tap from a *different* origin and on a sync-triggered redraw while already on the screen, neither of which should wipe an in-progress search; the resets live at the two actual trigger points (a chip click, a fresh section arrival) instead. No data-model, export, or sync changes — this is DOM/render-state wiring only. **Tests:** extended `tests/cases/25-recipe-search.js` (18 → 26 assertions, no new file) — a chip tap while a real match is being hidden by a stale zero-match search correctly clears the query/row/input and shows the chip's own recipes; leaving Recipes (`switchSection("home")`) mid-search and returning (`switchSection("recipes")`) correctly clears the same three and shows the whole book, not a filtered remnant. Verified non-vacuous: reverted each fix independently and re-ran — (a) reverted alone fails its 4 assertions with the list still reading "No recipes match "zzzzqqq"." after the chip tap; (b) reverted alone fails its 4 assertions with the query still `"curry"` and only 1 of 12 recipes showing on return. Restored both, re-confirmed **30/30 files green**. Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v385:** **Fix: a `null` entry in `fl4_recipebook` crashed the entire Recipes section (Cathal's ask 03/08/2026 — first of a queue of recently-found bugs being worked through one by one, alongside adding Recipes to global search; build-ready for plain Sonnet, no design call needed).** `renderRecipes()`'s category-collection loop did `(r.category||"").trim()` with no null guard, so one bad record (sync merges and CSV import can both produce one) threw before anything rendered — the section went fully dead, not merely missing one row. **Fix is at the source, not the symptom:** `getRecipeBook()` itself (index.html ~line 2533) now filters out null entries before returning, rather than patching `renderRecipes()`'s loop alone — the Tidy view, Week planner and Grocery-pick screen all call `getRecipeBook()` independently and would otherwise still crash on the exact same corrupt store. One line changed. No data-model, export, or sync changes — nulls are garbage entries, nothing legitimately depends on one being present. **Tests:** new `tests/cases/27-recipe-null-guard.js` (6 assertions) — `getRecipeBook()` drops a null; the list view renders and still shows the good recipe alongside a trailing OR a **leading** null (worst case for a naive filter); the Tidy and Week-plan views, which fetch the book independently, both survive too. Verified non-vacuous: reverted the one-line fix and re-ran — 5 assertions fail with the exact `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'category')` (list view) and `reading 'ingredients'` (Tidy view) the fix prevents; restored and re-confirmed 29/29 files green. `node tests/run.js`: **29/29 files green**. Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v384:** **Global search — a 🔍 finder on Home over Lists + Notes, built from the design record (Cathal's four confirmed decisions 02/08/2026, full brief `HEARTH-global-search.md`; build was a plain-Sonnet job from the doc, no design call needed).** Scans Grocery/General/Personal/Travel item names+notes and Notes (personal+work) title+body, groups results by list type under the search box on Home, and hands off to the section that owns a match rather than rendering records itself — no new store, no sync changes, nothing added to export/backup, nothing scheduled. **Matching (§2②): substring (`indexOf`) is PRIMARY; `fuzzyMatch` runs ONLY as a fallback, and only when substring found nothing in EVERY group at once** (never per-group — proven by a pinned test: a "curry" search with a real hit in Grocery correctly suppresses a fuzzy-eligible near-miss in Personal). This is the v383 recipe-search lesson applied on the first try, not relearned the hard way: `fuzzyMatch` as a *primary* matcher over-matches badly at app scale. 2-character minimum before searching. New top-level `listSearchText(item)` (name+notes, lowercased once, `!item`-guarded) mirrors v383's `recipeSearchText`. **Structural prerequisite built first, per the design doc's §4: `openRecord(type, id, opts)`**, a single deep-link helper replacing three hand-rolled copies of "set state + switchSection() + render" that had drifted slightly out of sync (the Today card's grocery/todo/trips/plants rows, the Family Log card's trips row, and a second trips copy inside the Today card itself). Handles list types (via `LIST_CONFIG`), notes (with an optional `ctx` to land on the matched note's personal/work sub-tab), trips and plants — state is now set *before* calling `switchSection()`, so its own dispatch-aware render (`renderTrips()`/`renderPlants()` already branch on `_tripView`/`_plantView`) paints correctly first time, removing the redundant extra render calls the old hand-rolled versions needed. **Hand-off resets the target list's own filters** (search query, travel-tag filter, "today" filter) rather than silently AND-ing with whatever was already set — the exact v381 bug class, pinned by a test that seeds stale filters before the hand-off and asserts they're gone after. **Results UI:** grouped by list type, capped at 5 each with a "+N more →" that jumps to that list with its own search pre-filled; empty groups omitted entirely; empty state names the failed query (never a bare "Nothing here yet."); the fuzzy-fallback state says "No exact matches — showing similar." 🔍 row lives near the top of Home's static markup (not a nav-bar slot), ~120ms debounced (the only search in the app scanning multiple stores per keystroke); clears on every fresh arrival at Home but NOT on a background re-render while already there (a sync landing mid-search must not wipe it — this lives in `switchSection`'s "home" branch, not in `renderHomeScreen()`, which also runs on sync-triggered redraws). **Bug found and fixed while building, in scope because global search's own code path exercises it every keystroke:** `normaliseNotes()` has no null guard (reads `n.body`/`n.title` straight off each entry) — a null in `fl4_notes_global(_work)`, which sync merges or a bad write can produce, would have crashed the search on every keystroke rather than just the Notes section. Fixed narrowly, inside the new `globalSearchNotesPool()`, by filtering raw nulls before handing the array to `normaliseNotes()` — `normaliseNotes()` itself is untouched (that's a separate, pre-existing, already-tracked bug class, see the Pending list). **Explicitly out of v1 (design doc §1/§9, not re-litigated):** Recipes, Trips, Plants, Baby, Medicine, Track logs; no id-level deep link into a single list item or note (lands on the right list/context, not the exact record); no search history; no ranking beyond name-beats-notes. **Tests:** new `tests/cases/26-global-search.js` (46 assertions) — `listSearchText`'s null guard and lowercasing; the 2-char minimum; a realistic multi-record fixture (deliberately not seed-scale, per §3.4) with 7 "milk"-matching grocery items to pin the 5-cap + "+2 more", cross-group matching across all 5 categories in one query, and an empty (General/todo) group correctly omitted rather than shown blank; the global-not-per-group fuzzy-suppression behaviour both ways (a real hit elsewhere suppresses a fuzzy near-miss; zero hits anywhere correctly triggers fuzzy across every group); the empty-state and fuzzy-label wording on screen; the "+N more" hand-off including the stale-filter-reset assertions; a Notes tap landing on the correct personal/work context; `openRecord`'s trip/plant paths (including that a plant hand-off discards a half-finished import and a stale area filter, per the design doc's per-domain table); and the Home-arrival-clears-but-background-redraw-doesn't-wipe search-state distinction. **Verified non-vacuous:** temporarily reverted the `normaliseNotes` null-guard fix and re-ran — the suite fails with the exact `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'body')` the fix prevents; restored and re-confirmed 28/28 files green. `node tests/run.js`: **28/28 files green** (26 file:// cases, incl. this new one, + 3 SW cases). Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v383:** **Recipe search actually works now — the third and correct diagnosis (Cathal 02/08/2026, Opus session).** **Read this before touching recipe search again.** v381 and v382 both fixed real bugs and neither was the one he was hitting. His decisive report: after v382 the chips *do* jump Favourites→All (so v381's fix was live), he was typing **a recipe's actual name**, and **"nothing changed at all"** — the list did not visibly filter. **Root cause: `fuzzyMatch` is a SUBSEQUENCE matcher**, not a substring one — `"curry"` matches any text containing c..u..r..r..y in order, e.g. Beef Stew's notes "slow cooker … crusty bread, very yummy". Harmless on the 1-recipe seed book (which is why githack-in-incognito "seemed to work" — test mode uses sandboxed storage and seed data, **never his real book**); on a realistic book it returned most of the shelf. Measured on a 12-recipe repro **before** the fix: `"curry"` → **7 of 12**, `"pie"` → 5, `"stew"` → 4. Grouped by category and scrolled on a phone, that reads as "the search did nothing". **Second, independent gap found on the way:** the haystack was `r.name + " " + r.notes` **only** — searching an ingredient, a method word or a category returned a silent zero ("penne" → 0, "scrambles" → 0, "breakfast" → 0). **Fix is a PAIR and must stay one — widening without tightening makes the over-matching strictly worse.** ① **Wider haystack** via a new top-level `recipeSearchText(r)` (name, notes, category, method, ingredient `name` + `groceryName`, lowercased once; `!r` guarded). ② **Tighter match:** substring is now primary; the fuzzy pass runs **only when substring finds nothing**, and when it does the list renders "No exact matches — showing similar" so approximate results are never passed off as hits. After: `"curry"` → 1, `"pie"` → 2 (Shepherd's + Fish, correct), `"penne"`/`"scrambles"`/`"breakfast"` → 1/1/3, typo `"chikcen"` → fuzzy fallback, labelled. ③ **Empty states now say WHY** — "No recipes match “zzzz”." / "Nothing in “Lunch” yet." instead of a bare "Nothing here yet.", because that unexplained empty state is precisely what made this read as a broken app three versions running. No `fav` empty-state branch on purpose: the `hasFav` fallback above already flips an empty Favourites filter to "all", so it would be dead code. **Tests: new file `tests/cases/25-recipe-search.js` (18 assertions)** — pins that a name search NARROWS the list (bounded at a third of the book, so it fails on real over-matching rather than on tuning), that ingredient/method/category searches hit, that an exact match does not drag in fuzzy near-misses, that the fuzzy fallback labels itself, that empty states name the query/category, and that a recipe with missing fields or a null ingredient does not throw. Verified non-vacuous by reverting the matcher and re-running: **7 assertions fail**, headline one reading `searching "curry" narrows the list: showed 6 of 12` — Cathal's bug, reproduced in the suite. 27/27 files green. **Latent bug found, NOT fixed, logged in Pending:** a `null` entry anywhere in `fl4_recipebook` crashes the whole Recipes section at the category-collection loop (`r.category` unguarded) — not what he hit, but a real unguarded-lookup trap of exactly the class `CLAUDE.md` warns about.
- **v382:** **The in-app version label no longer claims a version it isn't running (Cathal's report 02/08/2026: "v381 was supposed to fix the recipe search but hasn't"; diagnosed on Opus).** **The v381 fix was correct all along** — proved with a headless repro of the exact user gesture (seed one favourite + one non-favourite, tap Search, type the non-favourite's name): on v380 code the list reads "Nothing here yet.", on v381 code the recipe is found. Cathal was **still running v380 JavaScript** while the app displayed "v381". **Root cause:** `sw.js` and `index.html` update on independent schedules. The page loads the cached (old) shell — correct, that is v373's deliberate cache-first-with-background-refresh trade-off — then the new SW installs, `skipWaiting`+`claim`s the page, and the page asks its version; the `SW_VERSION` handler wrote that answer into `#appVersionLabel` **unconditionally**, so the label read the NEW version while the OLD build's code was executing. The update banner *did* fire correctly, but a label already "proving" you were up to date makes that banner read as a glitch — so it gets dismissed and the shipped fix stays invisible. `CLAUDE.md` anticipated this one way round ("a stale-looking version means the new SW hasn't activated yet, not a bug"); nobody anticipated the reverse, a **falsely-fresh** label. **Fix:** one guarded line — the label is written only when `_verNum(announced) <= _verNum(PAGE_VERSION)`. A strictly newer worker now gets the banner **only**; the label keeps naming what is actually executing until a reload makes it true. Same-or-older still writes through, preserving the original "label is sourced from sw.js" intent. **Tests changed — `tests/sw-cases/02-sw-update-banner.js`, and this is a real behaviour change, not a quiet test edit:** that test used "the label flipped to the announced version" as its signal that the SW update round trip had completed, which by design no longer happens in the NEWER direction. `installWorkerAnnouncing(version)` now takes a second `doneWhen` argument so each direction waits on the signal true for it (OLDER → label flip, as before; NEWER → the banner appearing). **One assertion added** pinning the new behaviour: after a NEWER worker announces itself the label must NOT read that version. Verified non-vacuous by reverting the one-line fix and re-running — it fails with `label read "v999 · 01/01/2030" while the page is v382`, i.e. it reproduces Cathal's exact bug. Suite otherwise unchanged: 26/26 files green. **Deliberately NOT fixed in this version (Cathal's call, offered and declined 02/08/2026 — both remain open, see Pending):** the `sw.js` `addAll` staleness window, and the two leftover recipe-search dead ends.
- **v381:** **Recipe search bug fix (Cathal's report 02/08/2026, build-ready for plain Sonnet, no design call needed).** Cathal reported recipe search "seems broken." Root cause, confirmed by a live headless-browser repro before and after: `renderRecipes()` defaults `_recipeFilter` to `"fav"` whenever any recipe is favourited (opens on Favourites by default, v-old behaviour), and opening the search box never reset that filter — so searching for a recipe that wasn't a favourite (or wasn't in whatever category chip was active) silently returned "Nothing here yet" with zero indication why, reading exactly like a broken search. **Fix:** the `rcpSearchBtn` click handler's opening branch now sets `_recipeFilter = "all"` and calls `renderRecipes()` before focusing the input whenever the filter wasn't already "all" — opening search always searches the whole recipe book, and the filter-bar chip highlighting stays in sync (jumps to "All" visibly). One-line change (index.html, in the `DOMContentLoaded` recipe-search wiring block, ~line 11313). No data-model, sync, or export changes. **Tests:** no test file changed — this is DOM/render-state wiring the `file://` harness doesn't specifically cover (same class as other UI-timing fixes); `node tests/run.js` re-run as-is, 27/27 files green (no regression). Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT. **Also discussed:** revisiting **global search** (parked backlog item, see External review triage note above) — flagged to Cathal as fuzzy/undecided design work per the standing model-check rule; no design session held yet, recommended he open a fresh Fable/Opus session for that when ready. No decisions recorded here since none were made.
- **v380:** **New "Medicine" tab in the Track section — a factual family medicine log with quick-add chips, and Food moved to the end of the subnav (Cathal's ask 02/08/2026, built on Sonnet — flagged the standing model-check rule since it touches the medicine domain + a new synced store, Cathal chose to continue on Sonnet given the well-defined scope; 3 further design decisions confirmed via AskUserQuestion).** He asked to keep the main "Log anything" tab for infrequent items by giving medicine its own page, with quick-add for the family's regulars — Nurofen, Panadol, Amoxicillin — and to move the rarely-used Food journal to the end of the tab row. **Confirmed decisions:** ① **new dedicated store** (`fl4_track_med`) rather than folding into `action_log` with a type tag — clean separation, easy to reorder; ② **name + dose + time + notes** fields, the same shape as the existing baby medicine log, not a minimal name-only entry; ③ quick-add chips **pre-fill the form** (name field, then focus dose) rather than logging instantly — avoids a mis-tap silently logging a dose; ④ **personal-only sync**, matching how the existing Log/Food/Body tabs already work — explicitly NOT household-shared like Baby medicine, even though a family medicine log has an obvious double-dosing argument for sharing, because that would be new scope beyond "give me a page to log this" and Cathal's call was to stay consistent with the rest of Track. **This is deliberately NOT the Baby section's dosing calculator** — no weight-based math, no product-strength chips, no interval advisories (≥4h/≥6h gap, max-per-24h). Those stay exclusively in `renderMedicine()`/`MED_RULES` for the Baby flow, never duplicated here; this new page is factual logging only, same safety posture as the existing Log tab. **Implementation mirrors `action_log`/`food_log` exactly** (the closest existing analog — personal-channel sync with tombstones, v369): `TRACK_VIEWS` gained a `medicine` entry (`notesMedicineView` div, `renderTrackMedicine()`); new `getTrackMed()`/`saveTrackMed()`/`doLogTrackMed()` alongside the existing `getActionLog()`/`saveActionLog()` pair; delete uses the generic `addTomb("track_med", id)` (new `fl4_tomb_track_med` store); `pushPersonal()` sends `track_med`/`track_med_deleted`; `applyPersonal()` gained an id-keyed-union + tombstone-filtered merge block (same shape as the food_log block right after it); `importBackupData()` gained an additive-only union restore line for `data.track_med`, deliberately with no tombstone filtering — same precedent as action_log/food_log, not the medicine/growth/milestone class that filters restores through tombstones. Export payload gained `track_med: getTrackMed()`, coverage comment updated. **Subnav reorder:** `trackSubNav` order is now Log · Medicine · Body · Food (was Log · Food · Body) — pure markup reorder, `TRACK_VIEWS` is keyed by name not position so nothing else depended on the old order. No data-model, export, or sync-protocol changes to any *other* store. **Tests:** no test file changed — this is a new personal-only log store with no dosing/interval logic, the same class of change v369's own changelog entry notes the harness doesn't specifically cover for action_log/food_log either; `node tests/run.js` re-run as-is, 27/27 files green (no regression). Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT. **Model note:** this would have been fine to flag-and-continue on Sonnet from the start — the design was well-defined (a logging page + quick-add chips, no dose math), and the two AskUserQuestion rounds (feature shape, then sync scope) were the kind of small confirms Sonnet handles fine; worth noting for calibration since the standing rule's "medicine" trigger fired on domain vocabulary rather than actual dosing risk.

- **v379:** **Plants — a permanent Home card, and area filtering (Cathal's ask 02/08/2026; designed + built on Opus, 2 decisions confirmed via AskUserQuestion).** Two asks in one: "add a link on the home page so I can get to Plants without clogging up the bottom navigation — it can show in the Today card but that won't be consistent", and "area tags so I can filter by outside plants or kitchen plants". **Confirmed decisions:** ① the Home link is a **third glance card** in the `today-split` grid alongside Training and Baby (not a generic "jump to" chip row for all the opt-in sections) — it carries state rather than being a bare link; ② area tags **reuse the existing `room` field** rather than adding a new one, so every plant already in the book and every `plant-v1` file written before v379 filters correctly with **nothing to migrate and no change to the import/export map**. **The Home card is the answer to "that won't be consistent": it never self-suppresses.** The v377 Today-card row still appears only when something is overdue — that stays — but the new `tcPlantsCard` is painted on every `renderHomeScreen`, showing the plant count plus one line from new top-level **`plantHomeSummary(plants)`**: the plant's name when exactly one needs watering, "N plants need watering" when several do, otherwise "All watered · next in N days" from the soonest scheduled plant, "Nothing scheduled" when no plant has an interval it has actually logged against, and "Tap to add your first plant" on an empty book. It carries the v377 **"don't nag" rules unchanged** — a plant with no interval, or one never logged, is not overdue and cannot make the card red. Tapping always lands on the **unfiltered list** (`_plantArea = ""`), because the card counted every plant and a filtered landing would contradict the number just tapped; the Today-card deep link clears the filter for the same reason. **Areas: three pure helpers, all testable.** `plantAreaKey()` trims + lowercases; **`plantAreas(plants)`** returns the distinct areas grouped case-insensitively so "kitchen" typed once doesn't split off its own chip — the chip label is the **commonest spelling, preferring a capitalised one on a tie**, deliberately not "first one seen", which would have made the capitalisation depend on which plant sorted first (caught in a live render check, not by a test); **`plantsInArea(plants, area)`** filters, with `""` meaning every plant and the JS-only sentinel `PLANT_AREA_NONE` meaning the untagged ones. **UI:** a horizontally scrolling chip row above the list — All · each area · "No area" — shown only when there are at least two things to choose between, since one area filters nothing. The overdue banner now counts **what is on screen**, so the banner and the list can never disagree. The filter is a **session-only module var, never stored** and never synced, and self-heals: if the chosen area vanishes (its last plant retagged, deleted, or dropped by a sync from the other phone) it falls back to All rather than showing an empty list with no explanation. The editor's **"Room" label is now "Area"** with a `<datalist>` of the areas already in use — still a plain text box for a new area, but spellings stop drifting at the source; the plant card subtitle shows `latin · 📍 area` instead of `latin || room`, so the thing the chips filter on is visible on every row. The `room` guidance line in `plantAIPrompt()` now names the area idea ("Kitchen", "Outside"). **No data-model change at all** — no new field, `plantExportObj`/`plantApplyImport` untouched, no sync or merge code touched. **Sentinel gotcha worth remembering:** the first draft used a NUL-prefixed sentinel string as the untagged marker, which turned `index.html` into a binary file as far as `grep` was concerned (and a NUL in an HTML attribute is rewritten to U+FFFD by the parser); it is a plain `"__none__"` string now, and it never reaches the DOM — chips carry `data-kind="all|area|none"` instead. **`tests/` changed (declared per policy): one new file, no existing test touched.** New `tests/cases/24-plant-areas.js` (25 assertions) — the area key, case-insensitive grouping, the tidiest-spelling label and its independence from book order, filtering in every mode incl. the untagged sentinel and an area nobody uses, null entries dropped, no mutation of the book, and every `plantHomeSummary` wording incl. due-today, never-logged and the empty book. `node tests/run.js`: **27/27 files green** (24 `file://` + 3 SW). Also verified in a live headless render: chips, chip taps, the filtered banner, the "No area" chip, the datalist and the Home card line. **Model note:** the two design forks were real, so Opus was a defensible call — but with those answers in hand this was a plain-Sonnet build: UI-only, no sync, no dosing, no service worker.

- **v378:** **Plant import/export — the AI round-trip (Cathal's ask 02/08/2026; designed + built on Opus, 3 decisions confirmed via AskUserQuestion).** He asked for the flow he already uses for trips, applied to plants: photograph a plant, have an AI chat write the care sheet from the photo + his own notes, get a file back, paste it into Hearth and have it fill in the sections. **Confirmed decisions:** ① **two-step import, ask each time** (paste → choose "Add as a new plant" or "Update <existing>") rather than always-new or silent name-matching; ② **"file wins, blanks left alone"** — a section the file fills in replaces what's there, a section it leaves `""` keeps the existing text (so re-running a better write-up over a rough note works, and a thin file can't wipe good notes); ③ **per-plant export + "Export all plants"**, the latter as a shareable care-book file, NOT a backup (the Settings backup is still the only thing that carries photos and logs). **File format `plant-v1`**, deliberately the trip-importer's shape: a bare plant object *or* `{"hearth":"plant-v1","plants":[…]}`, so a one-plant chat reply and a whole-book export come through the same door. **What is NOT in the file, by design:** `id` (regenerated), `updated` (set to now), `photo` (a chat can't write one, and base64 would swamp the paste box) and `waterLog`/`feedLog` (own history, not care information) — so an update **never** touches the photo or the logging streak, which is the promise the UI makes on that screen and is pinned by tests. **Field mapping is PLANT_SECTIONS-driven** — export, import and the AI prompt template all iterate the same table, so a ninth section stays a one-line change; only the six scalars (`name`/`latin`/`room`/`emoji`/`waterDays`/`feedDays`) are hand-listed, in `plantExportObj` + `plantApplyImport`, and both must be updated together (noted in `CLAUDE.md` alongside the trip rule). **The prompt template is the feature's real payload:** `plantAIPrompt()` builds it from `PLANT_SECTIONS` (skeleton JSON + one guidance line per section, using each section's own `hint`), so it can never drift from what the parser accepts; a "📋 Copy the prompt" button puts it on the clipboard (`navigator.clipboard` with an `execCommand` fallback — writeText needs a secure context and a gesture), with the full text also visible under a `<details>` for anyone who'd rather select it. Tests assert the skeleton is valid JSON, contains every section key, and carries no HTML entities (PLANT_SECTIONS labels are HTML source — `&amp;` is decoded for the plain-text prompt). **Defensive parsing throughout:** ```json fences stripped before parsing (chats add them even when told not to), wrong-tag files rejected by name (a trip file is not a plant file), null/non-object array entries dropped, every section capped at `PLANT_FIELD_MAX` (4000 chars) so a runaway reply can't eat localStorage, intervals coerced/clamped to 0–365 with junk text → 0 ("no reminder") rather than NaN, and `importPlantInto` guards the `findIndex` `-1`. Interval semantics: a field the file **omits** keeps its old value, but an explicit `0` is a real instruction and clears the reminder. **UI:** 📥 button in the plants header (mirroring trips), export buttons on the detail view and under the list, and `_plantImporting` added to the `flushSyncRenders` guard — the import screen holds pasted text that isn't saved yet, so an incoming sync must not redraw it away (the trip importer still has this gap; worth copying back if it ever bites). Every route out of the section (`bnPlants`, the Today-card deep link, opening the editor) clears the import state so a half-finished paste can't strand the view. **`tests/` changed (declared per policy): one new file, no existing test touched.** New `tests/cases/23-plant-import-export.js` (45 assertions) — parser guards, the update rule in both directions, photo/log survival, the export→import round trip, unique ids across a multi-plant import, the `-1` guard, and the prompt template's integrity. `node tests/run.js`: **26/26 files green** (23 `file://` + 3 SW). **Model note:** borderline. The import/export + fuzzy-design triggers made Opus the correct default under the standing rule, and the file-format and update-rule decisions were genuinely material — but with those three answers in hand the build itself would have been fine on Sonnet.
- **v377:** **New Plants section — a houseplant care book with watering/feeding tracking (Cathal's ask 01/08/2026; designed + built on Opus, 4 decisions confirmed via AskUserQuestion).** He asked for "a list of my plants, subsections showing a summary and feeding/watering info, then lower sections with even more information", and supplied a worked example (a Spider Plant write-up: species + botanical name, observations, watering, feeding, repotting, propagation, child/pet safety). The section is built to hold exactly that shape. **Confirmed decisions:** ① **reference + logging**, not reference only — a "Log" button per plant records when it was actually watered/fed, which is the part a note can't do; ② **named sections you paste into** (not one free-text blob, and explicitly NOT an auto-splitter that guesses at headings — a misfire would mean editing the app's guesses); ③ **photo per plant, auto-shrunk**; ④ **opt-in nav icon, off by default** (the bar already carries five). **Data model — one new store `fl4_plants`, household-shared.** Each plant is `{id, name, latin, emoji, room, photo, waterDays, feedDays, waterLog[], feedLog[], updated}` plus one text field per entry in the new top-level **`PLANT_SECTIONS`** table (`summary`, `watering`, `feeding`, `light`, `repotting`, `propagation`, `safety`, `notes`). That table is the single source of truth: the detail view, the editor and the section ordering all iterate it, so adding a ninth section later is a one-line change. `top`/`care` flags mark the three that render above the **"More on this plant"** divider (summary, watering, feeding) — that divider IS Cathal's "then lower sections" ask, made structural rather than left to paste order. An empty section simply doesn't render. **Sync: whole-record newest-wins by `updated` + deletion tombstones (`fl4_tomb_plants`)** — deliberately the recipebook contract, NOT the nested per-booking merge trips use: a plant is a single edit unit with nothing inside it for two devices to edit separately. New top-level **`mergePlantsData(local, incoming, tombs)` → `{plants, push}`**, same contract as `mergeTripsData`/`mergeMealPlanData`, wired into **both** channels (`pushPersonal`/`pushHousehold` send `plants` + `plants_deleted`; `applyPersonal`/`applyHousehold` merge and re-push to converge). Carries the **v296 field-fill rule** (keys `undefined` on the winner are filled from the loser, so a copy written by a pre-v377 build can't wipe fields it never heard of — the babySex incident class; a field deliberately cleared is `""` and stays cleared). `flushSyncRenders` gained a `dirty.plants` line guarded by `!_plantEditing`, so a sync landing mid-edit can't re-render the editor away. **Scheduling — the "don't nag" rules are the design, and they're pinned by tests.** `plantDueIn(p, kind)` returns null for *both* "no interval set" **and** "never logged yet": a plant you haven't watered yet is not overdue, or every plant would land red the moment it's added. Day counts run from local midnight to local midnight (the `tripDaysUntil` rule) so a DST change can't shift them. `waterLog`/`feedLog` are newest-first and **capped at `PLANT_LOG_CAP` (40)** — this is a "when did I last do it" tracker, not a diary, and an uncapped array would ride the sync payload forever. Each care row gets an **Undo** button while today's entry is the newest one (mis-taps are the obvious failure mode of a one-tap logger). **Photos are the one real risk and are treated as such:** `plantShrinkPhoto` renders through a canvas to a ~400px JPEG at q0.7 (~40KB) **before** anything reaches localStorage, and `plantStorageWarn` toasts once when the whole store passes 1.5MB — well before `storeSet`'s QuotaExceeded catch, and while it's still fixable. Photos ride inside the plant records, so they sync and export with everything else; that is the honest cost of the feature and the reason for both guards. **Home Today card** gained a self-suppressing 💧 row — it appears only when something is genuinely overdue, deep-links to the plant when exactly one is due, otherwise to the list (which leads with its own "N need watering" banner). **Nav:** `bnPlants` + `plantsSection` + a Settings toggle, added to `HIDEABLE_SECTIONS`/`navIds` with the default **off** (joining Sports/Train/Family Log); the Today-card deep link works whether or not the icon is pinned, which doubles as the discovery path. The editor is an `innerHTML` swap of `#plantsContent` like the trip editor, so `closeTopOverlay` gained the matching `_plantEditing && _plantCancelFn` discard branch (v345 back-button-v2 pattern), and the photo preview updates **in place** rather than re-rendering — a re-render would throw away everything already typed into the section boxes (the v366/v368 focus-theft lesson). Export payload + coverage comment gained `plants`; `importBackupData` gained an additive restore mirroring the recipebook block (a plant already present is left alone — its watering log is more current than any snapshot's — and a restored plant is stamped `updated=now` with its tombstone cleared, or the next pull would quietly re-delete it). **`tests/` changed (declared per policy): one new file, no existing test touched.** New `tests/cases/22-plants.js` (35 assertions) — the day arithmetic incl. never-logged and DST-safe midnight anchoring, all five `plantDueLabel` wordings, both "don't nag" rules, the care log round trip (water and feed kept separate, `updated` bumped, undo removing only the newest entry, undo on an empty log a no-op, the 40-entry cap), the full merge contract (newest-wins both directions, partner-only and local-only records kept, `push` flag, v296 field-fill, `""` staying cleared, a tombstone dropping a record and a re-add surviving its old tombstone), that `deletePlant` writes a tombstone, and that plants reach the export payload. `node tests/run.js`: **25/25 files green** (22 `file://` + 3 SW cases). Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT. **Model note:** this was the right call for Opus — the shape of the feature was genuinely undecided at the start (four forks, all material) and the sync/merge + storage-budget work is exactly the risky-area list. A follow-up that just adds a section to `PLANT_SECTIONS` or tweaks copy would be fine on plain Sonnet.
- **v376:** **Release E of the startup-freeze fix plan (`HEARTH-freeze-analysis.md`, claim 8) — the test harness can now register a real service worker, and the boot/update/dead-network scenarios are covered for the first time (3 design calls confirmed by Cathal via AskUserQuestion, 01/08/2026; designed + built on Opus). This is the last item in the freeze plan — Releases A–E are all shipped.** **No app code changed at all** — the only edits to `index.html`/`sw.js` are the version bump itself. **The gap this closes:** the analysis's claim-8 verdict found the reviewer's "test suite is broken" claim FALSE (21/21 green), but identified the real gap underneath — on `file://` **no service worker registers**, so the SW/update/network paths, i.e. *the exact area under suspicion in the whole freeze investigation*, had zero automated coverage. Everything v373 changed was pinned only by comments. **`node tests/run.js` now runs two phases:** phase 1 is the existing 21 `file://` case files, byte-for-byte unchanged in behaviour; phase 2 boots its own static server + its own browser and runs `tests/sw-cases/`. One command, one exit code — a phase-2 failure blocks a commit exactly like phase 1 (Cathal's call: "always, same command", over an opt-in `--sw` flag, on the grounds that the scenarios most likely to regress are the ones nobody remembers to run). Cost is ~8s on a ~56s run. **Confirmed decision ① (how the SW gets to register):** the app deliberately refuses to register a worker anywhere that isn't the real site (`_isTestBuild`, plus `_isPreview` which catches `localhost`/`127.0.0.1` explicitly) — so the analysis's literal suggestion of "an http://localhost server mode" **cannot work**; the page would skip SW registration exactly as it does on a raw.githack test link. Instead the harness serves the repo locally and points the **test browser's** name resolution at itself, so the app is served under the production hostname and its own guards see production. Chrome's existing `--host-resolver-rules=MAP * 127.0.0.1` (already there as the belt-and-braces network block) does the redirect, and `--unsafely-treat-insecure-origin-as-secure` on that one origin buys the secure context a worker requires — both scoped to the throwaway test profile, which is why phase 2 needs its own browser instance. **Nothing leaves the machine, and zero test scaffolding goes into `index.html`** — Cathal explicitly chose this over adding a test hook to the app, so the tests exercise the *real* production path, guards included (`01-sw-registers` asserts `_isTestBuild === false`, so if that guard ever drifts the suite says so instead of quietly testing nothing). **Confirmed decision ② (scope):** the four things with no coverage — SW registers/activates/claims/caches; the version label genuinely arrives from the worker by postMessage; old-page/new-SW **both ways round**; and opening on a dead connection. Cathal declined adding corrupt-localStorage and large-dataset boots in this release: both were on the review's wish list but neither needs a service worker, so they'd be riding along on the wrong harness. **New files:** `tests/server.js` (zero-dependency static server that can also misbehave on demand — `setMode('hang')` accepts a request and never answers it, `setMode('fail')` drops the socket, `setSwVersion(v)` serves `sw.js` with its VERSION line rewritten so the browser runs its *real* update flow against a genuinely different build) and three case files. **`tests/harness.js` gains four additive helpers**, none of which touch the existing `file://` path: `launch({extraArgs})`, `goto()` (navigate with no settle sleep, with a timeout), `waitFor()` (poll until true, swallowing the evaluate errors a navigation causes) and `resetSW()` (drop every registration + cache + localStorage between case files). **Two traps found and closed while building — both would have shipped a suite that passes without testing anything:** ⓐ a navigation does not swap the JS execution context instantly, so polling "has it booted yet" answered **yes from the page being navigated away from** — each open now stamps the outgoing document and requires the stamp to be *absent*; ⓑ far worse, **`Page.navigate` does not resolve until the navigation response arrives**, so a network wait in front of the paint lands *inside* the call and the timer started after the very thing being measured. The clock now starts before the navigation. **These were caught by a negative control, and it is the reason to trust the numbers:** `sw.js` was temporarily reverted to a pre-v373 network-first shell, and `03-sw-offline-open` was confirmed to go **red** on it (lie-fi open measured **3554ms**, the v295 3.5s cap) and green on real v375 (**~56ms**). Before the two fixes it passed the regression happily. The 2000ms deadline sits between those with ~35× headroom over a real open. Note the shapes fail differently and only one is diagnostic: a *hard* offline (socket dropped) opens fast even network-first, because the fetch rejects immediately — it is the **hanging** connection, the one Cathal actually reports, that a network-first shell mishandles. **`tests/` changed (declared per policy):** all of it is new test infrastructure plus the version bump; `tests/run.js` was refactored into two phases sharing one per-file runner, with the reporting output and phase-1 isolation rules unchanged. `node tests/run.js`: **24/24 files green** (21 file:// + 3 new SW cases, +37 assertions). Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v375:** **Release D of the startup-freeze fix plan (`HEARTH-freeze-analysis.md`, claim 6) — batched sync-apply rendering + the missing `applyPersonal` try/catch (3 design calls confirmed by Cathal via AskUserQuestion, 01/08/2026; designed + built on Opus).** This is **mid-session jank, not startup** — Release B already fixed opening. `applyPersonal` and `applyHousehold` used to render *while* merging: a single apply cycle called `renderRecipes()` **twice** (once after the recipe-book merge, again after the meal-plan merge, in **both** functions), plus `renderTrips()`/`renderBabyView()` mid-merge, then an unconditional `renderList()` + `renderHomeScreen()`/`renderTrainView()` at the end. **Now:** every merge block only sets a flag on a local `_rDirty` map, and one new top-level helper **`flushSyncRenders(dirty)`** runs once at the very end of the cycle — merge everything first, then redraw each affected + visible section exactly once. **The per-block guards are preserved, not dropped** (`currentSection === "recipes" && !_recipeEditing`, `currentSection === "trips" && !_tripEditing`, `currentSection === "baby"`) — they moved into the helper and are evaluated there. That is the same decision made once, not a weakening: `currentSection`/`_recipeEditing`/`_tripEditing` cannot change mid apply cycle, since the whole merge is synchronous. **Confirmed decision ① (batch scope):** dirty flags per render target, checked once at the end — *plus* `renderList()` gains a `currentSection === "lists"` guard. It previously rebuilt the entire list DOM on **every** sync apply even while Lists was hidden behind Home/Baby/Trips, which was the single biggest repeated cost in the cycle; `switchSection()` already re-renders the list on entry (the `section === "lists"` branch calls `renderList()`), so skipping it while hidden cannot leave a stale view. Cathal explicitly declined the third option (diff the data and skip the redraw when nothing actually changed) — that needs new comparison code *inside* the merge logic, which is the risky part of the app. So a dirty flag still means "this channel was present in the payload", exactly as the old inline render calls did; the win is 1-per-target-per-cycle, never fewer. **Confirmed decision ② (try/catch granularity):** whole-function on both — `applyPersonal` now has the `try { ... } catch (err) { console.warn(...) } finally { syncPaused = false; }` shape `applyHousehold` already had (the asymmetry was noted in the analysis but never fixed; a throw in any merge block used to leave `syncPaused` stuck **true** and skip every render below it) — **plus** each batched render is individually wrapped by an inner `safeRender()`, so one failing view can no longer block the others from redrawing. Cathal declined per-channel try/catch around every merge block: genuinely safer in principle, but a large edit spread across the sync/merge code in the same release as the render change. **Confirmed decision ③ (`syncPaused` parity):** deliberately **left alone**. `applyPersonal` still never sets `syncPaused = true` on entry (it only clears it on exit, now from the `finally`) while `applyHousehold` does — a real inconsistency, but changing *when* outgoing sync is paused alters sync timing, and Cathal chose not to mix that into a rendering release. Backlogged below. **Ordering preserved exactly:** `updateListTabDots()` still runs **after** `renderList()` (it attaches dots to the list tab bar that `renderList` rebuilds) and stays unconditional, via the helper's `dots` flag; `applyHousehold` passes **no** `train` flag, because the Train view is fed by the personal channel only and must not start redrawing on household syncs. `_newFromOther` was also hoisted out of `applyHousehold`'s `try` block so the partner-added toast underneath is still safe if a merge throws. No data-model, export, dosing, or sync-protocol changes — no channel, merge result, tombstone or payload field is touched, only *when* the resulting redraws happen. `node tests/run.js`: 21/21 files green (**no test file changed** — the existing merge suite drives `applyPersonal` and pins that the merges still produce identical data; the render batching itself is DOM/section-visibility timing, which the `file://` harness doesn't cover, per the analysis's own claim-8 verdict). Verifier: SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v374:** **Release C of the startup-freeze fix plan (`HEARTH-freeze-analysis.md`, claim 3) — `fetchWithTimeout` helper, build-ready, no design call needed.** Zero requests in the sync/auth stack had any timeout wiring — a hung request on a bad connection survived indefinitely behind whatever watchdog called it. New shared helper `fetchWithTimeout(url, options, timeoutMs)` (default 8000ms) wraps `fetch` with an `AbortController`, clearing the timer on either resolve or reject. Applied at exactly the 12 bare `fetch()` call sites the analysis flagged: token refresh, `firebaseSignIn`, `firebaseSignUp`, `doCreateAccount`'s two `auth.json` calls, its Firebase-mode `auth.json` PUT, `doLogin`'s `auth.json` GET, the household-code fetch inside `startSync`, `pushPersonal`, `fetchPersonal`, `pushHousehold`, `fetchHousehold`. (The analysis's 13th anchor — `verifySession`'s old `auth.json` probe — no longer exists; it was deleted in v373's Release B rewrite, so this lands at 12 sites, not 13.) Purely mechanical: no call site's success/error branching changed — an abort now just rejects like any other network failure and falls into the existing `.catch()`. Deliberately left untouched: sports API, backup list/restore, household join/leave, and the generic list-import fetch — outside the freeze investigation's sync/auth scope. No data-model, export, or sync-protocol changes. `node tests/run.js`: 21/21 files green; verifier SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v373:** **Release B of the startup-freeze plan (`HEARTH-freeze-analysis.md`) — opening is now instant and never waits on the network (trade-off confirmed by Cathal 31/07/2026 in the PR #138 follow-up: instant open at the cost of briefly running the previous cached build, with the update banner offering anything newer; designed + built on Fable, same session).** Three interlocking changes, deliberately shipped together because each one's safety depends on the others. **(1) Boot splash is non-blocking.** The splash now hides the moment the main script finishes booting — it covers parse+boot only, never a network wait. The whole v312 waiting machinery is deleted: `bootStaleReload`, `bootConfirmedCurrent`, `_bootUpdateInstalling`, `_bootUpdateCheckDone`, `_bootExtended`, `_bootDeadline`, `watchBootInstalling` and the 4s/8s deadline timers are gone. The version check runs entirely in the background: `SW_UPDATED`/`SW_VERSION` messages funnel into a new `showUpdateBanner()`, shown only on a **numerically strictly newer** SW version (the v312 lesson is kept — never banner when the page already is the new build, never act when the page is *fresher* than a not-yet-replaced old SW). **There is no automatic reload at startup any more** — which also closes the v356 documented residual (a stalled `bootStaleReload()` navigation froze every timer including the failsafe), the last known "doesn't open at all" path. **(2) `sw.js` shell strategy: cache-first with background refresh** (was network-first with the v295 3.5s lie-fi cap — that cap still cost 3.5s on every bad-signal open). The cached shell paints immediately; the network fetch still runs on every open and updates the cache; first-ever visit (nothing cached) waits on the network exactly as before; a failed background refresh is swallowed. **Worst-case staleness is unchanged** — the cached copy was always "the last version this phone downloaded while online"; it just paints instantly now instead of after a wait, and the banner offers anything newer. **(3) Session entry is local-only.** `verifySession` now calls `onLoginSuccess()` immediately when stored credentials exist — the v290 6s entry watchdog and the legacy `auth.json` probe are deleted (the probe entered the app on success, failure AND error, so it verified nothing and only added a request to every launch). `onLoginSuccess` renders Home from **local data at once** (`_homeAwaitingFetch` stays false at boot; the 4s blank-guard timer is gone) and sequences only the **sync kick-off** behind the background `getAuthToken` refresh — new local `startSync()` guarded by `_syncStarted`, with the 6s watchdog moved there so a hung refresh caps how late the first sync starts, never the UI. **The token-race trap this had to close:** `fetchPersonal` sends the auth token on the URL, so kicking sync off before the refresh completes would 401 and wrongly raise the stale-data/"re-login" banner on every cold open — that is why entry and sync-start are decoupled rather than both made immediate. **(4)** The v356 early-failsafe ceiling drops 12s → 8s (it only needs to outlast parse+boot on a slow phone now, not an update wait). **Mixed-state rollout note:** the first open after merging still goes through the OLD service worker's network-first path once; the new behaviour starts from the following open — expected, nothing to do. No data-model, export, dosing, or sync-protocol changes (channels and merges untouched — only *when* the first fetch starts moved). **Tests changed (declared):** `tests/cases/10-boot-splash-failsafe.js` extended 8 → 11 assertions — pins that the splash hides with no SW confirmation at all, and that `bootStaleReload` and `_bootDeadline` no longer exist (if either reappears, the frozen-open class is back). `node tests/run.js`: 21/21 files green; verifier SAFE TO COMMIT.
- **v372:** **Release A of the startup-freeze fix plan (`HEARTH-freeze-analysis.md`, from PR #138 — build-ready for plain Sonnet, no design call needed).** Four small, independent fixes, none changing designed behaviour: **(1) Debug overlay gated to test builds.** The "temporary" console-error overlay (~13993–14029, HTML markup ~14033–14041) was live in production for every real user — overrides `console.error`/`console.warn`, grows an unbounded `errors[]`, and rebuilds the whole log's `innerHTML` on every entry (O(n²) DOM work, worst under repeated sync failures — exactly the bad-network case this analysis is about). Its IIFE is now wrapped `if (_isTestBuild) (function() {...})()` — it still runs on raw.githack test-mode links (where Cathal needs to see errors before merging) but never in production. The HTML markup (`#dbgBox`/`#dbgBadge`) is left in place, harmlessly inert, since it's `display:none` until the gated JS shows it. **(2) `_bootUpdateInstalling` now resets.** It was set true on `updatefound`/an already-installing worker but never reset, so a completed-or-failed install could leave it stuck true — pointlessly extending the boot splash by 4s (the `bootDeadlineHit` extension branch) and blocking the fast same-version confirm. New `watchBootInstalling(worker)` helper attaches a `statechange` listener to the installing worker and clears the flag once it reaches `activated` or `redundant`. **(3) EventSource reconnect timers now guarded.** `startPersonalListening`/`startListening`'s `onerror` handlers scheduled a `setTimeout(...,5000)` reconnect with no guard against multiple pending timers — new `_personalReconnectPending`/`_householdReconnectPending` flags (set true when a reconnect is scheduled, cleared when it fires) stop reconnect attempts from stacking when several failures land close together. **(4) Wrong error message fixed.** `onLoginSuccess`'s household-code fetch catch handler (~8862) logged a copy-pasted `"SW registration failed:"` — unrelated to what actually failed. Now logs `"Household code fetch failed:"`. **Deliberately not touched:** the boot gate's 4s/8s wait, the SW's 3.5s network-first shell fetch, and `verifySession`'s 6s watchdog are all designed behaviour (v312/v295/v290) — Release B, which needs Cathal's trade-off call, covers those.
