No data-model, export, or sync-protocol changes. `node tests/run.js`: 21/21 files green (no new test file — all four fixes are startup/SW/timer wiring not covered by the `file://` harness per the analysis's own claim-8 verdict; the existing suite confirms no regression).
- **v371:** **Baby growth & milestones per-entry sync merge hardening (backlog item, design confirmed on Fable 19/07/2026, built build-ready on plain Sonnet per the 12/07 handoff policy).** Growth and milestones used to merge by simple "remote wins on same key" (`mergeByKey`) with no deletion tombstones — a deleted growth entry or milestone could resurrect from a partner's stale copy on the next sync, and a same-key concurrent edit resolved by push order rather than recency. Now mirrors the existing medicine pattern (v329): `mergeBabyData`'s old `mergeMedicine` is generalised into a shared `mergeIdKeyed(a, b, tombs)` (medicine + milestones — id-keyed, `updated` stamp falling back to `id` so pre-migration entries merge correctly with no migration) plus a new `mergeDateKeyed(a, b, tombs)` for growth (date-keyed — the date is the merge key, so recency falls back to `0`, never to the key itself). Both tombstone-filtered with the medicine rule (`tombTs >= entryTs` → drop). New `updated` stamps at every write site: growth's log/overwrite handler and its full-record inline edit, milestone add and its date-edit handler. New tombstones `fl4_tomb_growth` (keyed by date) and `fl4_tomb_ms` (keyed by id), written at each delete site via the existing `addTomb`/`getTombs` helpers. **The date-change hazard (growth only, since date IS the merge key):** the full-record inline edit can change a record's date — when it does, the OLD date is now also tombstoned (`addTomb("growth", oldDate)`), or a partner's copy under the old date would resurrect as a duplicate; milestones don't have this hazard (keyed by `id`, which never changes). **Channel payloads mirror `med_deleted` exactly:** new `growth_deleted`/`ms_deleted` fields on both `pushPersonal`/`pushHousehold`, merged+purged-before-baby-merge at both `applyPersonal`/`applyHousehold` sites, `mergeBabyData`'s signature grows to `(local, remote, medTombs, growthTombs, msTombs)` at both call sites. **Backup restore** (`importBackupData`'s baby block) stays additive-only union but now filters restored growth (by date) and milestones (by id) through the LOCAL tombstones, mirroring the existing medicine filter — a dose/entry delete is a correction of safety/diary data and must stick within the 90d tombstone window on a restore too. `countAllTombs` needed no change (already counts by the generic `fl4_tomb_` prefix). No data-model, export, or dosing changes — `updated` is a plain field riding inside the existing `bd.growth`/`bd.milestones` arrays, which already sync/export/back up wholesale via `getBD()`; `fl4_tomb_*` stays deliberately un-exported, matching every other tombstone store. **Mixed-version note:** a pre-v371 partner device ignores the new tombstone fields and `updated` stamps on these two collections, so it can briefly resurrect/lose an entry until it upgrades — same self-healing rollout class as v329/v333. **Tests:** `tests/cases/04-merge-baby.js` extended (9 → 15 assertions) — pins the new `mergeIdKeyed`/`mergeDateKeyed` newest-wins + tombstone + re-log-after-delete behaviour for both growth and milestones, alongside the existing medicine coverage; `tests/cases/11-growth-date-edit.js` extended (8 → 12 assertions) — pins the date-change hazard (old date tombstoned, new date not, moved record stamped) and that a plain delete tombstones the deleted date; `tests/cases/07-backup-roundtrip.js` extended (12 → 14 assertions) — pins that a local growth/milestone tombstone blocks a backup restore from resurrecting that entry; new `tests/cases/21-baby-milestone-sync.js` (7 assertions) — drives the real milestone UI (add/date-edit/delete) end to end, pinning the `updated` stamp on add and date-edit, that a same-id date-edit writes NO tombstone, and that delete does. `node tests/run.js`: 21/21 files green.

- **v370:** **Fix: re-adding a recipe to the grocery list at a different serving size didn't refresh the Amount field it wrote itself (found 29/07/2026 while probing v366, backlogged as out of that version's scope).** `addRecipeToGroceries`'s existing-item branch snapshots `_recipePrev` (used both for the `ownsAmt` field-ownership check and for Clear/revert) the first time an item is re-tagged — but that snapshot was only ever taken there, from whatever amount was already on the item. For a recipe-created item, that "already on the item" value was the recipe's own first-added amount, so the snapshot recorded it as if Cathal had typed it (`_recipePrev.amount` non-empty) instead of "" — `ownsAmt` came out false forever, and a later re-add at new servings correctly recomputed the scaled amount but could never write it to the field, falling back into the note instead. **Fix:** the NEW-item branch now also sets `_recipePrev = { done:false, catId, recipe:null, notes:"", amount:"" }` at creation time, marking the field as recipe-owned from the start — so a later re-add sees the correct (empty) snapshot, `ownsAmt` is true, and the Amount field refreshes to the new servings. No data-model, export, or sync changes — `_recipePrev` is existing device-local bookkeeping, unchanged in shape; the Clear-path `_recipeAdded` branch never reads `_recipePrev` for a still-new item, so this doesn't touch revert behaviour. **Tests:** `tests/cases/16-grocery-name-cleanup.js` extended (82 → 84 assertions) — adds a recipe at 1× servings (new item), re-adds the same recipe at 2× servings, and pins that the Amount field updates to the doubled value (not stuck at 1×) with no duplicate item created. `node tests/run.js`: 20/20 files green.
- **v369:** **Fix: Track/Log entries reappearing after delete (Cathal's report 30/07/2026).** `action_log` (the "Log anything" list plus the cardio sub-log, both stored under `fl4_action_log`) and `food_log` synced via `pushPersonal`/`applyPersonal` using a plain ID-keyed union merge — unlike every other synced store (lists, notes, medicine, trips), neither carried a **deletion tombstone**. Deleting an entry removed it locally and pushed the shorter array, but a stale remote snapshot arriving afterwards (the live Firebase listener firing on another device's in-flight write, or an explicit `fetchPersonal()` pull racing the delete) would unconditionally re-add it — there was nothing marking that id as deliberately deleted. **Fix, mirroring the existing `fl4_tomb_*`/`getTombs`/`addTomb`/`mergeTombs`/`purgeTombs` pattern:** `addTomb("action_log", id)` / `addTomb("food_log", id)` recorded at all three delete sites (`renderLogView`'s `.log-del-btn`, `renderCardioLog`'s cardio delete, `renderFoodView`'s `.food-del-btn`); `pushPersonal()` now sends `action_log_deleted`/`food_log_deleted` (purged tombstones) alongside the arrays; `applyPersonal()` merges incoming tombstones into new `fl4_tomb_action_log`/`fl4_tomb_food_log` stores and filters any tombstoned id out of the merged array before writing it back. **Deliberately left untouched:** `importBackupData()` (explicit file/cloud-backup restore) — its additive-only union there is by design, same precedent as list items (but not medicine/history) being allowed to resurrect on an explicit restore. No data-model/export changes — these tombstones are a new local+synced store, not a booking/recipe field. `node tests/run.js`: 20/20 files green (no new test file — bug is in the personal-sync merge path, which isn't yet covered by the harness's household-sync-style fixtures; existing suite confirms no regression).
- **v368:** **"Add to grocery list" is now a review screen — pick the ingredients, rename one before it goes, and both choices stick (Cathal's ask 30/07/2026; designed + built on Opus, 3 decisions confirmed via AskUserQuestion).** The button used to push every ingredient straight onto the list; now it opens a full-screen review step (`_recipeView === "grocerypick"`, `openGroceryPicker` → `renderGroceryPick` → `applyGroceryPick`) listing every real ingredient with a tick box, an editable **shopping name** pre-filled with the name that would actually land on the list, and a preview of the amount that rides with it. **Confirmed decisions:** ① a rename saves a **separate shopping name**, it does NOT rewrite the recipe's own line — the recipe keeps its full wording (amount, brackets, prep clause) so a rename can never lose detail, and the recipe view shows the preference instead ("· bought as “Passata”"); ② an unticked ingredient is **remembered per recipe** (a staple you never shop for stays unticked on every later add, re-tickable any time), rather than being a one-off; ③ full screen, not a sheet — a 15-ingredient recipe should not scroll inside a small box on a phone. **Two new optional fields on recipe ingredients: `groceryName` and `noBuy`.** They ride inside `recipe.ingredients[]` and so sync/export with the existing `fl4_recipebook` whole-record newest-wins merge — **no new sync, export or data-model plumbing.** `noBuy` is honoured on **every** path into the grocery list (single recipe, week planner, roll-over banner) because they all funnel through `addRecipeToGroceries`; the review screen is simply the only place the fields are set, so there is exactly one code path onto the list. **Three new top-level helpers:** `recipeGroceryName(ing, sp)` (override wins, else the derived name — used by the push, the saved-recipe chip and the screen), `carryGroceryPrefs(next, prev)`, and `groceryAmtLabel(ing, m, sp)` (the scaled amount label lifted out of `addRecipeToGroceries`' local `amtLabel`/`ascii`, so the screen's preview and the push can never disagree). **The field-loss traps this had to close** — all the same "object rebuilt from a hand-typed field list" class as the babySex incident: `rescueIngredientMeasure` re-parses a line into a **fresh** object (both fields now copied across), and both the **recipe editor save** and **`applyTidyLines`** rebuild every ingredient from text (both now run the new lines through `carryGroceryPrefs`, matched on `groceryNameKey` so a re-worded line keeps its preference). **Safety/UI details:** nothing on the screen re-renders on a tick or a keystroke (the button label is updated in place by `updateGrocPickAdd`) because a re-render steals focus from a name box mid-word — the v366 tidy-screen lesson; the delegated `input`/`click` listeners are wired **once** behind `_grocPickWired` since `#recipesContent` is permanent; `_recipeEditing = true` while the screen is open so a sync landing can't re-render half-typed names away; tick boxes sit in 44px padded wrappers; the Add button disables itself when nothing is ticked (so unticking everything can never flag a whole recipe as never-buy) and `applyGroceryPick` guards again; typing the derived name back in **clears** the override rather than freezing today's parser output; and the recipe's `updated` stamp is recorded when the screen opens — since the screen holds ingredient **indexes**, an edit landing underneath it is refused with a toast and the screen rebuilt, rather than writing a preference onto the wrong ingredient (the stale-copy-wins class, same guard as the tidy pass). **Tests:** new `tests/cases/20-grocery-ingredient-pick.js` (58 assertions) — the three helpers unit-level (including `carryGroceryPrefs` matching a re-worded line and never touching headers), the rescue carry, the push honouring both fields incl. the saved-recipe chip and an all-staples recipe adding nothing, and the real screen end to end: header rows excluded but shown for context, amount previewed, untick + rename → only the ticked items land under the new name, the recipe's own wording untouched, the preferences reapplied on the **next** open, re-ticking clearing `noBuy`, typing the default back clearing the override, the untick-all refusal, the stale-recipe refusal, and the preferences surviving a recipe-editor save. `node tests/run.js`: 20/20 files green.
- **v367:** **Run the tidy pass at CSV import time (the v366 council's recorded dissent, un-parked; build-ready for plain Sonnet, built same).** v366's "🧹 Tidy" tool only fixed the ~60 recipes already in the book, so every recipe imported afterwards via `recipesFromCSV` still needed tidying all over again — the whole point of the dissent was that this made the one-off tool permanent furniture. Fix, exactly as scoped in the backlog entry: `recipesFromCSV` now runs each freshly-parsed ingredient through the existing `tidyIngredient()`, keeping only confident (`ok`) rewrites — a line it can't confidently rewrite imports exactly as it does today, same as a flagged line on the tidy screen; the import preview is not a place to ask questions. `recipesFromCSV` gained a `tidied` count alongside `recipes`/`skipped` (including the empty-input early-return path), and the CSV import preview text now reads **"N recipes found · M ingredient lines tidied, X row(s) skipped"**. Confirmed against the backlog's own worked example: `"2 15-ounce cans black beans, rinsed / 425 g"` imports directly as `Black beans` / amount `2 cans` / note `(15-ounce, 425 g, rinsed)`, no manual tidy pass needed. Reuses the same safety model as v366 (losslessness + reparse-round-trip guard) untouched — a bad import is no worse than before. No sync/export/data-model changes: recipes still ride `fl4_recipebook` whole-record. **Tests:** new `tests/cases/19-csv-import-tidy.js` (13 assertions) — the sized-container+comma-clause example tidied on import, a flagged/unparseable line importing untouched, a section header surviving untouched, a glued-unit line ("200g") still getting tidied, and the empty-input edge case. `node tests/run.js`: 19/19 files green.
- **v366:** **"Tidy ingredients" — a one-off maintenance pass over the whole recipe book (Cathal's call 29/07/2026, designed + built on Opus, 4 decisions confirmed via AskUserQuestion).** He has ~60 CSV-imported recipes and asked whether a one-off cleanup made more sense than yet another parser rule. **It did, and it needed no new data field:** since v363 anything in brackets is stripped out of the grocery name and carried into the item's note, so the canonical shape `"<amount> <unit> <Name> (<detail>)"` already round-trips through the editor and produces exactly the item he asked for — `Black beans` / amount `2 cans` / note `(15-ounce, 425 g, rinsed)`. **The key architectural difference from v363/v365: everything before this was DOWNSTREAM rescue** (clean a name on its way to the grocery list, never touch storage); **this rewrites the stored ingredient**, so the recipe VIEW reads properly too and no rescue is needed on the way out. **Confirmed decisions:** ① one list, tick per recipe, edit any line inline, one Apply (not a 60-step wizard, not blind-apply); ② rewrite only what it is confident about, flag the rest as "needs your eye" and leave it byte-identical; ③ a suspicious Serves is FLAGGED with a number box, never guessed (a wrong guess silently rescales every amount); ④ a second step offers to unify names that mean the same product. **Safety model — the bar is losslessness, not cleverness:** new `tidyIsLossless(before, after)` requires every meaningful word of the original to reappear somewhere in the rewrite (compared through `groceryNameKey`, with glued `200g`→`200 g` normalised so a fix doesn't read as a loss; presence not count, so v363's deliberate duplicate-word drop still passes), AND the rewrite must reparse to the same amount/unit/name, AND the resulting name must contain no digit. Fail any one → flagged, original untouched. **New parsing this needed** (both shapes the leading-amount parser can never reach, because the number where it expects a product name is a container size): ⓐ **sized container** — `2 15-ounce cans black beans` → 2 × `can`, `15-ounce` to the bracket (`TIDY_CONTAINERS`/`TIDY_SIZE_UNITS`); ⓑ **dual-unit tail** — `… / 425 g`, the metric equivalent recipe sites print alongside imperial, to the bracket; ⓒ the **comma clause taken WHOLE** before `splitGroceryName` sees it (its trailing-prep rule keeps only the last word, so `onion, peeled and diced` was losing "peeled and" out of the middle — exactly the kind of quiet loss the pass must never make). **Deliberately left alone:** headers, and `"Juice of 1 lemon"` lines (v363's rescue already produces the right grocery item and any rewrite either duplicates the phrase or drops what you actually do with the lemon). **Step two (name merge):** `tidyNameGroups` groups only by (a) names that already fold to the same `groceryNameKey` (plural/spelling) and (b) a small `TIDY_SYNONYMS` UK/US table — **never by word overlap**, because `Coriander` (ground seed) and `Fresh coriander` (the herb) are genuinely different products, which v363's own changelog cites as live evidence. A synonym-formed group is labelled "spelling variant — check these really are the same thing" and nothing is preselected; the default is leave-as-they-are. **Two pre-existing bugs fixed because this build tripped over them:** **(1)** a `<b>Base</b>`/`Sauce:` header was serialized into the editor textarea as a bare word, so **saving any recipe with a section header silently demoted it to an ingredient** — new shared `ingredientToLine()` re-emits it as `<b>Base</b>`; **(2)** a typed fraction decayed to `1.6666666666666665 tbsp` in the editor — new `fmtIngredientAmount()` re-emits a common fraction, but only when it reparses to the same number. `openRecipeEditor` now uses `ingredientToLine` instead of its own inline map (single source of truth; `rescueIngredientMeasure` deliberately keeps its own, since it rebuilds a line in order to RE-parse it). **Two supporting changes:** `splitGroceryName` now strips brackets out of `body` up front (its trailing prep rule was matching THROUGH a closing bracket and returning `"rinsed)"` — harmless before, wrong the moment tidy started writing brackets on purpose; `s` stays whole for rule ①'s detail); and `amtDetail`'s bracket note no longer requires a digit inside the bracket, so `Garlic (minced)` still reaches the grocery note — without that, tidying would have quietly lost the prep wording that used to arrive as a trailing comma clause. **UI:** new `🧹 Tidy` button on the recipe list header, new `_recipeView === "tidy"` branch, `renderRecipeTidy` (collapsed per-recipe cards — only expanded recipes build inputs, so 60 recipes don't put 600 inputs in the DOM) → `applyTidyLines` → `renderRecipeTidyNames` → `applyTidyNames`. The screen sets `_recipeEditing = true` (same guard as the editor) so a sync landing mid-review can't re-render it away, edits are captured on `input` so expanding another card never loses typing, an export-a-backup-first button sits above the fold, and `applyTidyLines` refuses to write a recipe whose rewrite produced FEWER ingredient lines than it started with. Recipes ride the existing `fl4_recipebook` whole-record newest-wins merge — **no sync, export, or data-model plumbing changes.** **Tests:** new `tests/cases/18-tidy-ingredients.js` (51 assertions) — the serializer round-trips (header, `Label:`, fraction), all seven tidy shapes, the must-not-touch cases (header, juice-of, leading modifier, range, compound phrasing), the losslessness guard from both sides incl. a flagged line being byte-identical to its original, the `splitGroceryName` stray-paren fix, serves flagging in all three directions, and a full-screen e2e (tick/untick, inline edit surviving a re-render, typed serves applied, unticked recipe untouched, the name-merge pick applied across recipes, and the resulting grocery push). `node tests/run.js`: 18/18 files green. **Second pass, same version/PR (29/07/2026, before merge — Cathal tested PR #133 on his phone and asked for a council review of the whole Recipes section first).** **(a) Free-text merge option:** his screenshot showed the real book offering `scoop vanilla protein powder` vs `scoops Vanilla Protein Powder` — neither stored spelling is the one you want, so each merge group gained a text box. Typing selects it (no second tap); **nothing on that screen re-renders on a tap or keystroke** — the radios hold their own state and `updateTidyNamesApply()` updates the Merge button in place, because re-rendering stole focus from the box mid-word. **(b) Council review — verdict SHIP after four fixes, three built here.** ① **Stale-snapshot clobber (sync architect, high, the serious one):** proposals were built from ONE `getRecipeBook()` read, but reviewing 60 recipes takes minutes and the screen suppresses its own re-render (`_recipeEditing = true`), so a partner's edit landing mid-review would be silently overwritten by a rewrite of the pre-edit text — the stale-copy-wins class this project keeps getting bitten by. Each proposal now records the recipe's `updated` stamp and `applyTidyLines` skips anything that moved, reporting `"N changed on another device, left alone"`. ② **Event-listener leak (future maintainer, high):** `renderRecipeTidy` attached its `input` handler to `#recipesContent` on every render, and that element is **permanent** (only its `innerHTML` is replaced) — so listeners stacked with every expand/collapse/tick. Idempotent, so nothing visibly broke, which is exactly why it would have survived to confuse someone. Now wired once behind `_tidyInputWired`, reading `_tidyState` rather than a render-time closure. ③ **Touch targets + a double-control label (accessibility, high):** the free-text row was a `<label>` wrapping BOTH a radio and a text input (ambiguous for screen readers, and a tap meant for the box could hit the radio) — now a plain `div`; variant rows and the recipe header are min 44px, the accept checkbox got a padded hit box that can't be confused with the expand-row tap, and the new controls carry `aria-label`s. ④ **Open, needs Cathal's call:** the merge screen lists every case-only pair (`sea salt`/`Sea salt`, `soya sauce`/`Soya sauce`) even though `groceryNameKey` already folds case so merging them changes nothing on the shopping list — four of the five cards in his screenshot were busywork burying the one that mattered. Proposed: a one-tap "use the most common spelling everywhere" button for case-only groups. **Recorded dissent (Devil's Advocate, not absorbed):** this is a one-off tool that lives forever in a single-file app, and `tidyIngredient` belongs in the **CSV import path** so recipes imported after this don't need tidying again — see the backlog item below. He also objected that the header-corruption fix (a real silent data-loss bug) rode along inside a 500-line feature PR instead of getting its own version; upheld in substance, called out explicitly in the PR body rather than buried. Test file 51 → 57 assertions (free-text selection, blank-input-is-not-a-pick, a typed name replacing every variant, and the mid-review partner-edit case). `node tests/run.js`: 18/18 green. **Third pass, same version/PR (29/07/2026) — the first real-data numbers, and they were the useful part.** Cathal ran the tool over his actual book: **"41 recipes to look at · 17 lines tidied · 84 need your eye"**, with cards like "Apple pie — no changes · 4 needs your eye". Council item ④ built as agreed, plus two findings from that screenshot. **(a) Bulk spelling button:** `tidyPickCommon` — "Use the most common spelling everywhere (N)", shown when more than one non-synonym group exists; sets each `!viaSynonym` group's pick to `variants[0]` (already sorted count-desc, so it IS the most common). **Deliberately never touches the synonym groups** — those are the real judgement calls and the whole reason the screen exists. **(b) Full-recipe context (Cathal: "sometimes difficult to make a change without the context of the full recipe"):** the expanded card now lists **every** ingredient line, not just changed/flagged ones (untouched ones styled quieter but still editable), and renders the recipe's `method` below in a scrollable block — a flagged line in isolation tells you nothing about whether it's the tin or the topping. `tidyRecipeProposal` carries a new `method` field for this. **(c) Every flagged line now explains itself** — each `ok = false` path sets a plain-English `why` ("a number is still stuck in the name", "some of the wording would have been lost", "the tidied line wouldn't read back the same", "nothing left to buy once the measure was taken out"). This was for Cathal, and it doubled as the diagnostic that found (d). **(d) Root-causing the 84.** A probe over 40 realistic UK/scraped recipe lines flagged only 3 — so the 84 is NOT a blanket failure (the most likely explanation is that he had already applied the pass in an earlier test session, leaving the residue; his card text "no changes · 4 needs your eye" fits that exactly). All three probe failures were real and are now fixed: **the `"1 x 400g tin chopped tomatoes"` multiplier form** (new rule ⓐ0 — parseIngredients takes the leading "1" and stops dead on the "x"; pack size goes to the bracket, container becomes the unit, and a bare `x` is now a skipped noise token in `tidyIsLossless`'s bag since amount × pack size carries the multiplication); **`"zest and juice of 1 lime"`** (rule ① in `splitGroceryName` extended to accept the two-word form — improves the grocery path too); and **five stranded count words** — `scoop`, `rasher`, `sprig`, `bunch`, `stick` added to all THREE unit tables that must stay in step (`parseIngredients`' `unitMap`, `GROCERY_NAME_UNITS`, `scaleIngredient`'s `countUnits`), which directly fixes the `"2 scoops vanilla protein powder"` case from his own screenshot. Same probe after the fixes: **0 of 40 flagged.** **Fourth pass, same version/PR (29/07/2026) — the actual root cause, found from a screenshot of one expanded recipe.** "Need your eye" stayed high (45 recipes · 29 tidied · **77** flagged), so the `why` reasons earned their keep immediately: the expanded Vegan Spag Bol showed **`2 x 15ml tablespoons + 2 x 15ml tablespoons olive oil`** and **`1 tsp + 2 teaspoons Maldon sea salt flakes or ½ tsp …`**, both "a number is still stuck in the name". Cathal's recipes come from a source that **glosses every spoon measure with its metric size** — and a tablespoon IS 15ml, so the gloss restates the unit while stopping the parser dead (the leading amount parses, `x` isn't a unit, everything after it strands in the name). Because the source writes it on every spoon line, one shape accounted for a large share of all 77. **Three changes:** **(a)** new top-level `normaliseIngredientLine(line)` collapses `N x <size><unit> <spoon-word>` → `N <spoon-word>`, called inside `tidyIngredient` on the reconstructed line before re-parsing — which also lets the **compound** rule finally read `2 x 15ml tbsp + 2 x 15ml tbsp` and sum it to 4 tbsp. **(b)** New rule ⓒ2: a trailing **`or <alternative>`** moves into the bracket. v365 deliberately dropped "or" from the PARSER because it would have had to *choose* between the two measures — moving it to the bracket chooses nothing, so this doesn't reopen that decision; a number is required in the tail so `"cheddar or gruyere"` and `"salt or pepper"` stay untouched. **(c) `INGREDIENT_UNIT_MAP` hoisted out of `parseIngredients` to top level** so `tidyIsLossless`'s bag can fold a unit word to the parser's own canonical form — without it, rewriting `2 tablespoons` as `2 tbsp` read as a **lost word** and flagged the line. `bag()` also normalises through `normaliseIngredientLine`, so dropping a pure restatement is never counted as a loss. **Fifth pass, same version/PR (29/07/2026) — scraped tick boxes, the single biggest remaining cause.** Flags fell to 66, and Cathal spotted that ~half came from two recipes (one a duplicate) "which have tick boxes which is throwing the parser off". Confirmed from the screenshot: his Vegetarian Chili's stored ingredient names literally begin with the source website's **checkbox glyph** — `"☐ 2 tsp Ground Cumin"`. Because `parseIngredients` only takes an amount from a line that **starts** with a digit, one glyph stranded the entire measure in the name, flagging 16 otherwise-perfect lines in that one recipe. **Fixes:** **(a)** `parseIngredients` now strips a leading tick box/bullet glyph (`☐☑☒■□▢▣○●•◦▪‣✓✔✗✘❑❏` plus `[ ]`/`[x]` forms) as its very first step, before the `~` approx check. **Bare `-` and `*` are deliberately excluded** — a leading asterisk is a footnote marker (`***see note`), not a bullet. This is ingest noise in the same class as the existing markup strip. **(b) `"c":"cup"`** added to `INGREDIENT_UNIT_MAP` — that recipe writes `1 1/2 C Dry Beans`, and "C" means cup in either case so there is nothing to get wrong. **`"t"`/`"T"` deliberately NOT added**: they mean teaspoon vs tablespoon *by case*, and unit lookups are lowercased, so the two could never be told apart. **(c)** the compound joiner alternation is now `(?:\+|plus|and)` — `"1 cup and 4 tablespoons granulated sugar"` is the same shape as `"+"`, and it stays safe because both sides must still resolve to a recognised unit in the same family (`"2 cloves and 1 onion"` doesn't match, pinned by a test). **Verified on ALREADY-STORED data**, not just fresh parses — the rescue path recovers `{amount:2, unit:"tsp", name:"Ground Cumin"}` from the stored boxed name, and the tick box is dropped from the losslessness bag as punctuation, so these become clean rewrites rather than flags. **Sixth pass, same version/PR (29/07/2026) — scraped strikethrough, and the narrow undo built (council item ⑥, Cathal's go-ahead).** Flags fell to 36. **(a) `"<s>2 tbsp sugar</s>"`** — a struck-out ingredient on the source page arrived with its `<s>` tags intact, and since the line doesn't START with a digit the whole measure stranded in the name. `parseIngredients` now strips any **remaining** markup — placed deliberately **after** the `<b>`/`<strong>` header check (which matches on those tags) and **before** the amount parsing; `tidyIsLossless`'s bag strips markup too, or the stray `s` token read as a lost word. **(b) Narrow undo.** "Export a backup first" was real protection but wildly disproportionate — restoring a whole Hearth backup to walk back a recipe cleanup would also roll back baby data, trips and lists, and the screen was claiming "There's no undo" with a sledgehammer as the only mitigation. New device-local single-slot receipt **`fl4_tidy_undo`** (`TIDY_UNDO_KEY`, 7-day window): `tidySnapshotRecipe` deep-copies each recipe's ingredients + servings **before** the in-place mutation, and both `applyTidyLines` and `applyTidyNames` now write a receipt via `tidySaveUndo`. `applyTidyUndo` restores it, guarded by the **same stamp check as the apply path** — `appliedUpdated` records the stamp the cleanup itself wrote, so a recipe edited since (here or on a partner's phone) keeps its newer version and is reported rather than clobbered. The offer (`tidyUndoCardHtml` + `wireTidyUndoBtn`) renders at the top of the tidy screen in **both** the normal and the "nothing to tidy" states — finishing a cleanup lands you on the latter, which is exactly when you'd want it back. **Deliberately NOT version history:** single slot, device-local, **never synced and never exported** (added to `buildExportPayload`'s NOT-exported list; a receipt riding a sync path would let one phone's undo overwrite another's recipes). Warning copy corrected to say the undo exists but is this-phone-only and a backup is still the safer net. Test file 92 → 104 assertions (the `<s>` rescue + its lossless exemption + that `<b>` headers still parse, and the full undo round trip: no offer before an apply, receipt written, book restored *exactly*, receipt spent so it can't be replayed, and the safety half — a recipe edited after the cleanup keeps its newer version while an untouched one is still restored). **Two shapes left flagged on purpose:** `"3/4 of 1 tin of chopped tomatoes - 300g"` (idiosyncratic hand-written phrasing; a human should rewrite it) and `"2 cloves and 1 onion, chopped"` (two ingredients on one line — not something a parser should split). Test file 83 → 92 assertions. Probe over the exact screenshot lines: **11 of 11 now tidy** (`2 x 15ml tablespoons tomato puree` → `2 tbsp Tomato puree`; the compound → 4 tbsp; the Maldon line → `1 tsp + 2 teaspoons Maldon sea salt flakes (or 1/2 tsp table salt)`). Test file 74 → 83 assertions. Test file 57 → 74 assertions (the multiplier form ×2 + its lossless exemption, the scoop unit and its pluralisation, "zest and juice of", the `why` reasons, all-lines-shown + method-shown context, and the bulk button incl. that it picks the most common rather than the first-seen spelling and leaves synonym groups alone). `node tests/run.js`: 18/18 green.
- **v365:** **Recipe-parser second pass (un-parks the backlog item; design chat done same day on Fable via PR #131, 4 decisions confirmed by Cathal, built build-ready on plain Sonnet per the 12/07 handoff policy).** `parseIngredients` only ever peeled a single leading amount+unit off a line, so two shapes were left stranded in the ingredient NAME — wrong in the recipe VIEW and unable to scale. Scope per the confirmed design: **(a) compound amounts** (`1 3/4 cup + 2 tbsp`) and **(b) ranges** (`2-3 tbsp`) only — **(c) alternatives ("or") explicitly DROPPED**, **(d) multi-unit brackets explicitly SKIPPED** (already mostly handled by v363's `splitGroceryName`). **Compound:** new top-level `sumCompoundAmount` + `COMPOUND_UNIT_FAMILIES` table (tsp/tbsp/cup, ml/l, g/kg — three same-dimension families only; an unconvertible mix like cup+oz falls through unparsed rather than guessing at density) sums both terms into ONE normalized amount+unit inside `parseIngredients`; the original phrasing is kept on a new bookkeeping field **`_compoundNote`** so the grocery note can cite it (base servings only, same pattern as the existing bracket-text note) and the editor re-emits it verbatim on the next edit rather than a flattened decimal. `parseAmount` gained mixed integer+unicode-fraction support (`1 ¾` / `1¾`) — needed for the flagship Greek-yogurt example to parse at all. **Ranges:** new ingredient field **`amountMax`**, recognised in three written forms (`2-3`, `2–3`, `2 to 3`) with a safety guard — the second number must be strictly bigger than the first, so a hyphenated mixed fraction like `1-1/2` is never misread as a 1-to-0.5 range and instead falls through to the pre-v365 unparsed fallback. **Existing data (design decision ②): rescue-at-render, not migration.** New top-level `rescueIngredientMeasure(ing)` reconstructs an ingredient's original text exactly as `openRecipeEditor`'s serializer would and re-parses it with the upgraded parser — recovering the correct amount/amountMax from OLD-format stored recipes (where the leftover compound/range text is sitting untouched in `name`, per Finding 1: the editor textarea is a lazy-migration channel that already existed) without ever rewriting stored data. Called at the top of `renderRecipeDetail`'s ingredient loop and once over `addRecipeToGroceries`'s `ings` array. **Scaling:** `scaleIngredient` refactored — the per-unit formatting logic factored out into `formatScaledAmount` and called twice when `amountMax` is present; the returned unit label follows the TOP of the range for pluralisation (`"1-2 cloves"`, not `"1-2 clove"`). **Grocery push (decision ③):** a range's amount chip shows the scaled range string (`"4-6 tbsp"`); both `eff`-reconstruction branches in `addRecipeToGroceries` now carry `amountMax` through. **Editor round-trip:** `openRecipeEditor`'s serializer re-emits `amountMax` as `"2-3"` and a compound as its original `_compoundNote` phrasing — both verified to reparse back to identical data (the ripple-checklist's round-trip requirement). No sync/export plumbing changes: `amountMax`/`_compoundNote` are plain fields riding inside `recipe.ingredients[]`, which already syncs whole as part of `fl4_recipebook`. **Tests:** new `tests/cases/17-recipe-parser-compound-range.js` (30 assertions) — pins the unicode mixed-fraction parse, both compound examples (ascii and unicode phrasing) summing to the same normalized cup amount, the unconvertible-mix fallback, all three range spellings, the hyphenated-fraction safety guard, rescue-at-render for both old-format shapes (including the case with NO amount extracted at all), rescue's fast-path no-ops (ordinary ingredient / already-has-amountMax / header), `scaleIngredient`'s range scaling and top-of-range pluralisation, the grocery-push range chip and compound note, both editor round-trips, and an end-to-end `renderRecipeDetail` check that an old broken-looking stored ingredient now displays correctly (confirmed against the original bug report's own numbers — 1 3/4 cup + 2 tbsp Greek yogurt scales to exactly 460 g, matching the v363 changelog's cited figure). `node tests/run.js`: 17/17 files green.
- **v364:** **Recipe amount note trimmed to what the rest of the card cannot already say (Cathal's two screenshots 28/07/2026, straight after v363 merged: "remove the 'for {recipe}' at the end of the note as the tag has that info, and if the quantity is recognised and put in the Amount field there is no need to repeat it in the notes"). Both asks are duplication removals, no new parsing.** **(1) The recipe name is gone from the note.** v363 wrote `"200 g for Chickpea, mushroom & fine beans curry"` while the item's own purple recipe chip already said the recipe — said twice, and it blew past the card's 40-char note-snippet truncation. **That suffix was load-bearing, not decorative:** it was how `stripRecipeNote` found the recipe's OWN line again to replace on a re-add or remove on Clear. Replaced by a new per-item bookkeeping field **`_recipeNote`** holding the exact line text, removed by value — so `stripRecipeNote(notes, tag, own)` now filters on `l === own`, with the old `" for <tag>"` suffix kept **only as a legacy fallback** so notes written by the v363 pre-release test link clean themselves up on the next add (pinned by its own test). `addRecipeNote` replaced by **`setRecipeNote(item, tag, detail)`** which mutates in place and keeps `item._recipeNote` in step (empty detail = remove the line, leave a user-typed note untouched). `_recipeNote` is deleted alongside `_recipePrev`/`_recipeAdded` at all three Clear branches and is documented under Data & export as a third deliberate ride-along field. **(2) The amount is no longer repeated.** `amtDetail` gained an `amtInChip` flag: for a NEW item the amount goes in the item's Amount field so the note carries only what that field cannot say (the bracketed measure, a prep instruction, or the `"Juice of 1 lemon"` original line) — often nothing at all, so `Mushrooms 200 g` now has an empty note. **(3) A matched item's EMPTY Amount field is now filled** (third screenshot, same session — the recipe view shows all three amounts recognised in bold, but `Eggs` reached the list with an empty Amount because it pre-existed and took the matched branch). This **narrows the pass-1 decision ④** "never touch a matched item's amount" to **"never OVERWRITE it"**: an empty field gets the recipe's amount (and the note then omits it, per (2)); a field Cathal typed himself always wins and the recipe's amount falls back to the note. `_recipePrev` gained an `amount` key so Clear empties a field the recipe filled, leaving a typed one alone (older snapshots have no `amount` key and are skipped). **Caught by a test, worth remembering:** on a RE-ADD the recipe would otherwise see the amount it wrote itself as one Cathal typed, and start repeating it in the note again — ownership is resolved from the snapshot (`_recipePrev.amount === ""` means the recipe owns the field), not from whether the field is currently empty. **Quick-import twin:** a pasted list carries no amounts, so re-tagging an item that already has a `_recipeNote` now drops that stale line rather than leaving another recipe's note under a new chip. **Tests:** `16-grocery-name-cleanup.js` 74 → 82 assertions (every note assertion rewritten to the new format, plus `_recipeNote` bookkeeping, the "recipe name absent from the note" check, the v363-legacy-note cleanup, empty-field fill vs typed-amount-never-overwritten, the re-add ownership case, and Clear emptying a filled field). `node tests/run.js`: 16/16 green — note, the runner crashed once with "Chrome did not expose a page target within 10s" and passed unchanged on a straight retry; that is a harness flake, not a code failure.
- **v363:** **Recipe → Grocery name cleanup + fuzzy matching (Cathal's report 28/07/2026, screenshot: an item literally named "(1 ¾ cups + 2 tbs) Greek yogurt"; designed + built on Opus, 4 design calls confirmed via AskUserQuestion — ① amount chip stays short + full detail in the item note · ② conservative cleanup (brackets + trailing comma clause, no prep-word stripping) · ③ fuzzy matching key · ④ a matched existing item DOES get the amount appended to its note).** **Root cause:** `parseIngredients` (~4340) only peels a LEADING number+unit off a line, so "460 g (1 ¾ cups + 2 tbs) Greek yogurt" stores `amount:460, unit:"g", name:"(1 ¾ cups + 2 tbs) Greek yogurt"` — and BOTH grocery import paths then stripped only a **trailing** bracket (`/\s*\(.*?\)\s*$/`), so the leading one survived onto the list and no key could ever match a hand-typed "Greek Yoghurt". **Four new top-level helpers** (~3407, right after `headerLabel`, single source of truth for both paths): `cleanGroceryName(raw, light)` — strips brackets **anywhere** + unbalanced leftovers, `x2`/`~2` suffixes, a trailing comma clause ("onion, finely chopped" → "Onion"), a stray leading "of" (the `1 pinch of salt` → `of salt` parser gap), edge punctuation, and sentence-cases a fully-lowercase name (guarded so `iPhone` survives); `groceryNameKey(name)` — the **matching** key only (display names are never rewritten): case/punctuation-insensitive, plural folding (`eggs`=`egg`, `tomatoes`=`tomato`, `berries`=`berry`, `boxes`=`box`, `-ss/-us/-is` left alone), plus a deliberately tiny `GROCERY_SPELLINGS` table (`yoghurt`→`yogurt`, `chilli`→`chili`, `&`→`and`) — **only add pairs that have actually caused a duplicate**; `stripRecipeNote`/`addRecipeNote` — one note line per recipe, format **`"<amount> for <recipe name>"`** (amount FIRST because the card's note snippet truncates at 40 chars and the recipe name is already its own chip), so a second recipe tagging the same item appends and a re-add of the same recipe **replaces** its own line rather than stacking. **`addRecipeToGroceries` (~3485):** names now cleaned at the top (so the reusable `fl4_recipes` chip stores buyable names too — it gets re-imported through the quick-import path), `byName` keyed on `groceryNameKey`, and a new local `amtDetail(ing, amt)` builds the note detail = scaled amount + the bracket text that was stripped out of the name — **the bracket text is verbatim from the recipe so it is only true at base servings and is dropped whenever `m !== 1`**. New items: amount chip + note. Matched existing items: name and amount chip still never touched (typed text), but the amount now rides in the note — previously it was silently discarded. **`_recipePrev` gained a `notes` field** at both tag sites (~3545, ~8711) and the Clear/revert path (~4786) restores it exactly, falling back to `stripRecipeNote` for pre-v363 snapshots that have no `notes` key; "keep added items" deliberately leaves the note in place (the item is still to be bought). **Quick-import twin (~8703)** now calls the same helpers, with `light:true` on non-grocery lists — a trailing comma clause is real text there ("Call bank, ask about fees" must NOT become "Call bank"). **Tests:** new `tests/cases/16-grocery-name-cleanup.js` (33 assertions) — pins every `cleanGroceryName` rule incl. both light-mode carve-outs, the `groceryNameKey` folds, the real `addRecipeToGroceries` flow for new/matched/re-added/scaled cases, the exact note restore through the real `.recipe-clear-btn` dialog, and that headers are still never bought. `node tests/run.js`: 16/16 files green. No sync, export, or data-model plumbing changes (`notes` was already a synced/exported item field). **Second pass, same version/PR (Cathal's follow-up screenshot 28/07/2026, a chickpea curry — v363 had not merged yet, so this extends PR #129 rather than opening a v364; 2 further design calls confirmed: all six visible patterns, and recovered quantities scale when the unit is known).** The general problem: `parseIngredients` only peels a leading `<number> <space> <unit>`, so **every other way a recipe writes a measure is left stranded in the name**. New top-level `splitGroceryName(raw, hasAmount)` (~3441, after `cleanGroceryName`) returns `{name, amount, unit, qty, detail, prep}` and handles four shapes: **①** `"Juice of 1 lemon"` → name `Lemon`, amount 1, and the whole original line as the note (`detail` REPLACES the built-up amount note — "1 Juice of 1 lemon" reads badly); also zest/rind/peel, and `a`/`an`/`the` → 1. **②** a trailing `" - "` clause (spaces required, so `self-rising flour` is safe) — with a number+known unit it becomes a **scalable** amount (`"Garlic - 2 cloves"` → 2 × `clove`), with a number but no usable unit it becomes `qty`, an **unscalable literal** shown at base servings only and dropped when scaling (`"Ginger - ½ thumb size"`), and with no number at all it becomes `prep`, a note fragment (`"Fresh coriander - finely chopped"`). **③** a number glued to its unit (`"200g mushrooms"` — the parser's own regex demands a space). **④** a container word stranded at the front when the amount DID parse (`"1 tin chopped tomatoes"` → amount 1, unit `tin`, name `Chopped tomatoes`). Anything recovered as amount+unit is fed back through `scaleIngredient` via a synthetic `eff` ingredient, so it doubles with the recipe; `tin`/`tins` added to BOTH `parseIngredients`' `unitMap` and `scaleIngredient`'s `countUnits` (so new imports parse it directly, and it pluralises to "2 tins"). A comma clause, previously just discarded by `cleanGroceryName`, is now kept as `prep` too. Quick-import shares the splitter on Grocery only (no amount chip — a pasted list has no servings). **Deliberately NOT done:** the parked "recipe-parser second pass" is still parked — compound amounts (`1 ¾ cups + 2 tbs`), ranges and "or"-alternatives remain unparsed; this pass is all downstream rescue, which is why it fixes Cathal's ALREADY-IMPORTED recipes with no re-import. **Live evidence the conservative no-prep-stripping call from pass 1 was right:** the same curry put both `Coriander` (1 tsp, ground, Pantry) and `Fresh coriander` (Produce) on the list — correctly separate products. **Third pass, still same version/PR (28/07/2026):** malformed CSV-ingested names — `"Chilli deseeded chili"` (Cathal: "it's like that in the recipe… it means 1 Chili with a note to deseed it"). Two new **TRAILING-only** rules in `splitGroceryName` ⑤, plus a `GROCERY_PREP_WORDS` list: a last word that folds to the same `groceryNameKey` as the FIRST word is ingest noise and is dropped (`chili` ≡ `Chilli`), and a trailing prep word (optionally with an adverb — `finely chopped`) becomes `prep`. **The trailing-only asymmetry is the entire safety argument and must not be relaxed:** a modifier that LEADS is part of the product (`chopped tomatoes`, `condensed milk`, `smoked salmon`, `grated cheese` — all pinned in the tests as must-survive), one that trails is an instruction. Result: `Chilli` + note `deseeded`. **Deliberately NOT done:** the quantity 1 Cathal mentioned is **not** invented — nothing in the source line states it, and any "countable items default to 1" rule would stamp 1 on flour and salt; editing the recipe line to `1 chilli, deseeded` already parses correctly. Test file grew to 74 assertions (unit-level for all four shapes plus their carve-outs, a six-ingredient end-to-end push, and a scaled re-run pinning "4 cloves"/"400 g"/"2 tins" and the dropped thumb-size).
- **v362:** **Meal-plan rotation + weekly roll-over — the genuinely-new half of the v317 week-planner backlog item, built from the backlog entry alone on plain Sonnet (both design calls already confirmed by Cathal 27/07/2026: ① keep the shipped rolling day-key window — no ISO-week re-key, no plan history; ② prompt-first roll-over — banner, nothing mutates shared data without a tap).** Two new fields on recipebook records: `inRotation` (bool, set via a new checkbox in the recipe editor, `reInRotation`) and `lastCooked` (ts, stamped by a new "✓ Mark cooked" button in the meal-plan day sheet, `openMealPlanDaySheet` ~3843 — this also bumps `recipe.updated` so it actually syncs, meaning marking cooked counts as a recipe edit and a concurrent partner edit to the same recipe resolves newest-wins-loses-one-side, the accepted v242 merge class). Bulk onboarding: select-mode in the recipe list gained a second bulk action alongside "Set category" — `rcpSetRot` → `applyBulkInRotation(ids)` (~3911, next to `applyBulkCategory`), add-only by design (removing a recipe from rotation is a deliberate single-recipe edit via its own editor checkbox, never a bulk one). **Fill algorithm:** a pure top-level `mealPlanFillWindow(rb, plan, dayStrs)` (~2489, right after `mergeMealPlanData`) — pool = `inRotation` recipes sorted by `lastCooked||0` ascending (never-cooked first, ties broken by id for determinism), fills only EMPTY days in the window, skips a recipe already assigned elsewhere in the SAME window (no repeats within one fill run), partial-fills with an honest toast when the pool is smaller than the empty-day count. Wrapped by `runMealPlanFill()`, which saves the result and toasts a summary — reachable two ways: a new "🔁 Fill empty days" button in the planner (`mpFillBtn` in `renderMealPlanner` ~3729, not in the original design doc but a natural manual entry point for the same Fill function, consistent with prompt-first — every trigger is still a tap) and the roll-over banner. **Roll-over banner** (`#mealPlanBanner`, new HTML near `#backupNudge` ~1557): "New week — plan dinners?", shown by `maybeShowMealPlanBanner()` at the same two call sites as `maybeShowBackupNudge()` (visibility-resume and `onLoginSuccess`, same gating). Once-per-week-per-device via a Monday-anchored day-string marker `fl4_mealplan_prompted` (device-local, transient, **NOT exported** — added to the export-coverage doc-comment) written by `markMealPlanPrompted()` on EITHER banner button, so a dismiss goes quiet for the rest of the week same as a tap. Tapping "Plan it" runs Fill, then — only if it actually filled something — offers Add-to-grocery via the existing `confirmDialog` helper (banner→sheet, as the design note allowed) calling the existing `addWeekToGroceries()`; nothing new invented for the grocery half. CSV-imported and pre-v362 recipes lack both new fields — treated as `inRotation:false`/`lastCooked:0` everywhere via `||` fallbacks, matching the design note. **No sync/export plumbing changes** — `inRotation`/`lastCooked` ride `fl4_recipebook`'s existing whole-record newest-wins merge (household ~10888, restore ~6463, personal ~7321) for free; `fl4_mealplan` was already exported wholesale. **Tests:** new `tests/cases/15-mealplan-fill-rotation.js` (17 assertions) — pins `mealPlanFillWindow`'s pool ordering (never-cooked-first, id tie-break), the skip-assigned-day and skip-already-assigned-recipe rules, partial-fill behaviour, `runMealPlanFill`'s real-store wiring (including the no-op case when nothing is in rotation), and `mealPlanCurrentWeekKey`'s Monday-anchoring (a Sunday belongs to the PRECEDING week, not the next one). `node tests/run.js`: 15/15 files green.
- **v359:** **Fix-queue batch E — a nameless hist entry still crashed four other readers (v351 bug review finding 4; build-ready per the 25/07/2026 triage, no design call needed).** v350's D2 decision ("a corrupt entry must never throw") was applied to `mergeHist`/`capHistToLiveLimit` only; the other five call sites that read `hist[i].name`/`h.name` unguarded still threw on a nameless entry (reachable only via a corrupted `localStorage` write — quota truncation, a manual edit, a partially-applied merge — never through the normal add/sync/import flow, since `mergeHist` already filters nameless entries out). Worst case was `showAC`, which threw on **every keystroke** once one nameless entry existed, making the add row unusable with no visible error. **Fix:** the same `if (!h || !h.name)` skip added at all five remaining readers — `deleteHistEntries` (~1936, filter guard), `showAC` (~2176, `continue` in the autocomplete-match loop), `addToHist` (~2377, `continue` in the match loop), `renderHistory` (~4854, filter guard), `addToHistFor` (~9451, `continue` in the match loop). `buildGrocerySuggestions` already guarded (~4271) and was left unchanged. **Tests:** new `tests/cases/12-nameless-hist-readers.js` (8 assertions, extends `03-merge-hist`'s nameless-entry pattern) — seeds a nameless hist entry alongside a normal one and drives all five functions directly in the real page context, asserting none throw and that named entries are still read/updated/deleted correctly while the nameless one is silently skipped/dropped. No data-model, export, or sync-protocol changes.
- **v361:** **Recurring tasks (un-parks the "recurring items" backlog item; designed + built on Fable 27/07/2026 — 3 design decisions confirmed via AskUserQuestion: schedule-anchored next-due · simple presets only · General + Personal both).** New optional `repeat` field on General/Personal task items (`daily` | `weekly` | `biweekly` | `monthly`, absent/null = one-off), set via a new "Repeat" select in the item edit sheet (`itemSheetRepeatRow`/`itemSheetRepeatSelect`, same `isTask` gating as the due-date field and Track toggle; saved value whitelisted against `REPEAT_OPTS` so a tampered select can't store junk). **Recurrence fires ONLY on the user's own done-tick — deliberately NO midnight/timer logic**, per the v256 lesson (day-rollover auto-resets = double-reset sync hazards): inside `toggleItem`'s done-tick branch (the same safe single-call-site pattern as v357 Track logging — user-tap call sites only, never merge/apply/render, so sync echoes can't re-fire it), a repeating item never rests in "done": the tick runs the normal done side-effects (★ today-star clear, v357 Track auto-log if flagged), then reschedules IN PLACE — `done` back to `false`, `dueDate` advanced, `updated` stamped, toast "Done ✓ — next due Mon 3 Aug". Date maths in new top-level pure helper `nextRepeatDue(repeat, fromDate, today)` (string-in/string-out, UTC internally, no local-midnight/DST traps): **schedule-anchored** — steps from the existing due date by the interval until strictly after BOTH today and the current due date, so an overdue weekly Monday task ticked on Wednesday is due next Monday, a weeks-ignored task needs ONE tick (missed periods skipped, no pile-up of ghost occurrences), and ticking EARLY (due date still in the future) rolls to the occurrence after it; monthly clamps to month end (31 Jan → 28 Feb) without permanently losing the anchor day (→ 31 Mar). **Two pre-merge fixes from Cathal's phone test of PR #126 (same PR/version, 27/07/2026): (a)** the early-tick case was a silent no-op — `nextRepeatDue`'s while-loop never ran when the due date was already future, returning the date unchanged; now a do-while, every tick advances at least one period ("only goes to the next date and not further" report); **(b)** repeat now REQUIRES a due date, per Cathal's suggestion — the picker is disabled (greyed, reset to "Doesn't repeat") until one is set, live-wired to the due input + Clear button via top-level `updateRepeatSelState()`, and the save handler stores `repeat` only when a due date is present (the anchors-from-today fallback stays in `nextRepeatDue` as a robustness net only). Rows show a 🔁 badge (`repChip` in `cardHTML`, General/Personal only). Same item id throughout, so delete/tombstone still kills a repeating task for good, and the new field rides the existing whole-item newest-wins list merge — **zero new sync plumbing; no export/import, dosing, or data-model changes elsewhere**. Overdue behaviour is just the existing "Overdue Nd" chip — a repeating task you ignore sits there; it never nags or duplicates. **Tests: new `tests/cases/14-recurring-tasks.js` (22 assertions)** — pins the `nextRepeatDue` maths (anchor-keeping, single-tick catch-up, strictly-future, EARLY-tick advance in weekly + monthly, biweekly step, month-end clamp + anchor recovery, year rollover, today-fallback, junk-value/malformed-input nulls), the `toggleItem` behaviour e2e (in-place reschedule + stamp + star clear, early-tick-on-future-due advance, stale-partner merge survival via `mergeListItems`, Track-log + repeat combo, non-repeating tick unaffected), and the due-date gating of the picker (`updateRepeatSelState` disable/reset/enable). `node tests/run.js`: 14/14 files green.
- **App-privacy options researched, no version bump (27/07/2026):** Cathal asked whether Hearth can be made private and whether that needs paid GitHub. **Answer: paid GitHub does not solve it.** GitHub Pro (~£4/mo) makes the *repo* private but the published Pages site stays public to anyone with the URL; genuinely private Pages needs **GitHub Enterprise Cloud** (~$21/user/mo, org-level) — rejected as overkill. Netlify Pro ($19/mo) and Vercel (pricey add-on) do the same job as the free option for real money — rejected. **Chosen direction: Cloudflare Pages + Cloudflare Access** — free to 50 users, email one-time-PIN allowlist; see the backlog entry below for the full runbook. **Not built — Cathal's call, and the setup is mostly browser clicking in the Cloudflare dashboard, not code.** **Two findings worth keeping:** **(a)** `<meta name="robots" content="noindex, nofollow">` **already exists at `index.html:5`** and has for a long time — this session offered to "add" it before grepping, exactly the mistake quirk-list rule *never claim a feature is missing without grepping* exists to prevent. It is what actually keeps Hearth out of search results, and it needs no further work. **(b)** A `robots.txt` was written, committed, and then **dropped on Cathal's instruction (27/07/2026) as useless here** — Hearth is a GitHub Pages **project site** (`coshea321.github.io/miniature-journey/`, no CNAME), so a repo-root `robots.txt` is served at `/miniature-journey/robots.txt`, and crawlers only read robots.txt at the **domain root** (`coshea321.github.io/robots.txt`, a repo we don't have). **Do not re-add it** unless Hearth moves to a custom domain. **Also established: the family *data* was never exposed by the public page** — the Firebase DB URL is not in the repo (per-device localStorage `hearth_fb`, `index.html:6724`) and the data sits behind the username/password login + Firebase rules. The public page leaks the app *code*, not the family's information.
- **v360:** **Fix-queue batch F — the polish/hardening bundle, all 8 findings (v351 bug review findings 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 14, 15, 16; build-ready per the 25/07/2026 triage, all decisions already confirmed, no fuzzy design left). This closes out the six-version bug-review fix queue opened at v351 (A→F, PR #113) — every finding in it is now shipped.** **(7)** `bottleFreshness` (~11482): the "due" lead is now `min(14 days, 25% of window)` instead of a fixed 14 days — a short real-world after-opening window (e.g. Cathal's still-outstanding ask to set real bottle windows, likely ≤28 days) no longer goes amber from the moment the bottle is opened. **(8)** `renderFamlog`'s empty state (~7670): now distinguishes `!shown.length && hasMore` ("Nothing in the last N days — Load earlier shows older events") from the true first-ever-empty case (old "try the All filter" copy, unchanged) — previously both cases showed the wrong message with an unexplained "Load earlier" button sitting underneath it. **(9)** Item-sheet save (~8437): after normalising a bare domain to `https://`, any link whose scheme isn't `http:`/`https:`/`mailto:`/`tel:` is now rejected with a toast and an early return (sheet stays open) — closes the `javascript:` self-XSS hole; links travel over household sync so it wasn't strictly self-inflicted. **(10)** `importTripFromJSON` (~2505) and `mergeBookingsIntoTrip` (~2536): booking ids now use a per-index offset (`now + i*1000 + random(0,1000)`) instead of one `now` shared across the whole loop — closes the ~0.3%-per-12-booking-import id collision (booking ids are the merge AND tombstone key). **(11)** Tombstone comparisons normalised from `>` to `>=` at booking (~2582), trip (~2607), and recipebook (~10835 — only one recipebook site found, not the two the original review counted; likely consolidated in an earlier refactor) — now matches `mergeListItems`/`mergeMedicine`/`mergeNotes`'s existing `>=`, so a same-millisecond tombstone wins consistently everywhere (trip import's tight `updated: now` loop is exactly where same-ms stamps are normal). **(14)** `applyHousehold`'s per-list `forEach` (~10751/~10776): the second shadowed `var existingIds` renamed to `existingCatIds`, matching the personal-channel twin (~7222) — removes a shadowing trap. **(15)** Note editor `doSave` (~5444): `bodyEl.innerHTML` now runs through `DOMPurify.sanitize()` (guarded, falls back untouched if DOMPurify failed to load) before the empty-check — render-side was already safe; this is defence in depth for the HTML that gets synced/exported/backed-up. **(16)** Service worker registration (~12096): dropped the stale `./sw.js?v=121` query string — `updateViaCache:"none"` already revalidates. No data-model, export, or dosing changes anywhere in this batch. **Tests:** new `tests/cases/13-trip-import-unique-ids.js` (8 assertions) — pins booking-id uniqueness within a single large (500-booking) `importTripFromJSON` call and independently within a single large `mergeBookingsIntoTrip` call; deliberately does NOT assert uniqueness across two separate near-simultaneous calls (that cross-call guarantee was never made by the fix and an earlier draft of this test flaked on exactly that comparison — see the test's own comment). `node tests/run.js`: 13/13 green, confirmed stable across repeated runs.
- **v358:** **Fix-queue batch D — growth inline date edit breaks one-entry-per-date (v351 bug review finding 3, LOCAL half only; build-ready per the 25/07/2026 triage, semantics already confirmed — refuse on collision, never overwrite or keep-both).** The inline edit path (`renderGrowth`'s `._geSave` handler, ~11083) assigned `rec.date = nd` with no check for an existing entry at the new date, so two rows could end up sharing one date; the delete button then filtered `bd.growth` BY DATE, so deleting either of the pair deleted both. **Fix:** the save handler now checks `bd2.growth.some(function(x){ return x !== rec && x.date === nd; })` before applying a date change — on a collision it toasts ("An entry for that date already exists — edit or delete it first") and leaves the edit sheet open, exactly as decided (overwrite rejected — discards a measurement feeding the centile chart and dosing weight; keep-both rejected — breaks the one-entry-per-date invariant). Delete converted from a date filter to an index-based `splice`: each rendered row now carries a `data-gidx` (the record's real index in the unsorted `bd.growth` array, resolved via `bd.growth.indexOf(e)` at render time since the render list is a sorted `.slice()` sharing the same object references), so the delete button removes exactly the one record clicked — including cleaning up a merge-created duplicate one row at a time, independent of the still-open cross-device half of this finding (gated growth-sync-hardening item below, unchanged). **Tests:** new `tests/cases/11-growth-date-edit.js` (8 assertions) — pins refuse-on-collision (record count unchanged, target record untouched, edit sheet stays open), confirms a non-colliding edit still applies, and seeds a duplicate-date pair to confirm delete removes exactly one record while the other duplicate and an unrelated record survive. No data-model, export, or sync-protocol changes.
- **v357:** **Lists → Track auto-logging, built (backlog entry from PR #118, design confirmed 25/07/2026).** Ticking a **General or Personal** item done can now also record it in the Track **Log**. Opt-in per item via a new "Log to Track when done" toggle in the item edit sheet (`itemSheetTrackLogWrap`/`itemSheetTrackLogToggle`, gated by the same `isTask` check as the due-date field) — flag once, every future tick logs; grocery/travel items don't get the toggle at all (same spam reasoning that excludes grocery from the Family Log). Stored as `item.trackLog: true|null`, saved and merged for free via the existing list-item sync/merge path — no new sync plumbing. The append itself (`"✓ " + item.name` into `fl4_action_log`) lives **only** inside `toggleItem()`'s done=true branch (~4764), which is reached only from the two user-tap call sites (checkbox tap, default-list-tap) — never from merge/apply/render, so sync echoes can't duplicate entries. Untick leaves the entry (matches how the log already reads — an accidental one is deleted by hand via the Log's existing ✕); a later re-tick adds a new entry. The ticking user's own Track gets the entry (`fl4_action_log` rides the personal channel) — if a partner ticks a shared flagged item, it logs to *their* Track, not yours; accepted for v1, no shared-routing built. **Correction to the PR #118 record:** that PR's title/body described the design as confirmed with 4 decisions, but the actual backlog text committed to this file still read "design NOT confirmed" with the old open questions — the write-up and the file had drifted apart. This entry is the first place the confirmed decisions actually landed in the file; treat PR #118's body, not the old file text, as the real design record it was building from. No data-model export/import fields touched (this is list-item-only, not a booking field). No new `tests/` case added (pure UI wiring + a single-call-site array append, no new merge/data logic to pin — consistent with how v354's similarly-scoped UI-wiring fixes were treated).
- **v356:** **Fix: homepage sometimes stuck on the boot loading screen (Cathal's report, 25/07/2026 — "I tried it for a minute and it didn't change, but closing and reopening the app loads it").** Structural root cause, not a one-off race: **every** path that hid `#bootSplash` lived at the *bottom* of the single ~12,100-line inline script — including the v312 gate's own 4s/8s deadline timer, which is only armed at line ~12,050. So anything that stopped the script reaching the bottom (an uncaught throw thousands of lines earlier, in any of the boot-time renderers/session calls) meant the deadline never existed and **nothing at all was left to hide the splash** — the dark #1C0A04 overlay at `z-index:9998` sat over the whole app indefinitely. Reopening the app worked because the transient that caused the throw didn't recur. Three changes, all defensive, none touching the existing update/version-gate logic: **(a)** a new **failsafe block inlined immediately after the splash markup** (`index.html` ~673, right in the `<body>`, so it parses and runs *before* the big script exists and nothing in that script can disable it): a 12s hard ceiling that force-hides the splash, a `pageshow`/`e.persisted` handler (bfcache restores re-run no script, so a splash left up by the previous visit had no recovery path at all), and a `window.addEventListener("error")` handler that reveals the app in 1.5s if a throw lands *before* the gate armed. The 12s ceiling deliberately sits above the gate's own 8s worst case so it can never pre-empt a genuine "Updating…" extension. **(b)** the gate and the failsafe now share state both ways — `_bootSplashGone` is seeded from `window.__hearthSplashGone` (so a gate that finally arrives after the ceiling fired doesn't act as if the splash were still up, and can't trigger `bootStaleReload()` behind an already-visible app), `hideBootSplash()` delegates to `window.__hearthSplashHide()`, and the failsafe calls back into `window.__hearthSplashGoneHook` to clear the gate's pending deadline. **(c)** the INIT dispatch (`_isTestBuild`/`isLoggedIn()`→`verifySession()`/`_isPreview`/`showLoginOverlay()`) and `applySectionVisibility()` are each wrapped in `try/catch` with a `console.error`, so the most throw-prone boot step can no longer abort the script before the gate arms. A failed boot is still a bug — it just no longer takes the entire screen down with it. **Known residual (documented, not fixed — no in-page fix exists):** if the browser suspends the document's timers while a `bootStaleReload()` navigation is stalling, *every* timer freezes including this failsafe, so that specific case still relies on the OS resuming the page. **Tests changed:** new `tests/cases/10-boot-splash-failsafe.js` (8 assertions) — pins that `__hearthSplashHide` exists and hides the splash unaided (the "gate never ran" path), that the gate reports `__hearthBootGateArmed`, that the failsafe syncs `_bootSplashGone` through the hook, and that hiding is idempotent and clears the ceiling timer. No behaviour change hidden in the test edit: it is a new file, no existing case was modified. No data-model, export, sync, or dosing changes; `sw.js` logic untouched (VERSION bump only).
- **v355:** **Fix: physio session back button never confirmed exit (Cathal's on-device catch, 25/07/2026, testing the just-merged v354/PR #117 — Nerve & Hip programme).** Physio-type Train sessions have their own in-body "Exit?" confirm link (`renderPhysioSession`'s `.ses-exit-link`, ~9953), but `sesBackBtn`'s click handler (~9726 — the button v354's finding-5 fix newly routed the phone hardware back button through) only had branches for `SS.type === "workout"` and `SS.type === "yoga"`; physio had no branch at all, a pre-existing gap that v354 exposed for the first time via hardware back (the visible top-left `‹` arrow had the same gap, just less-used/less-noticed). Result: pressing phone-back during a running physio exercise exited the session instantly with zero confirmation, unlike workout/yoga. Added the missing `SS.type === "physio" && SS.pPhase === "running"` branch, reusing the exact "Exit?" wording the in-body link already uses. **Root-caused and the fix verified with a headless-CDP probe** (same harness as `tests/`) run before and after the change — confirmed the bug reproduced (immediate close, no confirm dialog, `pPhase:"running"`) and then confirmed the fix (confirm shown, overlay stays open, a second back press cancels it same as workout/yoga, intro/done phases still exit directly unchanged). No data-model, export, or sync changes; no new `tests/` case added since this is pure UI wiring already exercised by the probe. **Process note:** this fix was first committed on top of the already-merged v354 branch by mistake; per the one-PR-per-version / no-stacking-on-a-merged-branch policy, that branch was reset to origin/main and the fix re-applied cleanly as this fresh v355 commit — no functional rework needed, the code and its verification carried over untouched.
- **v354:** **Fix-queue batch C — Train session overlay unreachable by the phone back button + native `confirm()` in its exit path (v351 bug review findings 6 + 5; build-ready per the 25/07/2026 triage, no fuzzy design). Built findings 6 then 5, per the triage note that 6 unblocks 5.** **Finding 6:** `sesBackBtn`'s exit-confirm (~line 9713, both the "Exit workout? Progress will be lost." and "Exit yoga session?" prompts) used native `confirm()`, blocked (returns `false`) in the sandboxed test-mode iframe — same class as the v346 `prompt()` finding — so Exit silently did nothing there, stranding the user in a running session. Both converted to the app's existing `confirmDialog(msg, onYes, onNo)` helper, with `closeSessionOverlay` as the `onYes` callback and an early `return` after each call (SS.type is single-valued across workout/yoga/physio sessions, so the two `if` blocks are mutually exclusive by construction — confirmed, not assumed). **Finding 5:** `#sessionOverlay` toggles `style.display` directly rather than using the `.overlay` class or `role="dialog"`, so no branch in `closeTopOverlay()` (~7759) ever caught it — a back-press fell through to `switchSection()`, changing the section underneath a still-visible, still-running overlay (timer kept ticking, chimes kept firing). Added a new branch that clicks the **existing** `#sesBackBtn` when the overlay is display-visible, so its own exit-confirm guard still applies rather than reimplementing the close (same principle as the v345 `data-bbclose` step); a confirmDialog it raises is itself dismissible by a second back-press via the existing `#_cfNo` step. No data-model, export, or sync changes; no test case was named for this batch in the fix-queue notes (unlike batches A/B/D/E) — both fixes are pure UI wiring with no new merge/data logic to pin. PR #117.
- **v353:** **Fix-queue batch B — all four unstamped mutation sites now stamp `updated`/`updatedAt` (v351 bug review finding 2; build-ready per the 25/07/2026 triage, semantics already confirmed).** The rule the whole list/notes sync model rests on: every mutation bumps `updated` (notes: `updatedAt`), and the merge takes the newer stamp — `mergeListItems` gives ties to remote, so an unstamped change isn't just "not newer", it actively loses to a stale partner push. Four sites missed it. **(2a)** "Reopen all"/"Unpack all" (`elReopenBtn` handler, ~4780): the `Object.assign` rebuilding reopened items now includes `updated: Date.now()` — previously a stale partner push at the old `done:true` stamp would silently re-close everything just reopened. **(2b)** Recipe re-tag of an already-active item, both twins (`addRecipeToGroceries` ~3338, grocery quick-import ~8316): `existing.updated = Date.now()` was only reached inside the `if (existing.done)` revive branch; moved out so a re-tag of an *active* item stamps too. **(2c)** `doClear`'s two restore branches (~4530 kept-added-item, ~4537 restored-pre-existing-item): both now stamp `it.updated`. **(2d)** `toggleNoteHighlightById` (~5129): now sets `note.updatedAt = Date.now()` — previously the star never reached the partner device at all (`mergeNotes` ties to local, so the change wasn't reverted, it just never won a push). **Decided (on record, unchanged):** starring a note counts as modifying it — the editor's "Modified" date jumps, accepted, no separate star-stamp field. Merge tie-break asymmetry (`mergeListItems` remote-wins vs `mergeNotes`/`mergeTripsData` local-wins) stays as-is — see Open decisions. **Tests:** new `tests/cases/09-mutation-stamps.js` (9 assertions) drives the real production functions/DOM controls for all four sites (`#reopenBtn`+confirm, `addRecipeToGroceries`, the real `.recipe-clear-btn`→`#_rcKeep` sheet flow, `toggleNoteHighlightById`) and then feeds the stamped result through `mergeListItems`/`mergeNotes` against a stale/absent partner copy to pin that the fix actually changes the merge outcome, not just the local write. No data-model, export, or UI changes.
- **v352:** **Fix-queue batch A — the getWD() health-store "hand-typed field list" bug, both sides (v351 bug review findings 1 + 12; semantics already confirmed by Cathal and on record above — additive union, keep-local on collision).** **Finding 1 (`importBackupData`, ~6314):** the `data.workouts` block only ever read `data.workouts.workouts`, so restoring a backup silently dropped the whole `bodyweight` and `bp` arrays with no warning, even though they were sitting in the backup file the whole time. Now merges `bodyweight` by `date` and `bp` by `ts` (union, local wins on a same-key collision), matching `applyPersonal`'s existing precedence — and since the merge mutates the local `getWD()` object in place rather than rebuilding from a hand-typed field list, any future top-level key on that store now survives import automatically. **Finding 12 (`applyPersonal`, ~7128):** the rebuilt `fl4_workouts` object was based on `remoteWD` alone (`Object.assign({}, remoteWD, {workouts, bodyweight, bp})`), so a local-only top-level key the remote copy didn't have yet (e.g. a brand-new field added before the partner device had synced) was silently wiped on every personal-sync apply. Base changed to `Object.assign({}, remoteWD, localWD, {...})` — remote first, local overwrites, same precedence already used for the three merged arrays above it — so an unknown key now carries through from whichever side actually has it. Closes the `applyPersonal` half of sync-audit item ③ (the `lastPushTs` half already closed in v351). **Tests:** `07-backup-roundtrip` extended — seeds bodyweight+bp, asserts both restore, adds a colliding local bodyweight entry pre-import to pin keep-local-on-collision, and a new step calls `applyPersonal` directly with a synthetic remote payload to pin an unknown local-only `getWD()` key surviving the sync-apply rebuild (12 assertions, was 7). No data-model, export, or UI changes — both fixes are pure merge-logic corrections in existing sync/import code paths.
- **v351:** **Dead-code cleanup — the duplicated workout-session region removed + legacy sync-state duplicates deleted (the backlog item opened by the v350 CI duplicate-function grep; Cathal's call 25/07/2026 to investigate; investigated + built on Fable same day).** Answer to the backlog's open question: **inert dead code, NOT an active-and-shadowed bug — all 24 duplicated function pairs verified byte-identical** (brace-matched extraction + byte compare of every pair, the v297/v339 method), so deleting the shadowed copies is behaviour-neutral by construction. Deleted (355 lines, pure deletions in `index.html`, all boundary-asserted before splicing): **(a)** the dead first-copy block (~8532–8919 at v350; 21 functions: doAddExercise, saveSession, saveWD, getWD, getWorkoutForDate, getOrMakeWorkout, openSessionOverlay, closeSessionOverlay, startYogaTimer, openWorkoutSession, beginWorkout, renderWorkoutSession, completeWktRound, startWorkoutRest, skipWorkoutRest, startConditioning, completeCondRound, finishWorkoutSession, t2, getPosePhoto, inputsHtml) — removed **surgically, not as a range**: 7 single-copy LIVE helpers interleaved inside the region were kept in place (capHistToLiveLimit, mergeHist, saveNotes, getNotes, latestBodyweightKg, estimateSessionKcal, openPhysioSession — the latter three are called from the surviving TRAIN module); **(b)** the stray second `btnBusy` copy (~8960; the commented original at ~6646 kept); **(c)** the early `clearST`/`playChime` copies (~7352–7366; the live TRAIN-module copies kept); **(d)** the legacy "Sync state" block (~2012–2018): dead `fbUrl`/`syncRoom` (zero references) plus shadowed duplicate declarations of `syncPaused`/`lastPushTs`/`syncPrefs` — the household-sync block (~6657–6691) owns all three, and the shadowed `syncPrefs` initializer was stale (no `health` key, `baby:false`); verified no top-level reads between the duplicate declarations, so load-order-safe. **(d) closes the `lastPushTs` half of sync-audit item ③** — the `applyPersonal` generic-key carry-through half stays open, deliberately NOT bundled (it's a sync behaviour change; own version). **CI:** `ALLOWLISTED_DUPES` in `hearth-pr-checks.yml` emptied with the comment updated — ANY future duplicate top-level function now fails the build. Verified: zero duplicate names remain (the CI grep run locally), `node --check` clean, full test suite green (boot smoke exercises Train), file 741,878 → 720,243 bytes. Zero intended behaviour change anywhere.
